# Findings

Append-only. Claims are `F-nnn`, verdicts are `V-nnn` referencing a claim. Nobody edits another entry. A claim is citable once a confirmed verdict exists. See CLAUDE.md.

Index (maintained by the main session):
- Open: F-023, F-025 (unverified literature claims)
- Confirmed: F-001 (V-001), F-002 (V-002), F-003 (V-003; unmasked knockTF only, no masked Huber comparison exists), F-004 (V-004 + erratum; what is confirmed is that the 45→57 gain is an abundance leak, never cite the gain), F-005 numbers and part 2a (V-005; 94% is the best of three weights), F-009 (V-009; closes F-005 part 2b for decoupleR's input: gene kept. "Gene kept" is not "has the same leak"; under ULM their headline AUROC can only see the TF's own mRNA through a self-edge), F-006 (V-006; stable reading is "masked real graph is inside the null band", not a 2-point margin; null does not preserve signed in-degree), F-007 (V-007; cite the skeptic's co-regulated / logFC-matched controls, the script's own any/matched arms are near no-ops), F-010 (V-010; cite propagation's loss as "mostly, 84-90% of lost hits" via other regulons, and "reproduces masking" as a top-10% statement. Net 30 of ULM's 126 hits (24%) need the self-edge, so do NOT write "ULM's knockTF performance is largely autoregulation"), F-011 (V-011; cite as "in knockTF scored with CollecTRI"; direction high (per TF 81/84 worse for +1, 11/12 better for -1, p = 0.001), top-10% magnitude for the -1 group low (3 hits, HIF1A is 7 of 21). Write "+1 self-edge", not "positive autoregulator": CollecTRI assigns +1 to edges with no sign information), F-008 (V-008; Huber gives the TF's own gene a median weight of 0.09, so it never collected the self-edge leak: masking costs ULM 33 hits and Huber 5. Cite F-003 as "worse on unmasked knockTF, about half to two thirds of that being own-gene clipping; on masked knockTF worse on paired rank (p = 0.002) and top-5%, not on top-10% (p = 0.10)". Huber's deficit is confined to the 197 self-edge experiments)

V-016 also corrects V-011: the column was in the parquet all along, and ZHX2's -1 self-edge is a regulon inference, not curated)

- Split: F-014 (V-014 + erratum). Citable: TF-level drop 56 -> 41 of 155 (0.36 -> 0.27; 0.45 -> 0.30 if split two-experiment TFs count as hits), masking = self-edge removal under every collapse rule; F-002 survives the TF collapse unchanged and V-002's flip condition is not met; -1 self-edge TFs cannot be size-matched in knockTF. NOT ESTABLISHED: "matching on size removes the self-edge gap"; matched difference is about 0 (-0.02 to +0.05 over 13 estimators) but the proper CI is about [-0.17, +0.23] and contains the unmatched 0.126. This also weakens V-010's "residual gap is all size" (its +/-0.07 was about 3x too narrow)
- Split: F-017 (V-017). Citable: the crosstabs and group tables; "22 of 33 lost hits sit on a default-filled +1 self-edge (20 TFs), 7 on PMID, 4 on regulon; not distinguishable from a label-blind split at TF level (p = 0.05 one-sided, 0.33 within size bins)"; regulon size still limits ULM (Spearman masked rank vs size -0.27). NOT established: that 22/7/4 is a skew (TF-proportional expectation is 19.6, not 17); "the 61-150 bin tracks starting rank" (refuted at hit level); "sign quality is not what limits ULM" must read "cannot tell": +0.014, TF-cluster 95% CI [-0.14, +0.17], which contains the predicted 0.12. Skeptic's scratch leads, not findings: erasing all signs costs masked ULM 96 -> 81 hits; TFs whose PMID-signed edges are majority repressing score 5/43 masked vs 66/154 (T-033)
- Split: F-018 (V-018). Citable: all numbers (within-TF slope CI as [-0.25, +0.42], the printed +0.38 is a seed artefact); "F-015's EB-slope as designed (through origin, knockTF-calibrated, mu about 1) is rejected: the design had 73% power for it (60% with logFC noise) and the data sit in that model's 2-5% tail"; "knockTF cannot calibrate the abundance term in either form". decoupler's knockTF keeps only experiments with own logFC < -1 (hard edge at -1.0016, all 388), so there is no data between 0 and -1 and almost no within-TF dose range by construction. NOT citable: "the abundance component is a constant offset" (an offset predicts a nonzero abundance effect at m = 0), "three independent readings agree" (the placebo does not discriminate; the LOO overturning condition had 7% power), "no dose-response" (a within-TF slope of 0.5 had 35-45% power, likelihood ratio about 2 vs offset). The decoupling direction is untested, not refuted
- Split: F-021 (V-021). Citable: the Part A table; re-signing or deleting the default fills of the 197 repressor-majority TFs moves masked ULM by 0 to 2 hits; default edges are thin evidence (83% single-reference, 70% ExTRI-only text-mined); Part B as "pre-stated primary test failed (p = 0.158); secondary is an exploratory lead, 5/43 vs 34/98 at comparable sizes, p = 0.006 by experiment, 0.057 with one vote per TF". NOT citable: "default fills carry no information" (skeptic's scratch: flipping ALL defaults to -1 takes masked ULM 96 -> 62, so default-activating is the most informative sign decision in the network), "sign erasure is below every seed" (8 of 50 random-flip seeds are at or below 81), "default fills are below-average edges" as a statement about sign provenance (50 seeds: p about 0.10, and removing the least-supported edges without reading sign_decision reproduces it). Skeptic's scratch, to be committed (T-036): real signs beat within-TF-permuted signs by about 13 hits in 50 of 50 draws; default-activating beats default-repressing by 65 hits unmasked and 34 masked, so about half of that win is the own-gene leak
- CORRECTION to V-022 from F-029, verified independently by V-029: the per-TF base rate is 0.122 on the FILTERED 279 under the asymmetric mask (exact rational 0.1224991943, so 0.122 under any rounding) and 0.123 on all 643 scored. V-022 printed one three-decimal figure against both denominators, so one of its two rows is wrong by 0.001. WHICH one is not established and must not be asserted: masked ranks fit its 643 row and miss its 279; unmasked ranks fit its 279 and miss its 643. V-022's scratch is deleted and reproduces neither exactly. Publish the two numbers with their denominators and no provenance claim. Masked ULM is therefore +0.222 over base and 2.81x on the 279, not +0.221 / 2.80x. "Chance is about 12%, not 10%" is unaffected. studymate.py had been printing the correction in stdout all along and nobody reconciled it — which is the case for results/ existing at all.
- Confirmed: F-029 (V-029; the machine-readable record). results/master.csv, 643 rows x 39 columns, reproduces 30 of 31 published numbers with an assert that demands exactly that one mismatch and no other (sensitivity-tested: it fires on a silent fix, on any other row drifting, and on the failing row being swapped). Spot-checked exactly against the owning scripts. CORRECTED BY V-029: master.py's docstring claims the two files share a 21985-gene var axis and therefore reproduce EXACTLY; dc.pp.extract drops 50 all-zero genes from the filtered file, so leak.py scores on 21935 features and master.py on 21985. 19 of 279 masked rank fractions differ by 1-3 of 766 positions — NO hit flag moves at either threshold, so all 30 asserted counts stand, but the reproduction is empirical at the hit level, not exact by construction, and a rank-level statistic recomputed from master.csv is not byte-identical to its owning script's. COVERAGE is worse than F-029's own MEDIUM: master.csv carries no raw ULM score column, so F-022's within-TF slope and bin means, V-025's +0.360 and V-027's size law are not computable from it even as point estimates
- Split: F-022 (V-022). Citable: in unfiltered knockTF the perturbed TF's self-edge-dropped ULM score falls with its own logFC within TF: pooled slope 0.46-0.48 (95% TF-cluster CI about [0.15, 0.75]), within-TF Spearman +0.17 [+0.05, +0.28]; absent under permuted-logFC placebo and shuffled network; Knock.Method and Platform fixed effects raise it to 0.6-0.7. Size is 0.2 to 0.7, SS-weighted and carried by about ten TFs; median per-TF slope 0.27 with a CI including 0. Calibrating it gains nothing out of sample (LOO gain 0.035, CI includes 0). Masked ULM beats a per-TF base rate: 0.344 vs 0.123 on the filtered 279 (three nulls agree at 0.09-0.13), so the 34% baseline is not hub-TF identity bias; chance for perturbed TFs is about 12%, not 10%. Masked ULM is 13/38 on experiments whose own gene was never measured. REFUTED: "masked ULM is 3x chance where the TF mRNA did not fall, so recovery is not only abundance" (the m >= 0 bin is 38 unmeasured + 21 inside noise + 18 measurably raised; on those 18 masked ULM is 2/18, chance, and the score is positive), and "the curve flattens at -0.54 / a dose-independent offset of about half". NOT established: "not a dose-response through the origin" (own logFC is a noisy dose; reliability about 0.55 reproduces the intercept). An exact 0.0 in the knockTF matrix is a fill value for "not measured". Lead: inside multi-TF studies the perturbed TF barely beats its study-mates (0.277 vs 0.225, CI includes 0; T-037)
- Split: F-027 (V-027; the masked rerun of the 2026 benchmark). CONFIRMED: the anchor (an independent decoupler call reproduces their shipped numbers to six decimals and the act matrix to 3.55e-15; no read-back path exists; the clone is unmodified), the transfer test (masking moves their headline AUROC by -0.0001 against a pre-stated 0.05 threshold), and the by-group internal null. UPGRADED — the mechanism is now a measured LAW, not a story: masking each of the 613 scored TFs in turn gives log10|dscore| = -0.53 - 0.46*log10(regulon size), R2 = 0.80, Spearman -0.868; the -0.46 exponent is the 1/sqrt(n) one gene contributes to a t-statistic. Terciles: 7-target regulons |dAUROC| 1.1e-2, 28-target 5.5e-3, 92-target 2.5e-3, >=300-target 1.3e-3. TP53 (772 targets) sits where the law puts it and is smaller than 99.6% of self-edge TFs. Detection has NO marginal effect on channel width (Spearman +0.026); it acts through the second factor, the own gene's discriminative signal, which for TP53 is at chance (AUROC 0.4933). Cite the leak as a PRODUCT: channel width (1/sqrt(regulon size)) x own-gene signal. REFUTED: the shuffled band. "[0.433, 0.701] over 10 seeds" is a 10-seed artefact and a min-max, not an interval; 100 seeds give 0.47 +/- 0.14, central 95% [0.225, 0.762], and 42 of 100 fall below the quoted minimum. The corrected band is WORSE for the paper: 34 of their 60 published TP53__Dox columns fall inside it, including their own `random` column, and metaTF_PUIC 0.734 is INSIDE, not "just outside". CORRECTED: "it does not even go DOWN in counts" must not be quoted as scope — on the normalised layer their own filter.py uses, TP53 IS lower in perturbed cells (0.0950 vs 0.1144), and filter.py keeps a TF only if pert mean < ctrl mean, so their benchmark selects its positives on the own-abundance drop exactly as decoupler's knockTF thr_fc=-1 does. ORA: the conclusion holds on a stronger control (rescoring the UNCHANGED matrix under different feature orderings moves ORA by 0.169-0.189, the size of the claimed masking effect), but F-027's stated mechanism is wrong twice — decoupler permutes ALL features with a seed that depends on n, and masking drops the now-all-zero gene, changing n; and _ora.py selects 95% of features, not the top 775. ROUTE: F-027 is TF-perturbing, the third such benchmark here, not a second route, and it does LESS to F-023 than first filed: it closes the DIRECT own-mRNA channel only, while F-023's mechanism is indirect (weights fitted to TF-transcript covariation), so F-023 is neither weakened nor confirmed
- Confirmed: F-028 (V-028; the study-context lift). Cite as: inside a GEO series a TF is top-10% in 22% of its series-mates' experiments against 14% in same-cell-line experiments from other series (+0.073 [+0.019, +0.178], symmetric mask, 106 of 643 scored). MECHANISM, now tested: specific to that TF's real regulon (degree-preserving shuffle leaves 6%; a random-judged-TF placebo gives p = 0.003) and NOT regulon overlap with the series' other perturbed TF (the lift is +0.113 among pairs with Jaccard exactly 0, and +0.083 on non-shared targets against +0.019 on shared ones). NOT ESTABLISHED: "inside a series a TF's targets move together" as coherence — direction-free the lift is +0.032, placebo p = 0.066; what is elevated is the SIGNED knockdown-direction score. NOT LICENSED, and the main session relayed this error to Brandon: "regulon-specific therefore not batch" is a non-sequitur. Series-mean centering takes the lift to +0.020 [-0.034, +0.088], so it IS removable, at a cost of 0.036 on the own-arm baseline. SCOPE, the sharpest limit: pair-weighted the lift is -0.001, so it exists only under per-row weighting; GSE31912, the one systematic 78-TF screen, holds 38 of the 106 rows and 87% of the within-pairs and shows -0.004, while the other 23 small deliberate studies give +0.117 — which favours "series choose functionally related TFs" over "series share a reference sample", the two explanations the data cannot separate. SECOND KNIFE-EDGE: the symmetric core d "+0.066 [+0.003, +0.134]" is positive in only 2 of 20 bootstrap seeds and must NOT be cited as excluding 0 either
- Split: F-026 (V-026; the study-mate test). The 0.344 baseline STANDS: it is not mostly study context, the own-vs-mate gap dies under a degree-preserving shuffle (+0.056 -> +0.003), and study-controlling costs 0.09-0.17, leaving 1.3-1.9x the per-TF base rate. REFUTED: F-026's "no enrichment overall" in study-mate regulon overlap — that compares an observed mean whose pairs are 48% forced zeros (mates with no CollecTRI regulon) against a null drawn only from TFs that have one. Against a study-label permutation null the enrichment is real, 1.6-2.2x, p < 0.005, and V-022's 0.066 reproduces EXACTLY as the per-experiment mean over scored mates. V-022's number stands; only its READING needed correcting, and the overlap explains +0.017 of the +0.056. NOT ESTABLISHED, do not quote: "the CI includes 0" as evidence the within-study margin is small. Power at +0.05 is 0.28, MDE is +0.110, and the same CI reaches +0.138. CORRECTED: GSE31912 supplies 38 of the 155 rows, not 78; leaving it out changes d by 0.000. NEW and unclaimed by F-026: the masking asymmetry biases d DOWNWARD, and the symmetric fix gives +0.066 [+0.003, +0.134]. NEW and the striking one, to be committed as F-028 before citing: on the 106 rows that have both mate kinds, the study-context lift is +0.073, and it is quotable ONLY from the symmetric row, [+0.019, +0.178] (V-028: the asymmetric [+0.001, +0.192] is retired, its lower bound positive in 10 of 20 bootstrap seeds); "about as much as being the perturbed TF" is a point estimate, the ratio is 0.68, 95% [0.21, 1.60] — being in the same GEO series raises a TF's score by about as much as being the perturbed TF does
- CORRECTION to the F-024 line below, from V-025: V-024's filing of the +0.360 under the abundance-leak family is REFUTED. The +0.360 was already computed on MASKED scores, so the own-transcript channel was shut when it was filed; every condition that closes that channel gives the same number (self-edge deleted +0.359, own gene off every regulon +0.359) and the channel-OPEN condition is the lowest of six (+0.342). Do not cite it as leak evidence, and do not write "the only thing ULM tracks is abundance" (the occupancy CI is +/-0.33 wide, so "nothing else" is unmeasured). The positive half survives both nulls V-024 left unrun (derangement p = 0.032; per-timepoint centring raises it to +0.388; 0 of 50 shuffled networks reach it) but carries two limits: it is ENTIRELY between-TF (within a TF across its timepoints the same correlation averages -0.118, positive in 7 of 17), and the SIGNOR sign flip is load-bearing (unsigned protein gives +0.204, and the 7 negative-sign TFs give +0.028, nothing). "ULM is essentially tracking abundance" is NOT supportable and is withdrawn; the supportable wording is that knockTF-style benchmarks reward abundance detection and about a quarter of ULM's hits there come through the self-edge, while the other three quarters still beat the per-TF base rate about threefold. "The PTM channel is what ULM misses" is equally unlicensed: F-024's INCONCLUSIVE stands for that half. The experiment that would separate the two readings is WITHIN-TF — protein and regulon response across several levels of the same TF on a TF-sparing route; more TFs do not help
- Confirmed (cont.): F-024 (V-024; the project's first TF-sparing measurement). Citable: the inconclusiveness is real and survives dropping small regulons and a jackknife (masked ULM vs occupancy Spearman -0.018, CI [-0.33, +0.33], n=51 over 17 TFs). The second result is UNDERSTATED by F-024 and is the thing to cite: paired on the same 51 observations, uncorrected +0.227 vs corrected -0.018, d = -0.245, one-sided p = 0.034, and the mechanism is direct — masked ULM correlates +0.360 [-0.03, +0.65] with the PROTEIN-abundance component of the phosphosite change and -0.018 with the occupancy component. CORRECTIONS to F-024's own wording: the shuffled null IS centred near 0 (50 seeds: +0.036, sd 0.159), the +0.097 was seed-count noise, and "6 of 10 seeds beat the real network" is the wrong statistic (9 of 10 at 10 seeds, 33 of 50 at 50); the corrected panel is 17 TFs, not 19. REFUTED: the size projection. 206 TFs exceeds the 174 CollecTRI sources that have a directional SIGNOR activity phosphosite AT ALL, so +/-0.10 is unreachable with CollecTRI x SIGNOR at any number of datasets; ten pooled runs buy about +/-0.18, the whole resource about +/-0.11. CROSS-ROUTE: no evidence yet about any method's accuracy, but one weak cross-route corroboration of the abundance-leak family (F-005/F-009/F-023) — on a route where no TF is perturbed, the only thing masked ULM tracks is protein abundance
- Refuted: V-023 checked three main-session readings of Sugimoto 2026. (1) REFUTED: the paper says TF mRNA and TF phosphorylation have "no significant association ... in this setting", which is an underpowered null (back-solved n about 42, CI compatible with r = +/-0.3), NOT "abundance carries no information"; an equivalence claim would need about 400 TF-timepoint observations. (2) UNVERIFIABLE: "CollecTRI+ULM tracks phospho-activity significantly worse" has no named test, no n, no effect size, and each per-TF correlation is over 4 timepoints; the TFs with directional SIGNOR sites have median CollecTRI regulon 63 targets (six under 15) against TFActProfiler's ~1292, so it is partly a regulon-size comparison. Cite only as "the authors report". (3) CONFIRMED: the phospho design is reusable. 150 CollecTRI TFs (tmin=5) have a directional SIGNOR regulatory phosphosite (460 TF-site pairs); in the one MPXV dataset, 23 TFs and 38 sites are quantified, so a single run is anecdote-sized and only a pooled analysis is honest. Their own showcase site YBX1 S174 is annotated in SIGNOR as regulating protein QUANTITY, not activity, so their ground truth may itself be partly an abundance readout
- Unverifiable: F-015 EB-slope proposal (V-015). Machinery sound (all reductions hold), but missing the control that tests its premise: an intercept-only comparator / permuted-own-logFC placebo. Every knockTF own logFC is negative, so the through-origin slope is a rescaled mean. Skeptic's PREVIEW (scratch, not the experiment): corr(score, own logFC) = 0.095, within-TF slope 0.08 +/- 0.34, permuted-logFC placebo slope 0.75 vs real 0.82, and a no-logFC per-TF mean predicts held-out knockdowns better than the slope (LOO RMSE 2.74 vs 3.19). Kill condition K4 already met as written (32% of slopes negative) but is miscalibrated. Do not implement T-027 as designed.
- Refuted / confounded: none

## F-001 · 2026-09-17 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: ULM+CollecTRI ranks the true TF in the top 10% in 45% of 279 knockTF knockdown experiments (155 TFs, tmin=5).
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python gapcheck.py` prints "top-10% hit rate 0.45".
Source: analysis/gapcheck.py, analysis/gapcheck.csv
Confidence: high, deterministic script on cached data
Depends on: none

## F-002 · 2026-09-17 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: Regulon overlap does not explain ULM misses; the high-overlap tercile misses less (46%) than the low-overlap tercile (57%). Small regulons miss more (68% bottom tercile vs 47% top).
Evidence: stratified table printed by gapcheck.py.
Source: analysis/gapcheck.py
Confidence: high for direction, medium for magnitude (terciles of ~90 experiments)
Depends on: F-001

## F-003 · 2026-09-17 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: Huber-IRLS ULM is worse than ULM: top-10% 32% vs 45%, worse on 66% of experiments paired.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python robust_ulm.py` (about 25 min).
Source: analysis/robust_ulm.py
Confidence: high
Depends on: F-001

## F-004 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: One-step propagation of ULM scores from upstream TF regulators (summed, alpha 0.5) raises top-10% from 45% to 57%, and a degree-preserving shuffled network drops it to 32%. But masking the perturbed TF's own gene before ULM removes the gain entirely (28% vs 27% shuffled). The gain is the knockdown's own mRNA drop leaking through upstream regulators' regulons.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python propagate.py`, `controls.py`, `leak.py`.
Source: analysis/propagate.py, analysis/controls.py, analysis/leak.py
Confidence: high
Depends on: F-001

## F-005 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: knockTF-style benchmarks reward abundance detection. Adding the TF's own log fold change to ULM reaches 94% top-10%. ULM itself falls from 45% to 34% when that gene is masked. The 2026 Perturb-seq TF-activity benchmark (refs/tf-activity-benchmark-perturbation-2026.md) selects perturbations by the TF's own expression change and does not mask that gene.
Evidence: controls.py and leak.py output; refs/tf-activity-benchmark-perturbation-2026.md#Perturb-seq datasets.
Source: analysis/controls.py, analysis/leak.py, refs/tf-activity-benchmark-perturbation-2026.md
Confidence: high for the numbers, medium for the claim about the 2026 paper (based on reading its methods, not rerunning it)
Depends on: F-004

## V-001 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed
Claim checked: F-001 — plain ULM on CollecTRI (tmin=5) puts the knocked-down TF in the top 10% of signed scores in 45% of the 279 scoreable knockTF experiments, which cover 155 TFs.
How verified: reran `cd analysis && uv run python gapcheck.py` (9 s). Printed "279 experiments, 155 TFs, top-10% hit rate 0.45, beaten by a neighbour 0.56". Exact value 126/279 = 0.4516. Regenerated gapcheck.csv is byte-identical to the committed one. propagate.py independently prints "ulm top10%=0.45 top5%=0.35". Checked "knockdown": all 388 experiments in knocktf.h5ad have type_p = -1; 279 is the subset whose TF gets a ULM score, so 109 experiments are silently dropped and the 45% is conditional on the TF being in CollecTRI with >=5 targets.
Would flip if: a rerun on freshly fetched data (`dc.ds.knocktf()`, `dc.op.collectri()`) gave a hit count other than 126/279, or the rank were shown to be computed on the wrong sign (it is not: type_p * score, ranked descending).
Severity: high — every other claim in this log is a delta against this 45%.

## V-002 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed
Claim checked: F-002 — experiments whose TF regulon overlaps most with another regulon are missed less often (46%) than those overlapping least (57%), so overlap does not explain misses; small regulons are missed more (68% bottom size tercile vs 47% top).
How verified: reran gapcheck.py. "by max_jac" miss: low 0.57 (n=96), mid 0.60 (n=100), high 0.46 (n=83). "by size" miss: low 0.68 (n=93), mid 0.49 (n=95), high 0.47 (n=91). All four claimed numbers match and the direction is stated the right way round (miss = frac > 0.10; "high" = upper qcut bin). Adversarial check for a size confound (Spearman size~max_jac = 0.42): within each size tercile the high-overlap cell never misses materially more than the low-overlap cell (low size 0.64 vs 0.62 with n=11; mid 0.47 vs 0.50; high 0.40 vs 0.57), so the conclusion survives stratification. Caveats the claim omits: the mid overlap tercile is the worst (0.60), so the relation is non-monotonic; 57% vs 46% is about 1.5 SE on non-independent experiments (155 TFs, 279 rows), so "overlap does not hurt" is supported but "high overlap helps" is not; Spearman rank-fraction~max_jac = -0.03. The size effect is about 2.9 SE (Spearman frac~size = -0.20).
Would flip if: a TF-level (one row per TF) or size-matched analysis showed miss rate rising with max Jaccard, or an overlap measure other than max pairwise Jaccard (e.g. weighted shared-target mass) showed the opposite direction.
Severity: medium — it is the reason penalised joint fitting (MLM-style) was dropped as a direction.

## V-004 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed
Claim checked: F-004 — adding upstream regulators' ULM scores (up, sum, alpha 0.5) lifts top-10% from 45% to 57% and a shuffled graph gives 32%, but with the perturbed TF's own gene zeroed before ULM the propagated score is 28% against 27% shuffled, so the gain is own-mRNA leak. Note for the index: what is confirmed is the claim that the gain is confounded (reason: abundance leak). The 45→57 gain itself must never be cited as an improvement.
How verified: one run of `cd analysis && uv run python leak.py` (7 s) imports and reruns propagate.py and controls.py and prints all three tables. Unmasked: ulm 0.45/0.35; up sum a=0.5 0.57/0.47 (better 0.59, worse 0.37); SHUFFLED 0.32/0.22. Masked: ulm 0.34/0.28; up sum a=0.5 0.28/0.16 (better 0.42, worse 0.57); SHUFFLED 0.27/0.18. All claimed numbers match. Both controls exist and were reported. Mask audit: leak.py line 9 sets X.loc[exp, tf] = 0 on an experiments x genes frame; I confirmed exactly 388 cells change, one per row, always the perturbed TF's own column, and ULM is recomputed from the masked matrix (so upstream regulators' scores are also recomputed). Right axis. It zeroes the gene rather than dropping it from the regression; harmless. Shuffle audit: FAILS its own docstring. `shuffle_preserving_degree` checks for duplicate edges against the original A, not the current edge list, so swaps create duplicates that collapse when B is filled: 7296 edges become 6825 (-6.5%), max out-degree change 39, max in-degree change 29. It is not degree-preserving, and it is a single seed-0 draw. I reran with a corrected shuffle (duplicate check against the live edge set; asserted edges, in-degree and out-degree all identical) over 10 seeds: unmasked 0.306 mean (range 0.280-0.326) vs real 0.566; masked 0.259 mean (range 0.237-0.272) vs real 0.276. The buggy shuffle over 10 seeds gives 0.295 (0.254-0.315) unmasked and 0.258 (0.215-0.287) masked. So the conclusion is robust to the bug and to the seed. Under masking the real graph is 5 points above the shuffle mean at most one experiment above the best shuffle draw, and 7 points BELOW masked plain ULM (0.276 vs 0.344), so "removes the gain entirely" is if anything an understatement: propagation hurts once the leak is closed. The mechanism sentence (leak via upstream regulators' regulons) is consistent with the code (direction "up" adds scores of TFs whose regulon contains the perturbed gene) and with masking one gene per experiment costing 29 points, but no script isolates it from the autoregulatory edge path (71% of perturbed TFs have a self-edge in CollecTRI, which acts on plain ULM too).
Would flip if: a correct degree-preserving null with many seeds put the masked real-graph score clearly above the null band AND above masked ULM 0.34 (neither holds: 0.276 vs null max 0.272, vs ULM 0.344); or if zeroing a random non-target gene per experiment produced the same 57→28 collapse, which would mean the mask is breaking something other than the leak (not run; a cheap extra control).
Severity: high — this is the result that reframed the project from "beat ULM" to "the benchmark rewards abundance". Follow-ups owed by the experimentalist, not blocking: fix the duplicate check in controls.py and report a multi-seed range; README.md line "survives a degree-preserving shuffle" describes a shuffle that was not degree-preserving; controls.py and leak.py do not end with the assert CLAUDE.md requires (an assert on preserved degrees would have caught this).

## V-005 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: split — numbers confirmed; literature (a) selection by own expression change confirmed; literature (b) "does not mask that gene" unverifiable
Claim checked: F-005 — (1) ULM plus the TF's own logFC reaches 94% top-10% and plain ULM drops 45%→34% when that gene is masked, so knockTF rewards abundance detection; (2a) the 2026 Perturb-seq benchmark picks its perturbations using the target TF's own expression change; (2b) it does not mask that gene when scoring.
How verified: (1) leak.py run (which reruns controls.py) prints "ulm + 0.5*own mRNA 0.66 0.56", "ulm + 1*own mRNA 0.77 0.67", "ulm + 2*own mRNA 0.94 0.86 better 0.89 worse 0.03"; masked "ulm 0.34 0.28" vs unmasked "ulm 0.45 0.35". Numbers match, but 94% is the best of three weights (2x own logFC scaled by the global std of the TF-gene logFC matrix); at weight 1 it is 77%. The claim should say "up to 94%". Supporting fact found while auditing: every one of the 279 scored experiments has own logFC < 0 (median -1.74), i.e. the knockTF set as fetched is itself sign-selected on the TF's own mRNA. (2a) refs/tf-activity-benchmark-perturbation-2026.md, "### Perturb-seq datasets" (line 35): "for CRISPR interference (CRISPRi) perturbations, we selected those with decreased target TF expression relative to the control group (log2-fold change (FC) < 0); for CRISPR activation (CRISPRa) or open reading frame (ORF) overexpression perturbations, we selected those with increased target TF expression relative to the control group (log2-FC > 0)." This is a sign filter, not a magnitude threshold; the claim's wording covers it. The Discussion (line 244) concedes the underlying point: "the Perturb-seq datasets employed herein primarily rely on genetic-level perturbations, whereby TF perturbation effects are manifested in the altered expression of the TF's own mRNA." (2b) No sentence in the file says the target TF's gene is kept or removed. A search for mask/exclud/remov/own/itself finds only cell- and perturbation-level QC (mixscape escape cells, E-test weak perturbations). The nearest text is line 45: "all methods were supplied with identical initial input matrices and unified preprocessing workflows at the outset", with details deferred to Supplementary Table S1, which is not in refs/. So (2b) is an inference from silence, not something the paper states. Separately, refs/README.md and README.md go further than F-005 and say the paper "has the same abundance leak"; that is not demonstrated by anything here, since the paper's headline metrics (per-TF AUROC/AUPRC of perturbed vs control cells) differ from rank-among-TFs and nobody reran them masked.
Would flip if: (2b) becomes confirmed if Supplementary Table S1 or the paper's released code shows the expression matrix passed to decoupleR still contains the perturbed TF's gene, and becomes refuted if either shows it removed; "same leak" would need a masked rerun of at least one of their 17 datasets showing decoupleR's NRank/AUROC degrade. (1) flips only if a rerun prints different numbers.
Severity: medium — (1) and the 34% masked baseline underpin CLAUDE.md's benchmark policy and are solid. (2b) only matters if the write-up criticises the 2026 paper by name; until the supplement or code is checked, cite it as "selects on the TF's own mRNA change and reports no masking step", not as "does not mask" or "has the same leak".

## V-004 erratum · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict unchanged: confirmed
Claim checked: F-004 — correction to one sentence of my own V-004 above, appended rather than edited because this log is append-only.
How verified: V-004 says "Under masking the real graph is 5 points above the shuffle mean at most one experiment above the best shuffle draw". The arithmetic is wrong and the sentence is garbled. Correct reading from the same run: masked real graph 0.276 vs corrected-shuffle mean 0.259 is 1.7 points (about 5 experiments of 279), and 0.276 vs the best of 10 corrected shuffle draws 0.272 is about one experiment. All other numbers in V-004 stand.
Would flip if: n/a (erratum).
Severity: low — does not change the verdict or any quoted table value.

## V-003 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed
Claim checked: F-003 — Replacing ULM's least-squares fit with a Huber-IRLS fit (k=1.345, MAD scale, 10 iterations, slope t-stat ranked the same way as ULM) lowers the top-10% hit rate of the perturbed TF on knockTF from 45% to 32% and ranks the true TF worse than ULM in 66% of the 279 experiments.
How verified: Reran `cd analysis && uv run python robust_ulm.py` (exit 0, assert passed). Printed: `ulm n=279 top10%=0.45 top5%=0.35 median_frac=0.138 wrong_sign=0.23`; `huber_ulm n=279 top10%=0.32 top5%=0.23 median_frac=0.243 wrong_sign=0.29`; `paired: huber better in 0.33 worse in 0.66`. All three claimed numbers match exactly. Implementation audit on 30 randomly drawn experiments (seed 0, scratch script, not committed): (a) reduces to ULM: with k=1e9 the t-stats match decoupler's `score_ulm` to max |diff| 0.012 mean, 0.041 worst, so the ranking statistic is the same t-stat as ULM, not a bare coefficient; (b) convergence: 10 iterations is not fully converged for some experiments (t-stat max |diff| vs 50 iterations up to 39, mean 2.9), but true-TF rank fraction barely moves (mean 0.351 vs 0.357, top-10% 0.37 both); (c) one real defect: W and y are centered with unweighted means and the intercept is never re-estimated under the weights, and the MAD is not median-centered; a corrected version with weighted intercept and centered MAD gives top-10% 0.33 vs 0.37 for the script's version and 0.53 for ULM on the same 30, so the defect does not produce the loss; (d) ranking by robust coefficient instead of t-stat is far worse (0.10), so the t-stat choice is not what hurts Huber; (e) mechanism check: mean final Huber weight on the true TF's targets is 0.69 vs 0.82 on non-targets, with 33% of genes downweighted, i.e. the loss discards the strongly responding targets that carry the signal. The "worse" result is a property of robust loss in this setup, not an artifact. Controls: the script has neither the masked-own-gene nor the shuffled-network control. CLAUDE.md requires them for a claimed gain; this is a claimed loss, so their absence does not block the verdict on the numbers. It does limit interpretation: both arms are unmasked, so the 45% baseline includes the own-mRNA abundance leak (masked ULM is 34% per F-005), and part of Huber's 13-point deficit could be Huber clipping that leak rather than losing activity signal. F-003 should be cited as "worse on unmasked knockTF", not as "worse at activity inference", until a masked Huber-vs-ULM comparison exists.
Would flip if: a rerun prints top-10% or paired-worse numbers differing beyond rounding; or k large fails to reproduce ULM scores; or a correctly converged, weighted-intercept Huber fit on all 279 experiments matches or beats ULM (the 30-experiment subset says it does not). The interpretation caveat would be resolved either way by masked-gene Huber vs masked-gene ULM (34%): if Huber is at or above 34% masked, the deficit was abundance-leak clipping and the README sentence about signal living in strongly responding targets needs revising.
Severity: low — nothing downstream depends on F-003; it closes off a direction (README line 16). The untested masked comparison is medium only if someone wants to reopen robust losses.

## F-006 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: With `shuffle_preserving_degree` corrected (duplicate check against the live edge set, not the original matrix; edge count and in/out degrees now provably identical to CollecTRI's TF-TF graph), the degree-preserving null over 10 seeds is 0.31 top-10% (range 0.28-0.33) unmasked against the real graph's 0.57, and 0.26 (range 0.24-0.27) masked against the real graph's 0.28. The propagation conclusion in F-004 is unchanged: the unmasked 45->57 gain is far above the null, the masked 0.28 is 2 points above the null mean, inside a point of the best of 10 null draws, and 6 points BELOW masked plain ULM (0.34). The old buggy shuffle lost 6.5% of edges (7296 -> 6825, degrees off by up to 39) and was a single seed-0 draw; it happened to give the same verdict.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python leak.py` (12 s) prints, unmasked, "up sum a=0.5 0.57 / SHUF x10 mean 0.31 [0.28-0.33]" and, masked, "ulm 0.34 / up sum a=0.5 0.28 / SHUF x10 mean 0.26 [0.24-0.27]". controls.py now asserts every one of the 10 shuffled graphs has the same edge count and the same in- and out-degree vectors as the real graph; leak.py asserts the perturbed TF's own gene is zero in every row of the scored matrix. Numbers reproduce the skeptic's independent corrected shuffle in V-004 to two decimals.
Source: analysis/controls.py, analysis/leak.py
Confidence: high — deterministic given the seeds, degree preservation is asserted rather than claimed, and the 10-seed range never overlaps the real unmasked score.
Depends on: F-004

## F-007 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: The 57->28 collapse is specific to masking the perturbed TF. Zeroing one random measured gene per experiment instead leaves top-10% at 0.45 for ULM and 0.57 for up/sum/alpha=0.5 (5 seeds; identical to the unmasked run), and so does zeroing a random gene matched on regulon membership to the perturbed TF's gene (0.45 ULM, range 0.44-0.46; 0.57 propagated). The shuffled-graph null under random masking stays at 0.31. So masking is not breaking the pipeline in general; the 29-point loss in F-004 is the perturbed TF's own mRNA and nothing else.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python randmask.py` (35 s; imports propagate.py, controls.py and leak.py and reprints their tables). Random-mask table: any ulm 0.452/0.355, any up sum a=0.5 0.566/0.473 (better 0.59, worse 0.37), any SHUF 0.307; matched ulm 0.452/0.356, matched up sum a=0.5 0.566/0.472, matched SHUF 0.307. References in the same output: unmasked ulm 0.45, propagated 0.57; perturbed-TF-masked ulm 0.34, propagated 0.28. Matched picks sit a mean of 2.05 regulons from the perturbed TF's gene (median 8 regulons).
Source: analysis/randmask.py
Confidence: medium-high for the conclusion, with one caveat on the unmatched arm: 71% of measured genes belong to zero CollecTRI regulons, so most "any" picks cannot change a ULM score at all, which is why that arm has zero seed-to-seed variance. The matched arm is the arm that carries the control, and it moves the score by at most 1 point.
Depends on: F-004

## F-009 · 2026-09-18 · lit-scout (entered by main session) · status: proposed
Claim: The 2026 Perturb-seq TF-activity benchmark (Zhu, Han & Wang, Brief Bioinform, DOI 10.1093/bib/bbag513) keeps the perturbed TF's own gene in the expression matrix passed to decoupleR. Its released code has no step that drops, zeroes or masks that gene between preprocessing and scoring. This resolves F-005 part 2b as "does not mask". It does NOT show the paper "has the same leak": its metrics (per-TF AUROC/AUPRC, perturbed vs control cells) differ from rank-among-TFs and nobody has rerun them masked.
Evidence: repo https://github.com/zhuzhe0011/Benchmarking-Methods-for-Inferring-Single-cell-Transcription-Factor-Activity (from the paper's Data and Code Availability statement in the Europe PMC full text, PMC13584069; that section is missing from the local refs/ copy). `dataset/filter.py` reads the perturbed gene's value only to filter cells by perturbation direction (`adata=adata[adata.obs['gene'].isin(t)].copy()`, a row filter), then writes the full matrix (`index=adata.var_names`). `methord/decoupler/dcp.py` does `sc.read_h5ad(file)`, `normalize_total`, `log1p`, then `dc.mt.<method>(adata, Net, tmin=3)` with no gene subsetting. Both files read in full by lit-scout.
Source: the two files above; refs/tf-activity-benchmark-perturbation-2026.md line 45 ("identical initial input matrices").
Confidence: high for decoupleR's input (code read end to end); medium for the other seven methods (shared filtered.h5ad, but their scripts were not read); Supplementary Table S1 not read (binary ZIP, could not be extracted).
Depends on: F-005

## V-009 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed
Claim checked: F-009 — in the released code of the 2026 Perturb-seq TF-activity benchmark (Zhu, Han & Wang, Brief Bioinform, DOI 10.1093/bib/bbag513), the expression matrix handed to decoupleR still contains the perturbed TF's own gene; nothing between preprocessing and scoring drops, zeroes or masks it. Scope of this verdict: CONFIRMED FOR decoupleR's INPUT (all 11 decoupleR scoring methods, all three prior networks). For the other seven tools it is confirmed only that none has a step aimed at the target gene and that all read the same two unmasked artefacts; two of them apply generic expression filters that could drop a lowly expressed TF gene incidentally (below). "Gene kept" is NOT "has the same leak": no masked rerun of their metrics exists, so nothing here licenses writing that the paper's results are inflated, by how much, or for which methods.
How verified: (1) Provenance. Europe PMC fullTextXML for PMC13584069 has `<article-id pub-id-type="doi">10.1093/bib/bbag513</article-id>`, the same title as refs/tf-activity-benchmark-perturbation-2026.md (which lists the same DOI and authors), and `<sec id="sec32"><title>Code availability</title><p>The code to reproduce the results is in GitHub (https://github.com/zhuzhe0011/Benchmarking-Methods-for-Inferring-Single-cell-Transcription-Factor-Activity).` The local refs/ copy indeed lacks that section (grep for github: no hit). (2) Quotes. `git clone --depth 1` into the scratchpad, HEAD b904196 (2026-08-07, single commit "update"). dataset/filter.py line 181 is exactly `adata=adata[adata.obs['gene'].isin(t)].copy()`; `adata.obs['gene']` is the per-cell perturbation label and the index is on axis 0 only, so it is a cell filter. `t` is built at lines 160-179 by comparing mean normalised expression of the perturbed gene in perturbed vs CTRL cells (sign only), and line 163 `if gene not in adata.var_names: continue` means a TF whose gene is absent from the matrix is dropped as a perturbation, so every retained perturbed TF has its gene in the matrix by construction. Lines 192-197 write the CSV with `index=adata.var_names`; line 188 writes filtered.h5ad from the same object. methord/decoupler/dcp.py lines 45-58: `sc.read_h5ad(file)`, `normalize_total`, `log1p`, then `dc.mt.waggr(adata, Net, tmin=3)` and `getattr(dc.mt, method_name)(adata, Net, tmin=3)`; no var-axis indexing anywhere in the file. F-009's `dc.mt.<method>(...)` is a fair paraphrase. (3) Adversarial whole-repo grep (all .py/.R/.sh/.ipynb, patterns var_names, `[:,`, drop, del, isin, highly_variable, mask, != gene, remove, subset, setdiff, %in%, filter_genes, VariableFeatures): no hit removes or zeroes a perturbation-target gene. HVG: filter.py line 62 `highly_variable_genes(adata, subset=True, n_top_genes=4000)` does subset genes, but only on the working copy used for Mixscape/E-test; the saved object is rebuilt at line 93 from `adata2`, copied at line 57 BEFORE the HVG step, so HVG selection does not reach the scored matrix. The only gene filter that does is `sc.pp.filter_genes(min_cells=3)` (line 51), which is generic. Network side: dataset/net_collectri_filter.csv has 36454 edges, 861 TFs and 388 self-edges (net_chip 152, net_tflink_filter 35), so autoregulatory edges were not stripped; decoupler's own preprocessing removes only all-zero features, no self-edge handling. Shipped artefact check (the repo includes one dataset): dataset/Inhibition/Dox/filtered.h5ad is 617 cells x 15498 genes of integer counts (i.e. not the 4000-HVG subset), perturbation TP53 (197 cells) vs CTRL (420); TP53 is in var_names, normalised mean 0.114 CTRL vs 0.095 perturbed; TP53 has a CollecTRI self-edge and sits in 108 other TFs' regulons; result/express/Inhibition/Dox.csv also has 15498 rows including TP53. (4) Supplement. supplementaryFiles ZIP fetched and unpacked with python zipfile (nested supplementary_material_bbag513.zip -> Supplementary_Table_bbag513.xlsx, parsed from the sheet XML since openpyxl is not installed). Table S1 columns are Method | GRN Construction Strategies | Input Requirements | Additional Quality Control | Normalization | Selected Features | Potential Limitations. Row decoupleR: "scRNA-seq count matrix (H5AD format) | — | log-normalized | all". Selected Features is "all" for decoupleR, pySCENIC, PISCES, scMINER, TIGER, metaTF; "Genes detected in ≥1% of cells" for hdWGCNA; "Genes detected in ≥10 cells" for scRegulate (matches `filter_genes(min_cells=10)` in methord/scregulate/regulate.py line 41). A regex over all 12 sheets for mask/exclud/remov/own/itself/self/target gene/target TF/drop finds nothing about target-gene handling. So the supplement agrees with the code and, like the paper, is silent rather than explicit.
Where the kept gene can and cannot act (this is the part "kept" does not settle): result/auc/auc.py computes AUROC/AUPRC per TF from that TF's OWN activity row over its perturbed cells plus CTRL cells (lines 76-93). For a univariate scorer such as ULM the perturbed gene enters its own TF's score only through a self-edge, so for the headline AUROC the TF's mRNA sitting in OTHER TFs' regulons is irrelevant; the direct channel exists for the 388/861 CollecTRI TFs with a self-edge and not for the rest. That is a narrower channel than in our knockTF setup. Other regulons matter for (a) the rank metric (lines 97-129: control-z-scored activity ranked across TFs per cell), where a drop in the gene moves its upstream regulators' scores and those compete with the true TF, direction not obvious; (b) MLM, which is multivariate. For data-driven tools the channel may be wider: the shipped pySCENIC network result/scenic/Inhibition/Dox_scenic_net.csv has the TF listed as its own target in 198 of 233 regulons. One dataset, one method, and no masked rerun, so this is a lead, not a finding.
Caveats on "released code": the repo is not byte-for-byte what produced the paper. dataset/filter.py lines 121, 122, 146 call `.asType(...)` (AttributeError in pandas; line 146 is unconditional on the path every non-CRISPRko dataset takes), and result/auc/auc.py line 86 uses `auprc` before assignment in the Activation branch. The verdict therefore rests on three things agreeing, not on the code alone: the code as published, the shipped filtered.h5ad/CSV, and Table S1 "Selected Features: all". Paper states decoupleR 2.1.1; local check of decoupler internals was on 2.2.0.
Would flip if: the authors state or a corrected release shows a target-gene mask that the public snapshot omits; or any of their 17 filtered.h5ad files (only Dox is public in the repo) lacks a retained perturbed TF in var_names. Separately, "same leak" would become a confirmable claim only with a rerun of at least the Dox/TP53 dataset through dcp.py + auc.py with the TP53 column zeroed and the CollecTRI self-edge removed, reporting AUROC and rank before vs after; predicted from the mechanism above: a measurable AUROC drop for self-edge TFs under ULM, none for TFs without a self-edge.
Severity: medium — closes F-005 part 2b, so the write-up may now say the 2026 benchmark "selects perturbations by the sign of the TF's own mRNA change and does not mask that gene (code, shipped data and Table S1 agree)". It may still NOT say "has the same abundance leak"; README.md and refs/README.md wording flagged in V-005 remains unsupported until the masked rerun exists.

## V-006 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed
Claim checked: F-006 — with the edge-swap shuffle fixed to test duplicates against the live edge set, 10 degree-preserving null graphs give top-10% 0.31 (0.28-0.33) unmasked vs 0.57 on the real TF-TF graph, and 0.26 (0.24-0.27) with the perturbed TF's own gene masked vs 0.28 real and 0.34 masked plain ULM; F-004's conclusion (gain = abundance leak, propagation hurts once masked) stands.
How verified: reran `cd analysis && uv run python leak.py` (11 s, exit 0, both asserts pass). Unmasked: ulm 0.45/0.35; up sum a=0.5 0.57/0.47 (better 0.59 worse 0.37); SHUF x10 mean 0.31/0.19 [0.28-0.33] [0.16-0.22]. Masked: ulm 0.34/0.28; up sum a=0.5 0.28/0.16 (better 0.42 worse 0.57); SHUF x10 mean 0.26/0.16 [0.24-0.27] [0.14-0.20]. Exact values: real 0.5663 unmasked, 0.2760 masked, masked ULM 0.3441; null 0.3065 [0.2796-0.3262] unmasked, 0.2588 [0.2366-0.2724] masked. Every claimed number matches. Code audit from a scratch script (not committed): (a) live-edge check is right: `live` is updated on every accepted swap and both new edges are tested against it; all 10 graphs have exactly 7296 edges, identical in-degree and out-degree vectors (checked independently of the script's assert, which does test edge count + out-degree (sum(1)) + in-degree (sum(0)) for every B in Ashuf). (b) Self-edges: CollecTRI restricted to the scored TFs has 7714 TF-TF edges of which 418 are self-edges; propagate.py line 10 zeroes the diagonal, so the real A has 7296 edges and 0 self-edges, and the shuffle refuses swaps with a==d or c==b, so all 10 nulls also have 0 diagonal entries. Real and null are consistent: self-edges are neither preserved, created nor destroyed because none enter. The autoregulatory path (70.6% of the 279 scored experiments have a self-edge in `net`) lives in ULM itself, identically in the real and null arms, and is what the mask removes. (c) Mixing: 15120 of 20000 attempted swaps accepted (75.6%, 2.07 accepted swaps per edge); 12.7-13.7% of original edges survive per graph, against 12.3-12.6% expected for a fully mixed graph with these degrees (dense hubs force overlap); pairwise overlap between the 10 nulls is 12.3% mean, 12.9% max, i.e. the seeds are as different from each other as from the real graph: independent draws, adequately mixed. Pushing to 100k and 300k attempted swaps: survival 0.124/0.123, unmasked null 0.289 [0.258-0.308] / 0.295 [0.265-0.341], masked null 0.246 [0.226-0.258] / 0.254 [0.237-0.287]. Conclusion unchanged, but the null mean wobbles about 1.5 points between seed sets and at 300k the best masked null draw (0.287) beats the real masked graph (0.276), so "2 points above the null mean, inside a point of the best draw" is a description of this seed set, not a stable margin; the honest summary is "masked real graph is inside the null band". (d) Weak spot in the null: edge signs travel with the source's edge slot, so signed out-degree is preserved but signed in-degree is not (total +/- stays 5875/1421). "up" propagation reads the in-edges of the perturbed TF, so the null preserves how many regulators a TF has but not their activator/repressor mix. Not asserted, not mentioned in F-006; does not rescue the masked gain. (e) leak.py assert is real: 388 own-gene cells checked, all 388 were nonzero before masking, all are zero in X and in `masked.X` (the matrix ULM actually scored), and exactly 388 cells of the matrix differ from the original. Its second clause uses `.any()` where `.all()` would be the stronger test; with this data both pass. (f) `As`: nothing imports it any more (leak.py and randmask.py import `Ashuf`); `As = Ashuf[0]` on controls.py line 28 is dead code. No other script silently changed meaning.
Would flip if: a rerun prints different numbers; or a null that also preserves each TF's signed in-degree, run over >=30 seeds, put the masked real score (0.276) clearly above the null band and above masked ULM (0.344). The second half cannot happen by changing the null (0.276 < 0.344 is null-independent).
Severity: high — this is the control under F-004, the result that reframed the project. Residual issues are low: sign-composition of in-edges not preserved, 10 seeds give a band that moves by about 1.5 points, dead `As` line.

## V-007 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — numbers and "masking is not generically destructive" confirmed; "and nothing else" holds, but on stronger controls than the ones the script ships (its own controls are near no-ops)
Claim checked: F-007 — zeroing one random gene per experiment (any gene, or one matched on number of regulons) leaves ULM at 0.45 and up/sum/a=0.5 propagation at 0.57 with the shuffled null at 0.31, so the 57->28 collapse is caused by removing the perturbed TF's own mRNA value, not by masking as such.

Printed: any ulm 0.452 [0.452-0.452] top5 0.355; any up sum a=0.5 0.566 [0.566-0.566] top5 0.473 better 0.594 worse 0.370; any SHUF 0.307 [0.306-0.307]; matched ulm 0.452 [0.444-0.455] top5 0.356; matched up sum a=0.5 0.566 [0.566-0.566] top5 0.472 better 0.596 worse 0.366; matched SHUF 0.307 [0.306-0.309]; "mean |regulons(pick) - regulons(TF gene)| = 2.05, median regulons(TF gene) = 8". All claimed numbers match. Prediction was stated in the docstring before the run and is numeric. Audit of the control's strength (scratch script, 279 scored experiments x 5 seeds, same picker and seeds): ANY arm: 71.4% of 21985 measured genes are in no regulon (72.6% of picks); median |logFC| of the zeroed gene 0.062, 35.0% of picks were already exactly 0, only 1.6% have |logFC| >= 1.74; 91.1% share no upstream regulator with the perturbed TF. This arm is a no-op and carries nothing, as F-007 itself concedes. MATCHED arm: matching on regulon count works (20.6 vs 22.6 regulons on scored experiments, mean gap 2.69 there; the printed 2.05 and "median 8" are over all 388 experiments including the 109 unscored), the perturbed TF is excluded, but nothing requires the pick to sit in the relevant regulons: mean 3.9 of the TF's 21.2 propagation-upstream regulators are shared, 44.9% of matched picks share none, mean regulator Jaccard 0.052; 11.5% of picks are targets of the perturbed TF and 13.5% are themselves scored TFs (neither excluded). Magnitude: median |logFC| 0.134, 15.1% already exactly 0, 7.0% with |logFC| >= 1, 2.7% >= 1.74, against the perturbed TF's own median -1.74 (every one < 0) in a matrix whose median per-experiment sd is 0.37. So the shipped matched control zeroes a ~0.4 sd value that mostly is not on the leak path, where the real mask zeroes a ~5 sd value that is on it by construction. As committed it cannot support "and nothing else". Sharper controls I ran (top10 ulm / propagated / shuffled): gene drawn from the 20 with highest regulator-set Jaccard to the TF's gene, 3 seeds: 0.453 / 0.565 [0.563-0.566] / 0.306 (median logFC 0.00, 6.9 shared regulators); the most negative logFC among those 20: 0.434 / 0.563 / 0.295 (median -0.77, 7.2 shared); most negative among the top 100: 0.441 / 0.563 / 0.292 (median -1.51, 5.9 shared); any gene with logFC nearest the TF's own, 3 seeds: 0.456 / 0.566 / 0.306 (median -1.45); logFC-matched and regulon-count-matched, 3 seeds: 0.452 / 0.565 / 0.304 (median -1.28, 5.0 shared). None moves propagation by more than 0.3 points; the worst case costs ULM 1.8 points. The claim therefore survives the control it should have shipped with. Why it must: in "up sum" propagation the own gene x_t enters the propagated score of TF t as sum over upstream R of A[R,t]*w[R,t]*x_t, and A[R,t] = w[R,t] = +/-1, so every one of the ~21 upstream regulators adds x_t with the same sign: the method contains an algebraic "plus k * own logFC" term, which no other gene has (a co-regulated gene's terms carry mixed signs and cover about a quarter of the regulators). Split by autoregulation: TFs with a CollecTRI self-edge (n=197) ulm 0.538 -> 0.386 masked, propagated 0.660 -> 0.310; TFs without (n=82) ulm 0.244 -> 0.244 (masking cannot touch plain ULM there), propagated 0.341 -> 0.195. So the ULM 45->34 drop is entirely the self-edge path and the propagation collapse occurs with or without it; both are the same single cell per experiment (V-006: exactly 388 cells change). Assert audit: checks pick != perturbed TF for all 3880 picks, but checks "actually zeroed" only for the final seed of the matched arm; real, partial.
Would flip if: a rerun prints different numbers; or zeroing a non-TF gene that shares most of the perturbed TF's upstream regulators with coherent signs and a comparable (~5 sd) logFC dropped propagated top-10% by more than a few points (my closest attempt, top-100 co-regulated / most negative, -1.51 median, moved it 0.3 points). Note the interpretive limit: the control shows the loss is that one value; it cannot say whether that value is "abundance artifact" or legitimate autoregulatory signal for the 71% with a self-edge. For the 29% without a self-edge the propagation loss (0.341 -> 0.195) has no such defence.
Severity: medium — closes the "would flip if" left open in V-004. F-004/F-006 do not depend on it, but any write-up sentence of the form "masking only removes the leak" does, and should cite the co-regulated/logFC-matched numbers above rather than the any/matched arms.

## F-010 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: The two abundance-leak paths are separable on the network side and they feed different methods. ULM's 0.45 -> 0.34 masking drop is ENTIRELY the autoregulatory self-edge: deleting only the perturbed TF's own edge X->X (gene left in the matrix, still visible to every other regulon) gives 0.344 top-10% (96/279), the same as masking (0.344, 96/279), while deleting gene X from every OTHER TF's regulon and keeping X->X leaves ULM at 0.455 (127/279), i.e. unchanged. Split by self-edge: 197 of 279 experiments (71%) have a self-edge surviving tmin=5 and lose 0.538 -> 0.386 under masking; the other 82 are at 0.244 masked or not (20/82 both ways, top-5% 0.183 -> 0.195), so masking costs the no-self-edge group nothing. The propagation collapse of F-004 is the opposite path: for up/sum/alpha=0.5, removing only the self-edge keeps 0.538 (150/279) of the unmasked 0.566, while removing the gene from the other regulons drops it to 0.326 (91/279) and removing it from the network entirely gives 0.280, matching masked 0.276. So plain ULM sees the knockdown's own mRNA only through autoregulation, and propagation sees it almost entirely through the upstream regulators' regulons.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python selfedge.py` (about 2 min; imports propagate.py and leak.py, which reruns controls.py, so the 0.45/0.57/0.34/0.28 reference tables print above the new ones). Conditions are built by zeroing entries of decoupler's own adjacency matrix and calling decoupler's own ULM kernel (`dc.mt.ulm.func`), one network per perturbed TF; because ULM is univariate, dropping X->X changes only column X and dropping gene X from the others changes only the other columns, so the two conditions are exact complements whose combination ("gene off network", 0.348 ULM / 0.280 propagated) reproduces masking (0.344 / 0.276) to within 4 experiments. Paired vs unmasked ULM: no self-edge better 0.06 worse 0.59; no other regulon better 0.41 worse 0.04; masked better 0.15 worse 0.59. In the no-self-edge group the propagated score falls 0.341 -> 0.207 (no other regulon) and -> 0.195 (masked), reproducing the numbers V-007 reports for that group. CollecTRI has a self-edge for 412 of the 766 TFs scoreable here.
Source: analysis/selfedge.py
Confidence: high for the decomposition — the conditions are exact (the script's assert checks the ULM kernel reproduces propagate.py's score matrix to 1e-8 on the untouched adjacency and that the adjacency is restored), the no-self-edge group is a built-in null (its "no self-edge" row is identical to its ULM row by construction), and the two complementary conditions recombine to the masked numbers. Medium for any per-group comparison of levels: the self-edge group starts much higher than the no-self-edge group (0.538 vs 0.244) and stays higher after masking (0.386 vs 0.244), so self-edge TFs are easier for reasons beyond the leak (likely curation depth and regulon size), and the 82/197 split is not independent (155 TFs over 279 experiments). The masked row differs from "gene off network" in what the gene contributes as a data point (masking leaves it in the regression with y=0), which is why top-5% differs by a point or two.
Depends on: F-004, F-005

## V-010 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — every number reproduces and the decomposition is exact; two wordings to weaken when citing: propagation's loss is 84-90% the other-regulon path (not "almost entirely"), and the conditions reproduce masking on top-10% only. Does NOT license "ULM's knockTF performance is largely autoregulation": 24% of ULM's hits need the self-edge
Claim checked: F-010 — removing only the perturbed TF's CollecTRI self-edge X->X (gene left in the matrix) reproduces plain ULM's whole 0.45->0.34 masking loss, while removing gene X from every other TF's regulon leaves ULM unchanged; for up/sum/alpha=0.5 propagation it is the reverse, the loss comes mainly from gene X sitting in its upstream regulators' regulons. The 82 experiments whose TF has no self-edge lose nothing under masking with plain ULM.
How verified: reran `cd analysis && uv run python selfedge.py` (29 s wall, exit 0, assert passes). Header: "279 experiments, 155 TFs; 197 experiments (71%) ... self-edge surviving tmin=5, 82 without; 0 whose gene is not in the scored matrix"; "self-edges in the pruned network: 412 of 766 TFs". Table 2 (top10 hits/n top5 better worse): ulm 0.452 126/279 0.355; no self-edge 0.344 96/279 0.272 0.06 0.59; no other regulon 0.455 127/279 0.366 0.41 0.04; gene off network 0.348 97/279 0.283 0.15 0.58; masked 0.344 96/279 0.280 0.15 0.59. Table 1: self-edge 0.538 (106/197) -> 0.386 (76/197); no self-edge 0.244 (20/82) -> 0.244 (20/82), top-5% 0.183 -> 0.195. Table 3 propagated: 0.566 158/279; no self-edge 0.538 150/279; no other regulon 0.326 91/279; gene off network 0.280 78/279; masked 0.276 77/279; self-edge group 0.660 / 0.619 / 0.376 / 0.310 / 0.310; no-self-edge group 0.341 (28/82) / 0.341 / 0.207 (17/82) / 0.207 / 0.195 (16/82). Every number in F-010 matches. Agreement with V-007: my independent split there (self-edge read from the raw net, not from decoupler's pruned adjacency) gave n=197/82, ULM 0.538->0.386 and 0.244->0.244, propagated 0.660->0.310 and 0.341->0.195; identical on all eight numbers. F-010's "within 4 experiments" is conservative for top-10% (97 vs 96, 78 vs 77: one experiment each); on top-5% the propagated gap is 0.186 vs 0.165, six experiments, so "reproduces masking" is a top-10% statement. Predictions were numeric and stated in the docstring first (0.35 predicted, 0.344 observed; "near 0.50" predicted, 0.538 observed; "under 2 points" predicted, 0 observed). No gain is claimed, so the shuffled-network control is not required; the masked control is in the script and reported.
Code audit (scratch scripts audit_f010.py / audit2_f010.py in the session scratchpad, not committed; decoupler 2.2.0 read from mt/_ulm.py, pp/net.py, mt/_run.py, nothing under .venv touched): (a) Assert, first clause: real and on the untouched adjacency; es_full is computed at line 47 before any edit, max |es_full - propagate.S| = 0.0 exactly, sources == tfs (766, same order). Second clause is WEAKER than F-010's Confidence field says: it tests only that the 412 self-edge cells are nonzero after the run, not that the adjacency is restored. I checked restoration properly: post-run adjm is np.array_equal to a fresh dc.pp.adjmat(var, pnet) over all 21935 x 766 cells (40728 nonzeros both). Order dependence: rebuilt "no other regulon" from a fresh copy of the adjacency per TF, in reverse TF order; max |diff| vs the script's matrix = 0.0. No leaked zero. The all-self-edges-removed matrix is a .copy(), so it never touches adjm. Scoring a row subset is safe because the kernel's only cross-row term is a scalar mean that cancels against the centred adjacency. (b) Edits equal real edge deletion: public `dc.mt.ulm(sub, edge_deleted_pnet, tmin=0, empty=False)` on the long-format net for 12 random perturbed TFs / 29 experiments, both conditions, max |diff| 4.5e-12 vs the script. (With the default empty=True the public call on a few rows drops all-zero genes and differs by up to 2.3; that is the comparison's artefact, not the script's.) (c) Per-experiment and correctly assembled: "no self-edge" changes exactly 197 cells, all of them the perturbed TF's own cell; "no other regulon" changes only columns whose regulon contains gene X (median 12 per experiment) and never X's own column or a non-member column (largest stray diff 8e-12, float noise from batch size); "gene off network" built directly by zeroing the whole row matches the script's column splice to 2.9e-12. All five condition matrices are 388 x 766 with zero NaN, so every rank is over the same 766 TFs. (d) No re-pruning: one perturbed TF (TOX, 1 experiment) has a self-edge and exactly 5 targets, so is scored on 4; excluding it gives 96/278 = 0.345 vs 0.344. Under "no other regulon" up to 3 competitor TFs fall to 4 targets in 40 experiments; they stay in the ranking, and the public-API check in (b) shows scores are what decoupler gives at tmin=0. Immaterial. (e) Propagation: prop[] applies propagate(v, A, .5, "up", "sum") to each full condition matrix, so under "no other regulon" all 5902 (experiment, upstream regulator) scores are the recomputed ones (5902 of 5902 differ from the full-network score). A is array-equal to a fresh rebuild from the net, diagonal 0; nothing assigns to it.
Wording. "ENTIRELY" for ULM: supported at the level claimed. 96 vs 96 hits, the two hit sets differ by one experiment each way (95 shared), Spearman of the rank fractions 0.9995, median fraction 0.214 vs 0.208; the other-regulon path alone moves ULM by +1 hit (median fraction 0.1384 -> 0.1371, and "better 0.41 worse 0.04" says competitors holding gene X slightly HURT the true TF, they do not help). "Almost entirely" for propagation: weaken. Of the 80 hits lost (158 -> 78), the self-edge costs 8 removed first or 13 removed last (10-16%), the other-regulon path 67 first or 72 last (84-90%), interaction 5 hits (the paths overlap, hence 0.538 and 0.326 vs 0.280). Within the self-edge group: self-edge 12-19%, other path 81-88% of 69 lost hits. In the no-self-edge group it is 100% (28 -> 17, self-edge rows identical by construction). Cite as "mostly (84-90% of lost hits) through the upstream regulators' regulons; the self-edge adds 10-16% because it moves the TF's own ULM term".
Confound (level gap 0.538 vs 0.244, and 0.386 vs 0.244 after). Regulon size differs by about 4x: experiments median 97 targets (IQR 62-294) with a self-edge vs 23 (10-55) without; TF-level 86 vs 21. Size terciles over all 279 (n, ULM, no self-edge): low no-SE n=57 0.158 0.158 | SE n=36 0.583 0.250; mid no-SE n=21 0.476 0.476 | SE n=72 0.528 0.333; high no-SE n=4 0.250 0.250 | SE n=89 0.528 0.483. Reweighting the self-edge group to the no-self-edge group's size mix: unmasked 0.566 (terciles) / 0.528 (quintiles) vs 0.244, so the UNMASKED gap is not size; after self-edge removal 0.283 / 0.233 vs 0.244, so the RESIDUAL gap (0.386 vs 0.244) is all size. At matched size a self-edge TF without its self-edge looks like a no-self-edge TF. Restricted to regulons <= 60 targets: self-edge n=44 0.545 -> 0.227, no self-edge n=64 0.156. The self-edge's value is size-dependent inside the self-edge group: own-size terciles (median 45 / 98 / 425 targets) 0.507 -> 0.246 (51% of hits lost), 0.540 -> 0.365 (32%), 0.569 -> 0.554 (3%): one ~5 sd gene dominates a small regulon and vanishes in a large one. The number that decides the sentence: 33 hits lost, 3 gained, net 30 of 126 = 23.8% of ULM's top-10% hits need the self-edge (30.6% of the margin above the 0.10 chance rate); 76% survive. One row per TF (96 with, 59 without): 0.398 -> 0.277 overall, self-edge TFs 0.524 -> 0.328, others 0.194. So "ULM's knockTF performance is largely autoregulation" is NOT supported; supported is "about a quarter of ULM's knockTF hits, and about half of those for self-edge TFs with under ~60 targets, come through the autoregulatory edge; for regulons of several hundred targets it contributes nothing".
Would flip if: a rerun prints different numbers; or an order-randomised or fresh-copy rebuild of the per-TF networks gave a different "no other regulon" matrix (it gives max |diff| 0.0); or deleting the edges from the long-format net through the public API disagreed with the zeroed adjacency (agrees to 4.5e-12). The "entirely" reading would flip if the no-self-edge and masked hit sets disagreed on more than a handful of experiments (they disagree on 2 of 279). The size reading would flip if a size-matched comparison at TF level (one row per TF, matched on target count) left the self-edge group clearly above the no-self-edge group AFTER self-edge removal; on experiment-level reweighting it does not (0.23-0.28 vs 0.244), but the low-size self-edge cell is 36 experiments and the high-size no-self-edge cell is 4, so the matched estimate carries roughly +/-0.07.
Severity: high — this pins down what the 34% masked baseline in CLAUDE.md removes (for plain ULM: one edge per TF, nothing else) and tells V-009's open question what to expect (a ULM self-edge effect, concentrated in small regulons). Owed, not blocking: (1) selfedge.py's assert should compare the whole adjacency to a saved copy (np.array_equal) instead of the 412 self-edge cells, since Confidence cites it as a restoration check; (2) wording changes above when F-010 is cited in README.md; (3) the interpretive limit from V-007 stands: removing X->X shows the path, it cannot say whether that edge is abundance artefact or genuine autoregulatory signal, and because every knockdown has own logFC < 0 (V-005) positive and negative self-edges are not separated here; a split of the 197 by self-edge sign would show whether negative autoregulators are being penalised by the unmasked benchmark.

## F-011 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: The sign of the CollecTRI self-edge decides which direction the unmasked knockTF benchmark pushes the true TF, and the 197 self-edge experiments are 176 positive / 21 negative, so the leak inflates far more experiments than it deflates. Every knockdown has type_p = -1 and own logFC < 0, so a POSITIVE self-edge lowers the TF's ULM score and raises -1*score (rewarded) while a NEGATIVE one does the reverse (penalised). Removing only the edge X->X (gene left in the matrix) costs the positive group 0.557 -> 0.369 top-10% (98/176 -> 65/176) and 0.432 -> 0.290 top-5%, paired worse 0.94 better 0.00; the negative group instead GAINS, 0.381 -> 0.524 top-10% (8/21 -> 11/21) and 0.381 -> 0.476 top-5%, paired better 0.81 worse 0.00. n=21 over 12 TFs is a count, not a rate: the gain is 3 experiments. Masking reproduces edge removal in both groups (positive 65/176 both, top-5% 51 vs 52; negative 11/21 and 10/21 identical), as F-010 predicts for univariate ULM. The effect is concentrated in small regulons: positive-sign experiments with <= 60 targets (n=41, 31 TFs) fall 0.585 -> 0.195 (24/41 -> 8/41) on edge removal, against 0.557 -> 0.369 for the whole positive group; the negative-sign <= 60 cell is n=3 and is reported as counts only (0/3 -> 2/3). Median regulon size differs by sign: 93 targets positive, 330 negative, 23 for the 82 no-self-edge experiments, so sign is confounded with size and with which TFs are deeply curated.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python selfsign.py` (about 2.5 min; imports selfedge.py, which reruns propagate.py, controls.py and leak.py, so F-001/F-004/F-006/F-010's tables print above the new block 4). Conditions, ULM kernel and evaluate() are selfedge.py's, unchanged; this script only regroups its per-experiment rank fractions by np.sign of the perturbed TF's own cell in decoupler's pruned adjacency and adds regulon size. Self-edge weights in CollecTRI are exactly +/-1 here. Predictions were stated in the docstring first: positive 0.56 -> 0.38 (observed 0.557 -> 0.369), negative flat-or-gaining by about +3 points (observed +14.3 points, i.e. 3 of 21 experiments, direction right and magnitude larger than predicted but within what 21 experiments can resolve). No gain over ULM is claimed, so no shuffled network; "no self-edge" and "masked" are the controls and both are in the table. The 82 no-self-edge experiments are unmoved by either condition (20/82 throughout), as in F-010.
Source: analysis/selfsign.py
Confidence: medium-high for the positive group (n=176 over 84 TFs, a 33-experiment swing, and the mechanism is algebraic: one signed term in a univariate score). LOW for the negative group: 21 experiments over 12 TFs, so the 3-experiment gain has a binomial spread of roughly +/-10 points, and negative autoregulators have 14x the median regulon of the reference group, so size, curation depth and sign cannot be separated here. Experiments are not independent (155 TFs over 279 experiments); no TF-level rerun (T-018 owes that). "Penalised" is measured against the same benchmark that rewards the positive group, not against ground truth: nothing here says the negative group's true activity is better estimated, only that the unmasked scoring moves it the wrong way.
Depends on: F-010

## V-011 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — every number reproduces and the sign argument holds end to end (176/176 and 21/21 own-scores move the predicted way). Two wordings to weaken when citing: "inflates far more than it deflates" is a statement about knockTF x CollecTRI's sign labels, and a +1 self-edge in CollecTRI means "activating OR no sign information", not "positive autoregulator"

Sign logic (scratch audit_f011.py / audit2_f011.py / audit3_f011.py in the session scratchpad, not committed; nothing under .venv touched). (a) Raw data/collectri.parquet: weights are exactly {+1: 37168, -1: 5822}, no duplicate (source, target) rows, 461 self-edges (428 at +1, 33 at -1). Reading the self-edge from the raw net for the 279 experiments gives 176 / 21 / 82, exactly equal cell by cell to the sign selfsign.py reads from adjm_orig[var_i[tf], src_i[tf]] (the perturbed TF's own row, own column of decoupler's pruned adjacency). Pruned scoreable network: 383 at +1, 29 at -1 (412, as F-010). (b) type_p is -1 for all obs; own logFC < 0 for 279 of 279 (largest in the self-edge group -1.008, no NaN). (c) Code path: evaluate() ranks type_p * score descending, ULM's score is t of corr(adjacency column, logFC row) (decoupler mt/_ulm.py), so a +1 weight on a gene with negative logFC pushes the slope down and -1*score up. Measured, not argued: score(full) < score(no self-edge) in 176 of 176 positive experiments (median -0.48 t-units), score(full) > score(no self-edge) in 21 of 21 negative (median +0.41), exactly 0 change in 82 of 82 without a self-edge; S_noself differs from es_full in exactly 197 cells. (d) Real edge deletion: public `dc.mt.ulm(sub, pnet minus the X->X row, tmin=0, empty=False)` for all 12 negative TFs / 21 experiments agrees with the script's es_ns to 6.0e-13 (sub must be built from the extracted 21935-gene matrix; slicing the 21985-gene adata with empty=False keeps 50 all-zero genes and differs by 0.019, my comparison's artefact, as V-010 noted for empty=True).
Cleanups (T-013, T-019). No git history, so checked by reading and by behaviour. controls.py: no `As` token anywhere in analysis/*.py, nothing imports it (leak.py and randmask.py import only shuffle_preserving_degree and Ashuf); line 27 is a comment stating signed out-degree is preserved and signed in-degree is not, matching V-006. leak.py assert is `(X.values[r, c] == 0).all() and (adata.to_df().values[r, c] != 0).all()` and passes. selfedge.py: `adjm_orig = adjm.copy()` sits at line 40, before the first edit (line 53 edits a copy, lines 66-68 edit and restore adjm), and the assert's second clause is `np.array_equal(adjm, adjm_orig)`; zeroing one non-self cell makes it False where the old 412-cell check would still pass. `cd analysis && uv run python selfedge.py` (exit 0): stdout is byte-identical, warnings and timings aside, to both selfedge_before.txt and selfedge_after.txt, and carries F-010's numbers (126/279, 96/279, 127/279, 97/279, 96/279; 106/197 -> 76/197; 20/82 both; propagated 158 / 150 / 91 / 78 / 77).
