Negative group. The 12 TFs (experiments, targets): HIF1A 7 (447), ATF3 3 (108), MYC 2 (845), BCL6 (99), CEBPD (94), CTNNB1 (331), FOS (330), MSX1 (26), NR2E1 (17), WT1 (151), YY1 (252), ZHX2 (11). HIF1A is 33% of the group; HIF1A + MYC are 7 of the 8 baseline hits (BCL6 the other), so "0.381" is mostly two TFs. Paired: 17 of 21 improve in rank, 0 worsen, 4 tie, and all 4 ties are already rank 1 of 766 (HIF1A x2, MYC x2) so cannot improve; 0.81 is confirmed and is a floor. Exact two-sided sign test, ties dropped: p = 1.5e-5 at experiment level (not independent). One row per TF (median rank fraction): 11 of 12 better, 0 worse, 1 tie (MYC, rank 1), p = 0.00098; without HIF1A 10 of 11. Median shift -0.073 of rank fraction = -56 ranks of 766 (median fraction 0.184 -> 0.095); TF-level top-10% 3 -> 5 of 12. Hit transitions: 3 gained, 0 lost, exact McNemar p = 0.25. The three that cross: HIF1A DataSet_01_156 (rank 109 -> 36, own logFC -2.85), MSX1 (114 -> 24, 26 targets), NR2E1 (180 -> 73, 17 targets). Leave one TF out: gain stays +3 except without HIF1A (3 -> 5 of 14), MSX1 or NR2E1 (+2 each). So "the unmasked benchmark penalises negative-self-edge TFs" IS supported as a rank-shift statement, at TF level, p = 0.001, and the direction is algebraic anyway (21 of 21 scores move the predicted way); "by 14 points of top-10%" is NOT a rate and the count alone would not carry the claim. F-011's Confidence should read: high for direction, low for top-10% magnitude.
Positive group concentration: top 5 TFs STAT3 10, TP53 8, FLI1 6, NR2F2 6, STAT1 6 = 36 of 176 (20%), largest single TF 5.7%. Paired 0 better / 165 worse / 11 ties; per TF 0 better / 81 worse / 3 ties of 84 (p = 8e-25); TF-level top-10% 42 -> 25 of 84; hits 33 lost, 0 gained. Not dominated by any TF. Note V-010's "33 lost, 3 gained" now resolves fully: all 33 losses are +1 self-edges, all 3 gains are -1.
Size: no contradiction with V-010. V-010's "contributes nothing for several hundred targets" was a hit-count statement; in ranks the effect is small and nonzero, and it is symmetric by sign at matched size. Median change in rank (of 766) on self-edge removal, positive vs negative: <= 60 targets +97 (n=41, 24 -> 8 hits) vs -107 (n=3, 0 -> 2); 61-150 +44.5 (n=76, 41 -> 27) vs -65 (n=5, 1 -> 1); 151-300 +16 (n=25, 14 -> 11) vs -63 (n=2, 0 -> 0); 301-600 +9 (n=21, 5 TFs, 11 -> 11) vs -9 (n=9, 3 TFs, 5 -> 6); > 600 +5 (n=13, 8 -> 8) vs 0 (n=2, both rank 1). At >= 300 targets: positive 19 -> 19 hits, negative 7 -> 8. The score perturbation follows one size law in both groups (log |d score| per unit own logFC vs log size: slope -0.57 positive, -0.42 negative; median |d score| 0.48 vs 0.41 t-units). The negative group's larger overall median shift (-56 vs +33 ranks) is a starting-rank effect, not a sign or size effect: 62% of negative experiments start outside the top 10% against 44% of positive, and a TF in the dense middle of the ranking moves 40-113 ranks for the same fraction of a t-unit that moves a top-10% TF 14 ranks (positive) or 0.5 (negative). Of the 3 gained hits, 2 are regulons <= 60 targets, which is V-010's size finding again; the third is a 447-target HIF1A experiment that started 32 ranks outside the line with a large own logFC. So "just enough to cross the line for 3 experiments" is the right reading, and F-011's "concentrated in small regulons" holds in both signs.
Wording. (1) "the leak inflates far more experiments than it deflates": supported as 165 experiments pushed up vs 17 pushed down (176 vs 21 by label) in knockTF scored with CollecTRI; it needs that qualifier. The ratio is mostly CollecTRI's: 93% of its self-edges are +1 (428 of 461 raw; 383 of 412 scoreable), vs 88% of knockTF's self-edge TFs (84 of 96) and 89% of experiments. (2) refs/muller-dott-2023-collectri.md, Methods, "Assigning the TF-gene mode of regulation": "TF-gene interactions without any information were assigned an activating mode of regulation." A +1 self-edge therefore means "curated activating OR sign unknown"; F-011's Claim says "positive self-edge" (fine) but its Confidence says "negative autoregulators" and T-017/T-020 say "positive autoregulators" (not licensed for the +1 group; the -1 group is curated, so "negative autoregulator per CollecTRI" is fair). The mechanism does not care why the label is +1, so the numbers stand. (3) F-011's own caveat that "penalised" is relative to the benchmark, not to ground truth, is correct and should travel with any citation. (4) Runtime is 30 s here, not "about 2.5 min"; immaterial.
Would flip if: a rerun prints different numbers; the raw-net self-edge signs disagreed with the adjacency cells the script reads (they are equal on all 279); any positive experiment's own score rose, or any negative one's fell, when X->X is present (0 of 197 do); or the public-API edge deletion disagreed with es_ns (6e-13). The "penalised" reading for the -1 group would flip if a TF-level rerun found TFs whose rank worsens on self-edge removal: currently 0 of 12, and by the algebra that can only happen through rank ties or a positive own logFC, which knockTF does not contain. The magnitude reading (+3 hits) has nothing to flip: it is already not claimed as a rate.
Severity: medium — nothing built so far depends on the -1 group, but T-021's proposed pooling by self-edge sign and T-016's "drop the self-edge at scoring time" baseline both inherit it: dropping the self-edge removes a penalty for 12 TFs as well as a reward for 84, and the benchmark's net movement (-30 hits) hides a +3. Owed, not blocking: (1) T-020 as filed, but the TF-level sign test is now done here (11/12, p = 0.001; 81/84), so what T-020 still owes is the size-matched TF-level comparison, where the negative cells are 3 TFs or fewer per size bin and may simply be unanswerable in knockTF; (2) strengthen selfsign.py's assert to the direction check in (c); (3) a count of how many of the 84 "+1" self-edge TFs have a curated sign versus the default-activating fill, which needs CollecTRI's per-edge PMID/sign evidence (not in data/collectri.parquet as fetched by decoupler); until then write "+1 self-edge", not "positive autoregulator"; (4) when citing in README.md: "in knockTF scored with CollecTRI", direction high / magnitude low, and fix the runtime note.

## F-008 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: Most of Huber-IRLS ULM's deficit against plain ULM on knockTF is abundance-leak clipping, not lost activity signal, but Huber is still worse once the leak is closed. With the perturbed TF's own gene zeroed before scoring (the leak.py mask, X.loc[exp, tf] = 0), masked ULM is top-10% 0.34 / top-5% 0.28 and masked Huber is 0.30 / 0.21, Huber better on 40% of experiments and worse on 59% (n=279). Unmasked, the same run reproduces F-003 exactly: ULM 0.45 / 0.35 vs Huber 0.32 / 0.23, better 0.33 worse 0.66. So the 13-14 point unmasked top-10% deficit shrinks to 4 points under masking; about two thirds of it was Huber downweighting the knockdown's own mRNA drop, which is a benchmark artifact, not activity. Huber does not reach masked ULM, so no gain is claimed and no shuffled-network control is owed; README line 16 ("signal lives in the strongly responding targets") survives, but only as a 4-point effect, not a 13-point one. Top-5% is the exception: masking costs ULM 7 points (0.35 -> 0.28) and Huber only 2 (0.23 -> 0.21), and Huber stays below ULM there too.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python masked_huber.py` (about 55 min with a competing job; imports robust_ulm.py, which reruns the unmasked arm). Printed table: ulm unmasked 0.45/0.35; huber unmasked 0.32/0.23 better 0.33 worse 0.66; ulm MASKED 0.34/0.28; huber MASKED 0.30/0.21 better 0.40 worse 0.59. 388 of 388 experiments had their own gene zeroed; 279 scored. Median rank fraction masked: ulm 0.208, huber 0.285; wrong-sign masked: ulm 0.29, huber 0.33. Predicted before the run: masked ULM 0.34 (hit), masked Huber 0.26 (actual 0.30, so the deficit is smaller than predicted; the ordering predicted is right).
Source: analysis/masked_huber.py, analysis/robust_ulm.py, analysis/leak.py
Confidence: high for the numbers (deterministic, same data and same evaluate() as F-003, assert on the mask passed: exactly one cell zeroed per scoreable row, always the perturbed TF's own column, and the Huber arm saw the same masked matrix as the ULM arm). Medium for the interpretation "two thirds was leak-clipping": both arms lose accuracy under masking, so the shrinking gap could also be a floor effect (a masked benchmark is harder for everything, compressing differences) rather than Huber specifically clipping the leak. Nothing here measures the Huber weight placed on the TF's own gene directly. Deliberately uses robust_ulm.py's Huber as written, including the unweighted intercept and uncentered MAD V-003 flagged; V-003 showed those defects do not drive the loss, and the point was to compare against the implementation F-003 used.
Depends on: F-003, F-005

## V-008 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — every number reproduces, and the mechanism F-008 marked "medium" and unmeasured is now measured: Huber gives the perturbed TF's own gene a median weight of 0.09 in the TF's own regression, and pinning that ONE weight to 1 takes unmasked Huber from 88 to 156 top-10% hits. Two wordings to weaken when citing: "about two thirds" is the top-10% hit count only (mean paired rank gap and top-5% shrink by about half, gap of medians by a quarter), and "Huber is still worse once the leak is closed" holds on the paired and top-5% summaries but NOT on top-10% (12 hits, exact McNemar p = 0.10, TF-cluster CI includes 0). New and not in F-008: Huber's deficit, masked or not, is confined to the 197 self-edge experiments; in the 82 no-self-edge experiments Huber equals ULM (20/82 vs 20/82)
Claim checked: F-008 — zeroing the knocked-down TF's own gene (the leak.py mask) before scoring shrinks Huber-IRLS ULM's top-10% deficit against plain ULM from 14 points (0.45 vs 0.32) to 4 (0.34 vs 0.30), paired better/worse 0.33/0.66 -> 0.40/0.59; the entry reads this as "about two thirds of the deficit was Huber downweighting the knockdown's own mRNA drop", with Huber still worse afterwards, and itself flags a floor effect as the untested alternative.
How verified: (1) Numbers. Reran `cd analysis && uv run python masked_huber.py` (about 55 min wall, exit 0, assert passes). Printed: ulm n=279 top10%=0.45 top5%=0.35 median_frac=0.138 wrong_sign=0.23; huber_ulm 0.32 0.23 0.243 0.29; paired better 0.33 worse 0.66; ulm masked 0.34 0.28 0.208 0.29; huber masked 0.30 0.21 0.285 0.33; table huber MASKED 0.30/0.21 better 0.40 worse 0.59; "n=279 experiments scored; 388 of 388 experiments had their own gene zeroed"; "unmasked deficit = +0.14; masked deficit = +0.04". Stdout is byte-identical (warnings aside) to the experimentalist's mh.log. Every number in F-008 matches. Exact counts from my per-experiment table: ULM 126, Huber 88, masked ULM 96, masked Huber 84 of 279 (top-5%: 99 / 63 / 78 / 59), so the deficits are 38 -> 12 hits (13.6 -> 4.3 points). Prediction was numeric and in the docstring first (masked Huber 0.26 predicted, 0.30 observed). No gain is claimed (masked Huber < masked ULM), so no shuffled network is owed; the masked control is the experiment. (2) Code audit. Mask: masked_huber.py lines 26-27 are the same statement as leak.py lines 8-9 (`X.loc[exp, tf] = 0` on adata.to_df(), guarded by `tf in X.columns`), so it is the same 388 cells V-006 audited. Huber arm: line 34 builds Ym from the masked X and line 35 scores Ym; the unmasked `Y` imported from robust_ulm is used only inside the assert. `W` is the network and is rightly shared. Both arms: my scratch run asserts nan_to_num(masked.X) is array-equal to Ym, and adata.X has 0 NaN cells, so the nan_to_num is a no-op. Assert is real: clause 1 own cells are 0 in Ym, clause 2 one cell per row, clause 3 the set of cells where Ym differs from Y is exactly the own-gene cells that were nonzero, so a mask on the wrong axis, a stray zero, or a Huber input that was not masked at those cells all fail it. What it does not test is that `T` was computed from Ym; that is by reading line 35. (3) Per-experiment table (scratch v008_per_exp.py / v008_analyze.py / v008_extra.py in the session scratchpad, not committed, nothing under .venv touched): ALL 279 experiments, not a subset. `huber_ulm` is exec'd verbatim from robust_ulm.py's source via ast (importing the module would rerun the 25-min pass) and run in 24 processes, 9 min; it reproduces all four arms above to the hit, including medians 0.1384 / 0.2428 / 0.2076 / 0.2846, so the table is the script's computation. Self-edge read from W[gene X, column X]: 197 / 82, +1 176, -1 21, equal to V-010 and V-011.
(a) No-self-edge group (n=82, 59 TFs). Masking moves nothing there for either method, as the algebra requires: ULM 20 -> 20, Huber 20 -> 20, Huber rank fraction identical in 61 of 82 and max |change| 0.004 (max own-score change 0.17 t-units, via the mean shift and competitors that hold gene X). But the premise of the test fails: there is NO Huber deficit in this group to shrink or persist. Top-10% 20 vs 20 both ways, top-5% deficit 0 -> +1, paired better 0.45 worse 0.54 both ways, mean rank gap +0.009 both ways, TF-level 29 better / 30 worse (p = 1). So (a) is passed but uninformative about floor vs clipping, and it says something F-008 does not: the whole deficit is in the self-edge group. Self-edge n=197: ULM 106 / Huber 68 unmasked (deficit 38, top-5% 36, better 0.27 worse 0.71, mean gap +0.081) -> 76 / 64 masked (deficit 12, top-5% 18, better 0.38 worse 0.61, mean gap +0.035). Note the three levels (0.244 -> deficit 0; 0.386 -> 6 points; 0.538 -> 19 points) are ALSO what a compression-with-level story predicts, so group splits cannot separate the two readings; (b) and the counterfactual below do.
(b) Direct weight. Final Huber weight on the perturbed TF's own gene in the TF's own regression, unmasked, self-edge experiments: median 0.094 (IQR 0.051-0.153, mean 0.124); below 1 in 99%, below 0.5 in 97%; lower than 99% of the same TF's other targets (median percentile 0.010). Own logFC median -1.68 = -5.5 per-experiment sd. Against that, mean weight on targets 0.709 and non-targets 0.835 (V-003's 0.69 / 0.82 on 30). +1 group 0.092, -1 group 0.100; in the no-self-edge group the gene is a non-target and gets 0.086. Consequence, measured: under masking the TF's own score moves in the same direction for ULM and Huber in 197 of 197 self-edge experiments, but Huber's moves 0.37x as far (median |d t| 0.472 ULM vs 0.173 Huber; median per-experiment ratio 0.29). Hit transitions under masking: ULM loses 33 and gains 3 (V-010's numbers exactly; all 33 are +1 self-edges), Huber loses 5 and gains 1. Of ULM's 33 leak-dependent hits Huber had only 6 while unmasked. So 38 - 12 = 26 = (30 net ULM hits that came through the self-edge cell) - (4 net Huber hits that did), and since masking changes one cell per experiment and that cell reaches a univariate score only through X->X, there is nothing else the shrink can be.
Counterfactual (mine, not in the repo): the same IRLS with w[own gene, own TF column] pinned to 1 at every iteration, all else identical, unmasked data. Top-10% 156/279 = 0.559 (top-5% 0.470, median fraction 0.063), vs Huber 88 and ULM 126; paired vs ULM better 0.60 worse 0.37. One weight per experiment is worth 68 hits, so the clipping is causal and more than sufficient for the 38-hit deficit. It overshoots ULM because with every other gene at mean weight about 0.7-0.8 and a smaller robust sigma, an unclipped 5.5 sd gene has MORE relative leverage than under least squares; so this is "Huber plus extra leak", not a fair Huber, and 156 must never be cited as a gain. By sign it moves as F-011 predicts: +1 group 59 -> 130 (ULM 98), -1 group 9 -> 6 (ULM 8; paired vs ULM better 0.10 worse 0.86), no-self-edge 20 -> 20 (pinning a non-target's weight does nothing to the hits).
(c) By self-edge sign. +1 (n=176): deficit 39 -> 11 hits, top-5% 35 -> 15, better/worse 0.26/0.72 -> 0.38/0.61, mean gap +0.092 -> +0.033. -1 (n=21, 12 TFs, HIF1A 7 of 21): unmasked Huber 9 vs ULM 8 (deficit -1), masked 10 vs 11 (+1); top-5% +1 -> +3; better/worse 0.43/0.57 -> 0.38/0.62; mean gap -0.010 -> +0.047. Direction as predicted (clipping the own gene removes a penalty for -1 TFs, so unmasked Huber is level with or ahead of ULM there and falls behind once ULM is also rid of the cell); a 2-hit swing on 21 experiments is a count, not a rate. Size: self-edge <= 60 targets (n=44) deficit 16 -> 2 hits (ULM 24 -> 9, Huber 8 -> 7), > 60 targets (n=153) 22 -> 10, i.e. the clipped-leak share is largest where V-010 found the self-edge matters most.
Wording. (i) "Most / about two thirds" is the top-10% hit count: 26 of 38 = 68% (TF-cluster bootstrap over 155 TFs, 2000 draws: 0.69, 95% CI 0.40-1.09). Other summaries of the same run: top-5% deficit 36 -> 19 hits (47%); mean paired rank gap +0.060 -> +0.027 (55%; bootstrap 0.55, CI 0.33-0.91); median paired gap +0.0235 -> +0.0104 (56%); paired better/worse 0.326/0.656 -> 0.398/0.588 (worse-minus-better 0.33 -> 0.19, 42%); gap of medians 0.104 -> 0.077 (26%). The 68% is the largest of these because the leak moves experiments sitting near the 10% line. Cite as "about half of Huber's paired rank deficit, and two thirds of its top-10% hit deficit (38 -> 12 of 279), was the own-gene cell", and lead with the mechanism numbers (own-gene weight 0.09; masking costs ULM 33 hits and Huber 5), not with 14 -> 4. (ii) "Huber is still worse once the leak is closed": supported on paired ranks (111 better / 164 worse, exact sign p = 0.002; TF level 65 / 89, p = 0.063; self-edge TFs 36 / 59, p = 0.02; mean gap +0.027, TF-cluster CI +0.004 to +0.050) and on top-5% (29 ULM-only vs 10 Huber-only, McNemar p = 0.003; deficit CI +0.015 to +0.119). NOT supported on top-10%: 28 ULM-only vs 16 Huber-only, exact McNemar p = 0.096, deficit CI -0.008 to +0.091. So "README line 16 survives as a 4-point effect" should read "survives as a small paired-rank and top-5% effect; the masked top-10% difference is not distinguishable from zero". (iii) The residual is not general: masked, size tercile x self-edge gives deficits of 0 / 2 (low), 1 / 2 (mid), -1 / 8 (high) hits for no-self-edge / self-edge, so 8 of the 12 residual hits are large-regulon self-edge TFs (n=89, median 306 targets, better 0.34 worse 0.63) and the no-self-edge group shows none at any size (the high cell there is n=4). "Signal lives in the strongly responding targets" is therefore untested as an explanation of the residual; it is a description of where the residual is not (small regulons, no-self-edge TFs). (iv) F-008's "floor effect" caveat can be retired as a competing explanation of the SHRINK (one cell, one edge, weight 0.09, hit transitions 33 vs 5); it remains a fair caution about reading the masked 4 points as a precise size.
Would flip if: a rerun prints different numbers; or the own-gene weight were not small (it is below 0.5 in 97% of self-edge experiments); or masking moved Huber's hits in the no-self-edge group (20 -> 20, max rank-fraction change 0.004); or pinning the own-gene weight failed to restore hits in the +1 group (59 -> 130). The "still worse" half would flip to "indistinguishable" if a converged, weighted-intercept Huber (V-003's corrected version, run masked on all 279) closed the paired gap; V-003 (c) saw 0.33 vs 0.37 on 30 unmasked experiments, so this is not expected, but it has not been run masked. The "about half / two thirds" share is a statement about knockTF x CollecTRI's 176:21 sign mix (F-011); with more -1 self-edges the clipped cell would help Huber as often as hurt it and the share would fall.
Severity: low-medium — nothing built depends on Huber, and robust losses stay closed (masked Huber never reaches masked ULM, paired p = 0.002). It matters for two sentences: F-003 may now be cited as "worse on unmasked knockTF, about half to two thirds of that being own-gene clipping; on masked knockTF worse on paired rank and top-5%, not on top-10%", and README line 16 needs the narrowing in (ii)-(iii). It also generalises: ANY outlier-robust or rank-based scorer will clip a 5.5 sd own-gene cell and so look worse than ULM on unmasked knockTF for self-edge TFs, for a reason that has nothing to do with activity, which is one more argument for CLAUDE.md's masked-baseline rule. Owed, not blocking: (1) masked_huber.py should print the 197/82 split and the own-gene weight (huber_ulm already returns w; the script discards it), so the mechanism is in the repo and not only in this verdict; (2) its assert could add that S_hub_m differs from robust_ulm's S2, which would catch a Huber arm accidentally fed Y; (3) if README cites the residual, a masked run of V-003's corrected Huber; (4) the pinned-weight counterfactual is scratch only, and if it is ever committed it needs a docstring line saying 0.56 is leak amplification, not a gain.

## F-014 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: At one row per TF (median rank fraction over that TF's experiments, hit = median <= 0.10) ULM+CollecTRI on knockTF is 0.361 top-10% (56/155 TFs), 0.265 with the perturbed TF's self-edge removed and 0.265 masked (41/155, top-5% 0.258 -> 0.187 / 0.194; paired per TF: self-edge removal better for 0.07, worse for 0.52). Self-edge TFs (n=96) 0.469 -> 0.312, no-self-edge TFs (n=59) 0.186 either way. V-010's TF-level 0.398 -> 0.277 used a different collapse rule (mean over TFs of that TF's experiment-level hit rate); that rule reproduces exactly here (0.398 / 0.277 / 0.278), so both are right and the drop is 9-12 points whichever is used. (2) F-002's stratification survives the collapse and gets stronger for size: TF-level miss by regulon-size tercile 0.83 (n=53, median 11 targets) / 0.58 (n=50, 52.5) / 0.50 (n=52, 151.5) against 0.68/0.49/0.47 at experiment level, Spearman(median fraction, size) = -0.20; by max-Jaccard tercile 0.673 / 0.704 / 0.531, i.e. non-monotonic with the HIGH-overlap tercile missing LEAST, Spearman = -0.083. V-002's flip condition ("a TF-level analysis showing miss rate rising with max Jaccard") is not met. Size x overlap is noisy at 6-25 TFs per cell and shows no overlap gradient inside a size tercile (low size 0.76/0.91/0.83, mid 0.50/0.75/0.50, high 0.73/0.38/0.48). (3) After self-edge removal, self-edge TFs matched to no-self-edge TFs on target count are NOT better: nearest-neighbour matching on log10 target count (with replacement, 28 distinct self-edge TFs used, median size gap 1.03x, no pair beyond 1.29x) gives matched self-edge 0.169 vs no-self-edge 0.186, difference -0.017 with a paired bootstrap 95% CI of [-0.153, +0.119] over the 59 pairs (seed 0, 10000 draws). The unmatched gap is 0.312 vs 0.186, so matching on size removes it, as V-010's experiment-level reweighting predicted (0.23-0.28 vs 0.244). (4) The 12 TFs with a -1 self-edge are MYC 887, HIF1A 463, FOS 344, CTNNB1 341, YY1 266, WT1 156, ATF3 110, BCL6 101, CEBPD 97, MSX1 27, NR2E1 17, ZHX2 11 targets. In size bins <=60 / 61-150 / 151-400 / >400 they are 3 / 3 / 4 / 2 TFs against 30 / 35 / 12 / 7 with a +1 self-edge, so a size-matched -1 vs +1 comparison is UNANSWERABLE IN KNOCKTF; the counts are reported and no number is forced. Two side notes: at TF level the sign/size confound F-011 reports is much weaker than at experiment level (median 133 targets for -1 vs 78 for +1, against 330 vs 93 per experiment, because HIF1A alone supplies 7 of the 21 -1 experiments), and 11 of the 12 -1 TFs improve or tie when the self-edge is removed (MYC ties at fraction 0.001), consistent with V-011's 11/12.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python tflevel.py` (about 1 min; imports selfedge.py, which reruns propagate.py, controls.py and leak.py, and gapcheck.py, so F-001/F-002/F-004/F-006/F-010's tables print above the new blocks). Rank fractions, conditions and the ULM kernel are selfedge.py's unchanged; regulon size and max pairwise Jaccard are gapcheck.py's unchanged; this script only collapses by TF, stratifies, matches and bootstraps. Predictions were stated in the docstring first: (1) 0.40 -> 0.28 (observed 0.361 -> 0.265 under the median-fraction rule, 0.398 -> 0.277 under V-010's rule, so the predicted numbers were the other rule's); (2) overlap terciles flat or falling and the size effect persisting (both observed); (3) matched difference within +/-0.07 of zero (observed -0.017, though the CI is +/-0.14, twice as wide as V-010's +/-0.07 estimate). No gain over ULM is claimed, so no shuffled network; masked and self-edge-removed are both in the table and are identical at top-10% (41/155), as F-010 predicts for univariate ULM. The assert checks the 155-TF collapse, that selfedge.py's ULM fractions equal gapcheck.py's to 1e-12 on all 279 experiments, that self-edge removal leaves every no-self-edge TF untouched, and that the point estimate lies inside its own bootstrap CI.
Source: analysis/tflevel.py
Confidence: high for (1) and (2) — deterministic, two independent collapse rules agree on the direction and the experiment-level tables are reproduced in the same run. Medium for (3): the point estimate is stable but the CI spans 27 points, the bootstrap resamples the 59 matched pairs (so it carries the no-self-edge sampling and the match, not the self-edge pool's own sampling), 28 of 96 self-edge TFs carry all the matched weight, and matching on target count leaves curation depth and TF family uncontrolled. High for (4) as a statement about counts; the per-bin hit rates printed for the -1 group (0.67 / 0.33 / 0.00 / 1.00) are 2-4 TFs each and must not be cited as rates. TF-level medians hide within-TF spread: 103 of 155 TFs have a single experiment, so for two thirds of the rows "median" is one number and the collapse mainly reweights the 52 repeated TFs (e.g. STAT3's 10 experiments) down to one vote each.
Depends on: F-002, F-010, F-011

## F-015 · 2026-09-18 · method-designer (entered by main session) · status: proposed
Claim: Method proposal, EB-slope. Per TF t, fit the slope b_t of self-edge-dropped ULM score on the TF's own logFC across knockTF experiments, shrink it toward a regulon-size-tercile mean by closed-form empirical Bayes, and report the residual r = score - shrunken slope x own logFC with an interval as the post-translational component. The slope MUST be fit with the self-edge t->t deleted: with it in place, own logFC is a term inside the TF's own ULM score (F-010), so the regression would recover the self-edge weight, most strongly in small regulons (V-010). With it deleted, score and own logFC share no algebraic channel (ULM is univariate), so b_t is the regulon's downstream response per unit knockdown depth. Maths: a_te = ULM t for TF t, experiment e, edge t->t deleted; m_te = logFC of gene t; through the origin, b_t = sum_e m a / sum_e m^2, s_t^2 = sigma_g^2 / sum_e m^2, sigma_g^2 = pooled residual variance within size tercile g; prior b_t ~ N(mu_g, tau_g^2); mu_g = sum w b / sum w with w = 1/s_t^2; Q = sum w (b_t - mu_g)^2; tau_g^2 = max(0, (Q - (T_g - 1)) / (sum w - sum w^2 / sum w)) (DerSimonian-Laird); lambda_t = tau_g^2 / (tau_g^2 + s_t^2); btilde_t = lambda_t b_t + (1 - lambda_t) mu_g; v_t = lambda_t s_t^2; r_te = a_te - btilde_t m_te; Var(r_te) = sigma_g^2 + m_te^2 v_t; 90% interval r +/- 1.645 sqrt(Var). Reductions by substitution: tau^2 = 0 and mu = 0 gives lambda = 0, btilde = 0, r = a_te, i.e. plain self-edge-dropped ULM (plain ULM with the self-edge is a network switch, not a parameter); tau^2 -> inf gives lambda -> 1, btilde -> b_t, per-TF OLS through the origin; tau^2 -> 0 gives btilde -> mu_g, one pooled slope. Pooling groups: regulon-size tercile (a ULM t-stat scales roughly with sqrt(n_targets), so slopes are not exchangeable across sizes) and a single global group as comparator. NOT self-edge sign (the edge is deleted so the label has no causal path into a_te, and +1 includes sign-unknown edges, V-011), NOT knockTF dataset batch (that is where the confound lives), NOT TF family or cell line (too many levels at under 2 experiments per TF).
Evidence: none yet; this is a proposal. Design: leave-one-experiment-out over the 176 experiments of the 52 TFs with >= 2 experiments (26 TFs with exactly 2 = 52 experiments, 26 with >= 3 = 124; the other 103 TFs are singletons and still inform mu_g and tau_g^2; counts checked by the main session with value_counts on analysis/gapcheck.csv: 279 / 155 / 103 / 26 / 26 / 176 / 52 / 124). Numeric predictions stated before any run: (1) global pooled slope mu = 1.2 t-units per unit logFC, 90% CI within [0.6, 2.0]; by size tercile about 0.8 / 1.2 / 2.2. (2) between-TF slope sd tau = 1.0, median lambda = 0.55 (range 0.4-0.7). (3) LOO coverage of the 90% interval: partial pooling 0.87 (pass band 0.82-0.94, i.e. 144-165 of 176), fully pooled 0.85, per-TF OLS 0.90 with intervals 1.4x wider; coverage is declared in advance NOT to be the discriminator. (4) LOO RMSE ratio partial pooling / per-TF OLS = 0.80 (band 0.70-0.90); 0.72 on the 52 two-experiment folds, 0.90 on the 124 others. (5) RMSE ratio partial / fully pooled = 0.95 (band 0.90-1.00); this decides whether the hierarchy earns its place. (6) RMSE ratio partial / null predictor a = 0 is 0.85. (7) Shuffled-network control on the slope (controls.py's fixed shuffler, 10 seeds): mu_shuffled = 0.0 +/- 0.2 and tau^2 truncated to 0 in >= 8 of 10 seeds. (8) Placebo regressor (own logFC replaced by a gene matched on |logFC| and regulon count): mu = 0.0 +/- 0.2, RMSE ratio vs a = 0 of 1.00 +/- 0.03. (9) TGF-beta hepatic stellate set, dc.ds.hsctgfb(), 3 vs 3: SMAD3 ULM t in 4-6, |m_SMAD3| < 0.5, abundance component under 0.7 t-units (under 15% of the score), 90% residual interval (Var = 1 + m^2 v_t off knockTF) excludes 0 for SMAD2 and SMAD3; specificity arm: at least one TF in that contrast's ULM top 10 has |btilde m| > 1 and leaves the top 10 in the residual channel, otherwise (9) has failed even if SMAD2/3 pass. Kill conditions: K1 tau_g^2 = 0 in every grouping AND (5) >= 0.99: one pooled slope suffices, hierarchy unnecessary (the one-parameter "subtract mu x own logFC" survives and is the result to write down). K2 mu_shuffled >= 0.5 mu_real or placebo RMSE ratio < 0.95: slope is a global knockdown-depth artefact, dead. K3 (6) >= 0.98: activity unrelated to knockdown depth, dead. K4 more than 30% of the 155 b_t negative: linear dose-response premise wrong. Controls note: the masked control is degenerate here because the input is already the masked score (F-010: self-edge deletion = masking for ULM, 96/279 both), so (7) and (8) carry the claim; under rank-among-TFs on knockTF this method must look WORSE than ULM by design (residual of a pure knockdown should be near 0), so nobody should report a top-10% number for it. Errors-in-variables: attenuation factor sum m^2 / (sum m^2 + n sigma_u^2) = 2.89 / (2.89 + 0.09) = 0.97 at m = -1.7 and logFC SE 0.3, about 3%, far below the slope's posterior spread, so no correction. Knockdown efficiency and time since knockdown change the true slope, inflate sigma_g^2 and are not fixable within knockTF (no dose or timepoint field); a dose-resolved set (X-Atlas/Orion, refs/README.md) is the upgrade path.
Source: method-designer proposal for tasks.md T-021, 2026-09-18; builds on analysis/selfedge.py (`es_ns` is a_te already), controls.py (shuffler), randmask.py (matched picker).
Confidence: medium that the machinery is right (closed form, three reductions shown by substitution). Low that it will pass: every knockTF experiment has own logFC < 0 with median -1.74, so within a TF the slope is identified by the level of m, not by variation in it, and linearity is barely tested; the method is applied near m = 0 where it has no data, so on the TGF-beta set the residual is nearly the raw ULM score, which is why (9) ships with a specificity arm. DerSimonian-Laird tau^2 is biased low with few TFs per group, so K1 needs its RMSE half as well. Cost estimate 2-3 hours, numpy only, one ~2 MB download, no new dependency.
Depends on: F-005, F-010, F-011

## F-016 · 2026-09-18 · lit-scout + main session · status: proposed
Claim: About half of CollecTRI's edges carry a sign that is a default, not evidence, and the fetched network says which. Müller-Dott 2023 assigns sign in three tiers: PMID prevalence for that edge, else the majority sign of the TF's other PMID-signed edges ("regulon"), else default activating; a TF-role prior (GO, UniProt keywords, KRAB, Soto et al.) was tested and dropped because it lowered benchmark performance, and default-activating was chosen over default-repressing because it benchmarked better, not on biological grounds. The paper gives 19541 PMID / 1904 regulon / 21730 default of 43175 edges. data/collectri.parquet as fetched by decoupler has a `sign_decision` column (values "PMID", "regulon", "default activation") alongside source, target, weight, resources, references: 19396 PMID (13721 at +1, 5675 at -1), 1895 regulon (1748 / 147), 21699 default activation (all +1) of 42990. Every -1 is evidence-based; 58% of +1 edges (21699 of 37168) are defaults. Self-edges are worse: of 461, 343 are default activation, 98 PMID (68 at +1, 30 at -1), 20 regulon (17 / 3), so 80% of the 428 "+1" self-edges have no sign evidence. For the 96 knockTF TFs with a self-edge: 50 default (+1), 29 PMID +1, 5 regulon +1, 11 PMID -1, 1 regulon -1; by experiment 92 default, 74 PMID +1, 10 regulon +1, 20 PMID -1, 1 regulon -1, 82 no self-edge (sums to 279). The paper never mentions autoregulatory or self edges, and the saezlab/CollecTRI construction scripts have no step that filters or flags source == target. This corrects V-011, which said per-edge sign evidence is "not in data/collectri.parquet as fetched by decoupler", and the lit-scout's conclusion for T-023 that the split is impossible from a public file; both missed the column.
Evidence: refs/muller-dott-2023-collectri.md, Materials and methods, "Assigning the TF-gene mode of regulation": "we assigned the mode of regulation for each TF-gene interaction primarily based on the number of literature references and secondarily based on other TF-gene interactions in the regulon. TF-gene interactions without any information were assigned an activating mode of regulation."; Results, "Construction of TF regulons from CollecTRI": "For 19541 TF-gene interactions the mode of regulation was determined based on the prevalence of PubMed references. Meanwhile, for 1904 interactions, the mode of regulation was assigned by the TF classification based on other interactions in the regulon. For the remaining 21730 TF-gene interaction an activating mode was assigned by default"; "a default activating mode of regulation outperformed a default repressing mode of regulation". Counts: pd.crosstab of sign_decision x weight on data/collectri.parquet, on its source == target rows, and on those rows joined to analysis/gapcheck.csv's tf column (main session, one-off, no script yet; T-028 commits it). Script provenance of the column: saezlab/CollecTRI scripts/CollecTRI/02_mode_of_regulation.R sets sign.decision, with "default activation" for edges left NA (lit-scout, fetched from GitHub).
Source: refs/muller-dott-2023-collectri.md; data/collectri.parquet; lit-scout report for tasks.md T-023.
Confidence: high for the counts (read straight off the file; they sum to 42990, 461, 96 and 279 and match V-011's 428 / 33 and 176 / 21) and for the quotes. Medium for "regulon" meaning exactly the paper's second tier (inferred from the script and the near-match 1895 vs 1904). The raw-net self-edge counts are before tmin=5 pruning; F-010/F-011's 197 / 82 split is after pruning and agrees (176 + 21 = 197).
Depends on: F-011

## V-014 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: split — every number reproduces; (1) and (4) confirmed; (2) confirmed for "F-002 survives the TF collapse" and "V-002's flip condition is not met", REFUTED for "gets stronger for size" (tercile cut points moved; at the experiment-level cut points the size gradient is the same 0.20); (3) the numbers reproduce but "matching on size removes the gap" is NOT established: the -0.017 has the sign it has because np.argmin breaks ties alphabetically, and a bootstrap that resamples both TF pools and re-matches gives a CI of about [-0.17, +0.23], which contains both 0 and the unmatched 0.126
Claim checked: F-014 — collapsing knockTF to one row per TF (median rank fraction, hit = median <= 0.10) gives ULM 56/155 -> 41/155 with the self-edge removed or the gene masked; the size effect of F-002 survives and is said to get stronger while the overlap terciles do not rise; self-edge TFs matched on target count to no-self-edge TFs are said to be no better once the self-edge is gone (0.169 vs 0.186), so size explains the residual gap; and the 12 TFs with a -1 self-edge are too few per size bin (3/3/4/2) for a size-matched sign comparison.
How verified: reran `cd analysis && uv run python tflevel.py` (exit 0, assert passes). Printed: ulm 0.361 56/155 top5 0.258; no self-edge 0.265 41/155 0.187 better 0.07 worse 0.52; masked 0.265 41/155 0.194; self-edge TFs 45/96 0.469 -> 30/96 0.312; no-self-edge TFs 11/59 0.186 (masked top-5% 0.119); other rule 0.398 / 0.277 / 0.278; size terciles n=53/50/52 miss 0.83 / 0.58 / 0.50, medians 11 / 52.5 / 151.5; max_jac terciles 0.673 / 0.704 / 0.531; size x overlap rows 0.76 0.91 0.83 | 0.50 0.75 0.50 | 0.73 0.38 0.48, cells 6-25 TFs; Spearman -0.083 and -0.203; matched 0.169 vs 0.186, -0.017, CI [-0.153, +0.119], 28 distinct of 96, median gap x1.03, max x1.29; -1 TFs and target counts as listed, bins 3/3/4/2 vs 30/35/12/7, medians 133 / 78 / 22. Every number in F-014 matches. Audit in scratch scripts (session scratchpad v014_dump.py, v014_audit.py, v014_extra.py, v014_x2.py; not committed, nothing under .venv touched), working from tflevel.py's own `tl` and `df` frames.
(a) Collapse. Experiments per TF: 103 with one, 26 with two, 26 with three to ten. V-010's TF-level numbers were the mean over TFs of each TF's hit rate (audit2_f010.py line 34, `hit=("hit","mean")`); the experimentalist's reading is right, and that rule reproduces all of V-010's TF-level numbers here (0.398 -> 0.277, self-edge TFs 0.524 -> 0.328, others 0.194). Of the 26 two-experiment TFs, 11 straddle the 0.10 line under full-network ULM (CTCF, HMGA1, ID1, KLF7, PAX3, SALL4, SOX17, SOX4, SPDEF, TEAD4, XBP1) and the mean-of-two convention calls ALL 11 a miss; only PAX3 (0.077 / 0.129 -> 0.103) and XBP1 (0.109) are near the line, the rest have one experiment far out, so for n=2 the rule is in effect "both must hit". With the self-edge removed 5 straddle (1 hit, 4 miss), masked 4 (0 hit). 29 TFs in all have mixed hit/miss experiments under ULM. If straddling ties go the other way (lower median): ulm 70/155 = 0.452, no self-edge 46/155 = 0.297, masked 46/155; self-edge TFs 58/96 -> 34/96, no-self-edge 12/59. Upper median: 56 -> 40 -> 40. Mean fraction <= 0.10: 53 -> 35. The 103 single-experiment TFs alone, where no convention applies: 38 -> 25 (0.369 -> 0.243). So the LEVEL 0.361 is the floor of the reasonable conventions (0.361 to 0.452) and the DROP is 15 to 24 TFs, 9.7 to 15.5 points, not "9-12 points whichever is used". Direction and rough size of the collapse are not in doubt; self-edge removal and masking agree under every rule.
(b) F-002 at TF level. Experiment-level size cut points are 48.3 / 110 targets, TF-level ones 27 / 90.7, so F-014's "low" tercile is a different, smaller set of regulons (median 11 targets vs 16). TFs binned at the EXPERIMENT-level cut points: miss 0.736 (n=72) / 0.565 (n=46) / 0.541 (n=37), low minus high 0.195, against 0.677 / 0.495 / 0.473 for experiments at the same cuts, low minus high 0.204. Same gradient; every bin is 5-7 points higher at TF level because repeated TFs are the better-recovered ones. Spearman(fraction, size) is -0.203 at TF level and was -0.20 at experiment level (V-002). "Gets stronger for size" is a cut-point artefact; "persists unchanged" is what the data show (TF-level own-tercile low minus high +0.330, TF bootstrap CI [+0.162, +0.489], Spearman p = 0.011). Overlap at the experiment-level cut points (0.125 / 0.197): 0.694 (n=62) / 0.700 (n=50) / 0.488 (n=43), no rise; Spearman -0.083 (p = 0.30); partial Spearman given size -0.016 (ulm), -0.038 (self-edge removed); TF-level low minus high by max Jaccard +0.142, CI [-0.041, +0.319]. V-002's flip condition is not met on either the TF-level or the size-adjusted arm. As at experiment level, "overlap does not hurt" is supported and "high overlap helps" is not.
(c) Matching. Concentration: 28 self-edge TFs carry the 59 pairs, reuse counts {1: 17, 2: 4, 3: 4, 6: 1, 7: 1, 9: 1}; BCLAF1 (9 targets) is used 9 times, KLF7 (11) 7 times, TOX (5 targets, scored on 4) 6 times, all three misses, so 3 TFs carry 22 of 59 pairs (37%); Kish effective n of the self-edge side 14.8. Tie-breaking: target counts are integers, 25 of the 59 no-self-edge TFs have two or three equally near self-edge TFs, and np.argmin takes the first in alphabetical TF order. Three self-edge TFs have exactly 9 targets, BCLAF1 (miss), NFE2L1 (hit), SMARCC1 (miss), and the 9 no-self-edge TFs with 7-9 targets all go to BCLAF1 because B sorts first. Remove BCLAF1 and they all go to NFE2L1: the script's estimate moves from -0.017 to +0.136, above the unmatched gap. Leave-one-self-edge-TF-out range: argmin [-0.068, +0.136]; random tie-breaking (2000 draws) range [-0.068, +0.102], 61% positive. Averaging over tied nearest neighbours, which is the estimator with no arbitrary choice in it: matched self-edge 0.203 vs 0.186, difference +0.017 (leave-one-out range [-0.034, +0.068]). Other estimators: k-NN k=3 +0.023, k=5 -0.014, k=10 +0.046; with replacement and a caliper of x1.05 / x1.1 / x1.25: +0.023 (36 pairs) / -0.015 (44) / -0.012 (54); 1:1 without replacement (optimal assignment, and greedy in 2000 random orders, same answer) +0.034 but it is not a size match any more, 34% of pairs differ by more than 2x, max x13.7, because only 33 self-edge TFs have <= 60 targets against 50 no-self-edge TFs; without replacement inside a caliper x1.25 / x1.5 / x2: +0.000 (40 pairs) / +0.022 (45) / +0.042 (48); size-bin reweighting (6 bins) 0.225 vs 0.186, +0.039; regulons <= 60 targets 7/33 = 0.212 vs 7/50 = 0.140. So the SIGN is not stable (F-014's Confidence says "the point estimate is stable"; it is not, 9 of 11 estimators are >= 0), the MAGNITUDE is: every estimator is within -0.02 to +0.05, and all are below the unmatched 0.126. Bootstrap redone (numpy, seed 0, 5000 draws, self-edge TFs and no-self-edge TFs resampled independently, re-matched inside each draw): script's argmin matcher CI [-0.186, +0.305] (sd 0.127), tie-averaged [-0.169, +0.233] (sd 0.103), k=5 [-0.146, +0.203]. The entry's pairs-only CI is 0.27 wide, the proper one 0.40-0.49. P(draw >= 0.126) = 0.14-0.19. The unmatched gap itself, same draws: +0.125, CI [-0.011, +0.260]. Unmatched minus matched gap on the same draws: +0.109, CI [-0.079, +0.271], P(<= 0) = 0.12.
(d) What (3) can and cannot say. On the entry's own CI the unmatched 0.126 sits 0.007 above the upper bound +0.119, less than half a TF in 59, so even as printed it excluded the unmatched gap by nothing. On the proper CI 0.126 is well inside. The data CANNOT distinguish "size matching removes the gap" from "size matching does nothing to the gap"; they also cannot exclude a matched deficit of 0.15. What they do support: the matched point estimate is about zero under every estimator tried (-0.02 to +0.05) and sits below the unmatched 0.126 every time, which is the direction V-010's experiment-level reweighting gave (0.23-0.28 vs 0.244). That is a consistent point estimate from 59 no-self-edge TFs with 11 hits, not a demonstration. At TF level the thing being explained is itself thin: 0.312 vs 0.186 has a CI that reaches -0.011. My own "+/-0.07" in V-010's flip condition was too optimistic by a factor of about three; the matched estimate carries roughly +/-0.2.
(e) Assert. Clauses 2 and 3 are real: selfedge.py's ULM fractions equal gapcheck.py's on all 279 experiments, and self-edge removal leaves all 59 no-self-edge TFs untouched. `len(tl) == 155` is a row count. `len(idx) == len(ns_tf)` is a tautology (argmin over axis 1 of a 59 x 96 matrix has length 59). `lo <= d <= hi` cannot fail short of NaN: a percentile bootstrap of paired differences is centred on its own mean however the pairs are aligned, so it would pass with `idx` shuffled. Nothing in the assert touches the matching. One line that would: `se_tf.has_self.all() and gap.max() < np.log10(2)`.
(f) Small things. "11 of the 12 -1 TFs improve or tie" is 11 improve + 1 tie (MYC, identical fraction 0.0013) + 0 worse, i.e. 12 of 12 improve or tie; V-011's 11/12 was "better". The experiment-level medians "330 vs 93" are quoted from F-011 correctly; with gapcheck.py's size column, which this script uses, they are 341 vs 97. No gain over ULM is claimed, so no shuffled network is required; masked is in the table. Predictions were numeric and stated first; (1) missed under the script's own rule (0.40 -> 0.28 predicted, 0.361 -> 0.265 observed), and the entry says so.
Would flip if: a rerun printed different numbers. (1) would flip if some collapse rule made the self-edge loss vanish or made masked and self-edge-removed disagree (seven rules tried, none does). (2) the size reading would flip to "stronger at TF level" if at common cut points the TF-level low-minus-high gap clearly exceeded the experiment-level one (0.195 vs 0.204, it does not); the overlap reading would flip on the same evidence V-002 names, not seen here. (3) would become "confirmed" if a pre-specified, tie-free estimator (tie-averaged or k-NN) with a two-pool re-matching bootstrap gave a CI that excludes the unmatched gap, or equivalently if the CI of (unmatched gap minus matched gap) excluded 0; at [-0.079, +0.271] it does not, and with 59 no-self-edge TFs and 11 hits knockTF alone probably cannot get there. It would become "refuted" if such an estimator put the matched difference near or above 0.126; none of 11 does (max +0.046, or +0.136 only by deleting BCLAF1 under alphabetical tie-breaking).
Severity: medium — (1) is the TF-level form of the masked baseline and is safe to cite as "0.36 -> 0.27 (56 -> 41 of 155 TFs) under a convention that counts every split two-experiment TF as a miss; 0.45 -> 0.30 if splits count as hits; 0.37 -> 0.24 on the 103 single-experiment TFs". (3) matters more: it is the evidence that a self-edge TF without its self-edge is an ordinary TF of its size, which is what lets the project treat the self-edge as the only thing masking removes; cite it as "matched difference about 0 (-0.02 to +0.05 over 11 estimators), 95% CI roughly [-0.17, +0.23], cannot be distinguished from the unmatched 0.126", never as "matching on size removes the gap". Owed, not blocking: replace argmin with the tie-averaged matcher and the pairs-only bootstrap with the two-pool re-matching one in tflevel.py; replace the vacuous assert clause; in (2) print the TF-level table at the experiment-level cut points next to the TF terciles and drop "gets stronger"; state the n=2 convention in the docstring.


## V-015 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: unverifiable — the machinery is sound (all three reductions hold by substitution, DerSimonian-Laird is written correctly, the singleton residual is identically 0, the sign convention matches the code, dc.ds.hsctgfb exists) and the predictions are numbers, but the design is missing the one control that tests its premise: an intercept-only (constant knockdown offset, no own logFC) comparator, equivalently a permuted-own-logFC placebo. Every knockTF m is negative, so a through-origin "slope" is a rescaled mean of a, and the placebo (8) and kill condition K4 as written are decided by construction, not by biology. K4 as written is ALREADY MET on the raw data (50/155 = 32.3% of b_t negative). Do not implement T-027 as designed
Claim checked: F-015 — EB-slope proposal: regress self-edge-dropped ULM score a_te on the TF's own logFC m_te through the origin per TF, shrink the slope to a size-tercile mean with closed-form DL empirical Bayes, call r = a - btilde m the post-translational component; reduces to self-edge-dropped ULM / per-TF OLS / one pooled slope; nine numeric predictions, four kill conditions, shuffled-network and placebo-regressor controls in place of the masked control.
How verified: (A) Reductions by substitution. tau^2 = 0, mu = 0: lambda = 0, btilde = 0, v_t = 0, r = a_te, Var = sigma_g^2, interval a +/- 1.645 sigma_g; holds (mu = 0 is a pinned hyperparameter, prior N(0,0), since mu is otherwise estimated). tau^2 -> inf: lambda -> 1, btilde -> b_t, v_t -> s_t^2, Var = sigma_g^2 (1 + m^2 / sum m^2), the textbook through-origin OLS prediction variance; holds. tau^2 -> 0, mu != 0: btilde -> mu_g, v_t -> 0, Var -> sigma_g^2, which drops Var(mu_g) m^2 (global se(mu) 0.13, so 0.05 against sigma^2 7.9: negligible; S tercile se 0.12, M 0.30, still under 3% of sigma_g^2). DL: Q and the (sum w - sum w^2 / sum w) denominator are the standard DL moment estimator; correct. Two gaps: the shrink target mu_g is the FIXED-effect mean (w = 1/s^2), whereas DL's pooled estimate uses w* = 1/(s^2 + tau^2); on the real data these are 0.815 vs 0.672, a full se apart. And for a TF absent from knockTF (SMAD3, below) lambda s_t^2 is 0 x inf; it must be coded as tau^2 s^2 / (tau^2 + s^2) -> tau^2. Singleton: b = m a / m^2 = a/m, a - b m = 0 identically; a singleton adds one experiment and one TF, so dof = n_g - T_g handles it (global dof 124 = 279 - 155; S tercile has only 15 dof behind sigma_S). (B) Premise, selfedge.py lines 45-48: adjm_ns zeroes EVERY self-edge in the pruned network and es_ns scores all TFs on it, so es_ns.loc[e, tf] is a_te as claimed (one perturbed TF, TOX, falls to 4 targets, under tmin = 5, and is still scored). "No algebraic channel" is literally false: gene t stays in the regression as a non-target, entering the covariance through (0 - xbar)(y_t - ybar) and the t-stat through Syy. Measured in scratch with decoupler's own kernel (dc.mt.ulm.func, zero gene t, rescore column t, all 279): |change in a_te| median 0.0024 t, 75th pct 0.007, max 0.33 (NCOA1; HIF1A 0.30); as a slope da/dm median -0.0015, min -0.33, max +0.04. Against a slope of 0.8-1.2 that is about 0.2% at the median, and its sign is opposite to the predicted slope, so it is conservative. Negligible; the wording should be "no channel above 0.01 t in 75% of experiments", not "none". (C) Sign: type_p = -1 for all 388, m < 0 for all 279 (median -1.739, obs.logFC equals the matrix entry exactly), a_te < 0 in 199/279 (71%), so b = a/m > 0 is the code's convention for "knockdown lowers activity"; the method must use raw a_te, NOT type_p x a. A repressor regulon also gives b > 0 because CollecTRI weights are signed (REST: 71 of 75 targets -1, a = -14 to -20 in 5 experiments). So b_t < 0 means wrong sign labels or noise, not "repressor"; only 4 of 155 regulons have net-negative weight. (D) hsctgfb: decoupler 2.2.0 ds/_bulk.py line 9 defines hsctgfb(), GEO GSE151251 raw COUNTS, 6 samples. CollecTRI: SMAD2 108 targets, SMAD3 189, neither has a self-edge. SMAD3 is NOT among the 155 knockTF TFs (btilde = mu_g, v = tau_g^2, pure prior); SMAD2 has 3 experiments with raw b = -0.11. (E) PREVIEW, scratch only, not the experiment (raw through-origin slopes from es_ns-equivalent scores, no shrinkage, no LOO of hyperparameters, sigma not re-estimated per fold): b_t negative 50/155 = 32.3% (12/52 multi-experiment TFs, 38/103 singletons), median b 0.35. K4 (> 30%) fires. But K4 is miscalibrated: singleton s_t is about sigma/|m| = 2.8/1.7 = 1.6, so if the model were exactly TRUE with the fitted mu 0.815, tau 1.07, sigma 2.8, the expected negative fraction is 0.318 +/- 0.037; under the proposal's own predicted mu 1.2, tau 1.0 it is 0.239 +/- 0.034, so the 30% line sits 1.8 sd from the proposal's own expectation. Pooled mu 0.815 (formula as stated), RE mean 0.672 +/- 0.134 (90% CI about 0.45-0.89: prediction (1)'s point 1.2 is outside and the CI is not within [0.6, 2.0]); terciles S/M/L (n 4-25 / 27-87 / 88-1259) 0.14 / 0.97 / 1.09 against predicted 0.8 / 1.2 / 2.2. tau 1.07 global (predicted 1.0, good), terciles 0.44 / 1.60 / 0.86; median lambda 0.365 global, 0.20 / 0.50 / 0.33 by tercile (predicted 0.55, range 0.4-0.7). THE PREMISE: regression with an intercept across all 279 gives slope 0.41, intercept -0.90, corr(a, m) = 0.095; the within-TF slope (TF intercepts, 176 experiments) is 0.08 +/- 0.34. Permuting m across experiments (500 permutations, a regressor with the right distribution and no link to the experiment) gives mu = 0.752 (5-95%: 0.69-0.83) against the real 0.815, in-sample RMSE ratio vs a = 0 of 0.919 against the real 0.905; subtracting the constant mean(a) = -1.68 gives 0.904. LOO on the 176: RMSE null 4.50, global slope 3.98, global MEAN 3.96, per-TF slope 3.19, per-TF MEAN 2.74 (median |error| 1.62 / 1.66 / 1.73 / 1.65 / 1.39). The model with no own-logFC term predicts held-out knockdowns better than the slope model at both levels. Five REST experiments are 42% of the null's squared error; without them per-TF slope 3.02 vs global slope 2.99 (the pooled/OLS ratio moves from 1.25 to 0.99 on one TF), per-TF mean 2.74. Coverage preview: null 0.79, global slope 0.852, per-TF OLS 0.915; TF-cluster bootstrap sd 0.030-0.049 against iid binomial 0.021-0.031 (largest cluster STAT3 = 10, sum k^2 / sum k = 4.4). (F) Predictions classified. (1) number + band, but no formula for the CI of mu is given; terciles "about", no band. (2) number + band. (3) number + band for partial pooling; 0.85, 0.90 and "1.4x wider" have no tolerance; the band is about -1.2 / +1.7 cluster sd around 0.87, so it can fail, but all three estimators are predicted inside it, so it discriminates nothing (declared in advance, fair). (4) number + band. (5) number + band; what a value BELOW 0.90 means is not said (the preview, pooled/OLS 1.25, suggests about 0.8: a miss on the favourable side). (6) number, no band; K3 is its only threshold. (7) number + band + count. (8) number + band, ill-posed: if the matched gene is matched on SIGNED logFC then m' is about m and mu_placebo is about 0.75 with RMSE ratio about 0.92, so (8) fails and K2 (< 0.95) kills the method by construction; if matched on |logFC| with free sign, mu is about 0 by symmetry and (8) passes uninformatively. (9) band (SMAD3 t 4-6), bound (|m| < 0.5), a bound implied by the previous one (< 0.7 t), binary (interval excludes 0), binary existential (specificity arm). (9)'s main arm is a test of plain ULM: with |m| < 0.5, r is a to within 0.7 t, and whether the interval excludes 0 is decided by the unexplained switch from sigma_g^2 (about 7.9 on knockTF, half-width 4.6-4.8 t, straddling the predicted t of 4-6) to Var = 1 + m^2 v. The entry also leaves the DE step unspecified: the set is raw counts, pydeseq2 is not installed (a new dependency), and a ULM t scales with sqrt(number of genes), so "t-units per logFC" fitted on knockTF (36% of matrix entries zero-filled) is not portable to another gene universe without a stated normalisation. K1-K3 numeric; K4 numeric but already met and miscalibrated. (G) Goals and Conventions: interpretable, yes (weighted mean of target statistics minus one scalar times own logFC). Replacing the masked control is legitimate, not a dodge: (B) shows masking moves a_te by a median 0.002 t, so the masked arm would be a no-op, and no rank gain is claimed. But the substitutes do not reach the premise: (7) tests only that mean(a) < 0 is regulon-specific (F-001/F-006 already say so), and (8) is decided by how the placebo's sign is matched. With a shuffled network the chance self-edges must be deleted again after shuffling.
Would flip if: to confirmed (sound and testable): the design adds (i) the intercept-only comparator, per-TF mean shrunk to a group mean by the same DL machinery, with a stated numeric prediction and a kill condition such as "LOO RMSE slope / intercept-only >= 1.00 on the 176 means no dose-response is identified and the abundance component cannot be written as btilde x m"; (ii) a permuted-own-logFC placebo (m permuted across experiments within tercile) with the prediction stated as a DIFFERENCE from the real fit, since the placebo's mu is about 0.75 and not 0; (iii) K4 restated against the model-implied negative fraction rather than a flat 30%; (iv) a justification for sigma^2 = 1 on the TGF-beta set, or sigma_g^2 used there too. To refuted: the implemented experiment reproduces the preview, i.e. the intercept-only model beats the slope model out of sample with shrinkage and per-fold hyperparameters in place. The preview's direction would be overturned by a within-TF slope clearly above 0 (now 0.08 +/- 0.34) in any dose-resolved set.
Severity: high — T-027 (2-3 hours of implementation) and README's "Where this points" both rest on "a slope calibrated on knockdown data predicts the abundance-driven activity". In knockTF that slope is not distinguishable from a constant offset, and applied at m near 0 the two models give different abundance components (btilde x m -> 0 versus a constant that has no meaning outside a knockdown).
