## V-016 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — every count reproduces, the column is upstream's (not local), all three quotes are in the paper, and the 185-edge gap to the paper is fully explained (decoupler's `dropna()` on `references`). V-011 was wrong on the point F-016 names, and on one more: V-011 called the -1 self-edge group "curated", but ZHX2's -1 is a "regulon" inference. Three wordings to weaken when citing: "every -1 is evidence-based" (147 of 5822 are TF-level regulon inference, not per-edge evidence), "not on biological grounds" (the Methods give a base-rate reason as well as the benchmark), and the tier-2 rule as implemented is stricter than the paper's sentence, so "default activation" also covers 3209 edges of 197 TFs whose PMID-signed edges are majority REPRESSING
Claim checked: F-016 — CollecTRI signs come in three tiers (per-edge PMID prevalence, else a TF-level "regulon" majority, else default +1); the file decoupler fetches records the tier per edge in `sign_decision`; about half of all edges, 58% of +1 edges and 80% of +1 self-edges are default fills; for knockTF's 96 self-edge TFs the split is 50 default / 29 PMID +1 / 5 regulon +1 / 11 PMID -1 / 1 regulon -1; the paper tested and dropped a TF-role prior, chose default-activating on benchmark performance, and does not discuss self-edges; V-011's statement that this evidence is not in data/collectri.parquet was wrong.
How verified: (1) Counts (scratch v016.py / v016b.py in the session scratchpad, not committed; nothing under .venv touched). data/collectri.parquet columns: source, target, weight, resources, references, sign_decision; 42990 rows, 0 duplicate (source, target), 0 NaN sign_decision. crosstab sign_decision x weight (-1 / +1 / all): PMID 5675 / 13721 / 19396; regulon 147 / 1748 / 1895; default activation 0 / 21699 / 21699; all 5822 / 37168 / 42990. source == target rows: PMID 30 / 68 / 98; regulon 3 / 17 / 20; default 0 / 343 / 343; all 33 / 428 / 461. 343/428 = 80.1%, 21699/37168 = 58.4%. 155 knockTF TFs in analysis/gapcheck.csv, 96 with a self-edge: default +1 50, PMID +1 29, regulon +1 5, PMID -1 11, regulon -1 1. By experiment: 92 / 74 / 10 / 20 / 1, none 82, sum 279. Every number in F-016 matches. Experiment by experiment: imported selfedge.py (exit 0) and compared its post-pruning `has_self` and selfsign.py's `w_self` with the raw-net lookup over its 279 (exp, tf) pairs: membership equal on 279 of 279 (197 / 82, 0 mismatches), sign equal on 279 of 279, and gapcheck.csv's exp/tf rows are the same 279 in the same order. crosstab of sign_decision x pruned self-edge weight reproduces 20 / 74, 92, 1 / 10, 82. The one regulon -1 is ZHX2 (11 targets); the other 11 -1 TFs are all PMID. (2) Provenance. decoupler 2.2.0 op/_collectri.py downloads https://zenodo.org/records/8192729/files/CollecTRI_regulons.csv, rewrites `resources` and `references`, then `ct.dropna()`, optional complex removal (default off), `_infer_dtypes`, `drop_duplicates(["source","target"])`. It selects no columns, so whatever Zenodo ships is kept. Fetched the Zenodo CSV with curl: header is `source,target,weight,resources,references,sign_decision`; 43175 rows; sign_decision x weight = PMID 5694 / 13847 / 19541, regulon 154 / 1750 / 1904, default 0 / 21730 / 21730, i.e. EXACTLY the paper's 19541 / 1904 / 21730 of 43175 and its Methods' "13 847 and 5694" and "1750 and 154". Applying dropna + drop_duplicates to it gives 42990 rows that outer-merge with the local parquet 42990 both / 0 left-only / 0 right-only, weight and sign_decision equal on every row. So the column is upstream's and the local file is an unmodified decoupler fetch. The gap is explained, not a version difference and not complexes (AP1 and NFKB are still in the file, 1395 edges): 185 upstream rows have NaN `references` (184 TFactS-only, 1 SIGNOR-only; 145 PMID, 9 regulon, 31 default; 159 at +1, 26 at -1) and decoupler's `dropna()` removes them: 19541 - 145 = 19396, 1904 - 9 = 1895, 21730 - 31 = 21699. One of the 185 is a self-edge: HOXA5 -> HOXA5, +1, default activation. HOXA5 is in the scored knockTF set (1 experiment), so upstream CollecTRI has 462 self-edges and that experiment sits in the local "82 no self-edge" group only because of decoupler's dropna; it changes no number here (every script reads the parquet) and being a default fill it would join the 92. F-016's "Medium for 'regulon' meaning exactly the paper's second tier" can be raised to high: the upstream counts are identical to the paper's. (3) Quotes, refs/muller-dott-2023-collectri.md. Methods, "Assigning the TF–gene mode of regulation" (line 43): "in the published CollecTRI-derived regulons we assigned the mode of regulation for each TF–gene interaction primarily based on the number of literature references and secondarily based on other TF–gene interactions in the regulon. TF–gene interactions without any information were assigned an activating mode of regulation." Present (the file has en dashes where F-016 types hyphens). Results, "Construction of TF regulons from CollecTRI" (line 125): "For 19541 TF–gene interactions the mode of regulation was determined based on the prevalence of PubMed references. Meanwhile, for 1904 interactions, the mode of regulation was assigned by the TF classification based on other interactions in the regulon. For the remaining 21730 TF–gene interaction an activating mode was assigned by default" and "a default activating mode of regulation outperformed a default repressing mode of regulation (adjusted P-value < 2.2 × 10−16, t-value equal to 337 and 294 for AUROC and AUPRC, respectively)". Present, same section as cited. TF-role prior tested and dropped: Methods line 39 lists GO terms, UniProt keywords, KRAB and Soto et al.; Results: "assigning a general mode of regulation for TFs based on prior knowledge led to a decrease in performance (adjusted P-value equal to 6.5 × 10−5 and 1.5 × 10−10 ...)", and the final recipe omits it. Confirmed. Self-edges: grep for autoreg / self / itself / own gene|promoter|expression / feedback / loop finds ONE hit, Introduction line 13, "can generally alter the core autoregulatory circuitry of a cell (1–3)", a sentence about cancer, not about CollecTRI's edges. So "never mentions autoregulatory or self edges" holds for edges but the word does occur once; cite as "does not discuss self-edges". (4) GitHub. curl of raw.githubusercontent.com/saezlab/CollecTRI/main/scripts/CollecTRI/02_mode_of_regulation.R (203 lines): line 43 `mutate(sign.decision = case_when(sign != 0 ~ "PMID"))`, lines 90-94 / 115-118 set "regulon", line 146 `network.signed$sign.decision[is.na(network.signed$sign.decision)] <- "default activation"` under `if (unknown.to.pos)`; the published network is the call with use.PMIDs = T, use.classification = F, save.decision = T. Confirmed. grep of 01_resource_aggregation.R, 02, 04.3_weight_CollecTRI.R for source/target equality, self, autoreg, loop: nothing, so "no step that filters or flags source == target" holds. One thing F-016 does not say: 03_pypath_collecTRI.py line 15 is `network.Network(netres.collectri, allow_loops = True)`, so the OmniPath build keeps self-loops on purpose; they are not an oversight.
Wording. (a) "Every -1 is evidence-based": 5675 of 5822 (97.5%) are per-edge PMID; 147 are "regulon", a TF-level inference with no evidence on that edge. For self-edges 30 of 33 are PMID, 3 regulon; in knockTF 11 of 12 TFs / 20 of 21 experiments are PMID and ZHX2 is regulon. Cite as "every -1 rests on PMID evidence for that edge or for the majority of that TF's regulon; none is a default". (b) "chosen ... because it benchmarked better, not on biological grounds": the benchmark half is confirmed (quote above), but Methods line 39 also gives a base-rate reason: "Since both approaches resulted in a majority of interactions being assigned a positive mode of regulation (PMIDs: 71%, TF classification: 76%), we also assigned an activating mode of regulation in cases where no information was available". The paper says something weaker than "not on biological grounds"; cite as "chosen on the base rate of activation among signed edges and because it benchmarked better on a knockTF-style perturbation benchmark". Not claimed by anyone yet and not tested here: that benchmark is unmasked, so by F-010/F-011 a default +1 on 343 self-edges is itself rewarded by it; how much of t = 337 is that is unknown. (c) Tier 2. F-016's "majority sign of the TF's other PMID-signed edges" is a fair paraphrase of the paper ("classified the TF based on whether the majority of these interactions were linked to activation or repression"), but the script divides by ALL of the TF's edges, unknowns included (`perc_repressor = sum(sign == -1)/nrow(TF.interactions)`, applied where `> 0.5`). Measured on the upstream file: the 144 TFs with regulon edges all have a PMID-signed share >= 0.50 of their whole regulon in one direction (minimum exactly 0.5, NFYA 47 of 94) and none has a default edge; the 887 TFs with default edges all have both shares <= 0.5. So "default activation" is not "no information about this TF": 425 of those TFs (16717 default edges) have a PMID majority activating, 197 TFs (3209 edges) a PMID majority REPRESSING, 265 (1804 edges) a tie or no PMID sign. For knockTF's 50 default-self-edge TFs: 36 PMID-majority activating, 10 majority repressing (ID1 9/13, NR2F2 11/19, TWIST2 12/15, NCOR1 1/15, YBX1 20/21, HINFP 0/1, TFAP4 4/6, FOXP1 4/7, NANOG 15/17, SOX17 5/12 as act/rep), 4 tie or none (TOX, RFX5, KLF2, KLF7). F-016's numbers are unaffected; its reading "no sign evidence" is right per edge and should not be stretched to per TF.
Correction to V-011 (that entry is not edited; this is where the correction lives). Wrong: Severity item (3), "needs CollecTRI's per-edge PMID/sign evidence (not in data/collectri.parquet as fetched by decoupler)". The file has had a `sign_decision` column all along; V-011 read `weight`, checked duplicates and self-edges, and never listed the columns. My error, asserted without looking. Item (3) is no longer owed: the count it asked for is 29 PMID / 5 regulon / 50 default of the 84 "+1" self-edge TFs (74 / 10 / 92 of 176 experiments). Also wrong: Wording (2), "the -1 group is curated, so 'negative autoregulator per CollecTRI' is fair": true for 11 of 12 TFs and 20 of 21 experiments; ZHX2's -1 self-edge is a regulon inference. Still right: Wording (2)'s main point, that a +1 self-edge means "activating OR no sign information", now quantified (50 of 84 TFs, 92 of 176 experiments are fills) and its instruction to write "+1 self-edge", not "positive autoregulator". V-011's verdict on F-011 is NOT affected: every F-011 number, the 176/176 and 21/21 sign argument, and the 176 / 21 / 82 split are functions of `weight` only, and step (1) above re-derives 176 / 21 / 82 cell by cell from the same file. The lit-scout's T-023 conclusion ("not possible from any public file") is wrong for the same reason and is doubly wrong: the column is in the public Zenodo CSV.
Would flip if: a recount of the parquet printed different numbers; the Zenodo CSV lacked `sign_decision` or disagreed with the parquet on any shared row (it agrees on 42990 of 42990), which would make the column a local addition; any of the three quotes were absent (all present); or the R script did not write "default activation" (line 146 does). "Does not discuss self-edges" would flip on a supplementary-material passage about self-regulation: refs/ holds the main text only, the supplement was not checked. The HOXA5 note would matter if any script switched from the parquet to the raw Zenodo file.
Severity: high — F-017/T-028 (splitting the 33 lost hits by sign source) and any README sentence about "positive autoregulators" rest on this column meaning what F-016 says, and it does. It also changes what the 0.45 -> 0.34 masking story can claim: for 92 of the 176 +1 experiments the rewarded self-edge sign is a fill. Owed, not blocking: (1) T-028's script should assert the three counts (19396 / 1895 / 21699) so a decoupler change to the dropna is caught; (2) when citing, use wordings (a)-(c) and say "per edge" for "no sign evidence"; (3) CLAUDE.md/README say the data is "as fetched by decoupler": worth one line that this is 185 edges fewer than published CollecTRI and drops HOXA5's self-edge; (4) untested: whether the 10 knockTF TFs with a default +1 self-edge but PMID-majority-repressing regulons behave like F-011's +1 group or its -1 group is NOT a test of the label (the mechanism follows the label, V-011 (c)), so the only open empirical question is the one T-028 asks; (5) the supplement of Müller-Dott 2023 has not been read for self-edge handling.

## F-012 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: The perturbed-TF mask control survives arms that are genuinely on the leak path, so F-007's conclusion holds on controls that are not no-ops. Zeroing, instead of the perturbed TF, the most negative-logFC gene among the 100 whose CollecTRI upstream-regulator set is most Jaccard-similar to the perturbed TF's own gene ("coreg": median masked logFC -1.601, median |logFC| 1.601, |logFC| >= 1 in 70% of picks, 0% exactly zero, mean 5.1 shared upstream regulators) leaves ULM at 0.437 top-10% / 0.362 top-5% and up/sum/alpha=0.5 propagation at 0.563 / 0.459, with the degree-preserving shuffled null at 0.292. Zeroing the gene whose logFC is NEAREST the perturbed TF's own among genes sitting in at least (the TF gene's regulon count minus 3) regulons ("logfc": median -1.464, |logFC| >= 1 in 89% of picks) leaves ULM at 0.444 / 0.358 and propagation at 0.566 / 0.473, shuffled 0.300. References in the same output: unmasked ULM 0.452 / propagated 0.566 / shuffled 0.307, and perturbed-TF-masked ULM 0.34 / propagated 0.28. So an equally large, equally negative, co-regulated logFC costs propagation 0.3 points where the perturbed TF's own value costs 29; the 57 -> 28 collapse is that one value and not "a big negative logFC on the regulon path". F-012 supersedes F-007's control arms (F-007 is not edited): its "any" arm zeroes a median |logFC| of 0.066 and hits an already-zero cell in 36% of picks, its "matched" arm 0.131 and 19%, against the perturbed TF's own median -1.68 (negative in 100% of experiments); and the matched arm's regulon gap is 2.69 on the 279 scored experiments, not the 2.05 randmask.py prints over all 388 (median regulons of the TF's gene 12 scored, 8 over all 388).
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python coregmask.py` (1 min 47 s; a copy of randmask.py with the two new arms, so F-007's file and printed table are untouched; imports propagate.py, controls.py and leak.py, so the unmasked, 10-draw shuffled and perturbed-TF-masked reference tables print above the new one). The any/matched arms reproduce F-007 exactly: any ulm 0.452 [0.452-0.452] top5 0.355, any up sum a=0.5 0.566 (better 0.594 worse 0.370), any SHUF 0.307; matched ulm 0.452 [0.444-0.455] top5 0.356, matched up sum a=0.5 0.566, matched SHUF 0.307. Predictions were stated in the docstring before the run, from V-007's scratch numbers: coreg 0.44 / 0.56 / 0.29 (observed 0.437 / 0.563 / 0.292), logfc 0.45 / 0.565 / 0.30 (observed 0.444 / 0.566 / 0.300). Both new picks are deterministic given the perturbed TF and the experiment, so the seed loop (3 seeds; 5 for the copied arms) only re-derives them and the [min-max] ranges are zero-width by construction. The assert covers every arm and seed, as T-012 asks: all 6208 picks differ from the perturbed TF, every picked cell is 0 in the matrix that was scored, and the perturbed TF's own cell is unchanged in all of them (so the arm is a control and not a second mask).
Source: analysis/coregmask.py
Confidence: high for the numbers (deterministic picks, decoupler's own ULM, same evaluate() as F-001/F-004/F-007). Medium-high for "and nothing else": these are the two strongest single-gene substitutes available in this matrix, but a single co-regulated gene cannot reproduce the algebra V-007 names -- in "up sum" propagation the perturbed TF's own value enters its propagated score once per upstream regulator with the same sign (about 21 terms), while a co-regulated gene's terms carry mixed signs and cover a minority of those regulators -- so no single-gene control can ever be an exact analogue. Interpretive limit, unchanged from V-007: the control shows the loss is that one value; it does not say whether the value is an abundance artifact or legitimate autoregulatory signal for the 71% of TFs with a self-edge (F-010, F-011). Shared-regulator counts here (mean 5.1) are over all of CollecTRI's regulators, not only the TF-TF propagation-upstream set V-007 counted (5.9), which is why the coreg numbers sit within 0.01 of V-007's scratch rather than on top of them.
Depends on: F-004, F-007

## F-013 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: Supersedes F-005's "Adding the TF's own log fold change to ULM reaches 94% top-10%" (F-005 is not edited). 94% is the best of three weights and must be cited as "up to 94%": adding w * (own logFC, scaled by the global std of the TF-gene logFC matrix) to every ULM score gives top-10% / top-5% of 0.66 / 0.56 at w = 0.5, 0.77 / 0.67 at w = 1, and 0.94 / 0.86 at w = 2, against plain ULM 0.45 / 0.35; paired against ULM the three weights are better 0.91 / 0.91 / 0.89 and worse 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.03. The abundance point F-005 makes is unaffected: even the weakest weight tested beats ULM on 91% of experiments. Second, per T-008, every rate quoted in F-001, F-004, F-005 and here (45%, 57%, 34%, 94%) is conditional on the perturbed TF getting a ULM score, i.e. on 279 of knockTF's 388 experiments (155 TFs). The 109 dropped experiments split 90 (63 TFs) whose perturbed TF is not a source in CollecTRI at all and 19 (16 TFs) whose TF is in CollecTRI but has fewer than tmin=5 measured targets (those 16 TFs have 1-4 measured targets). No rate here is a rate over knockTF.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python leak.py` (about 1 min; reruns propagate.py and controls.py) prints "ulm + 0.5*own mRNA 0.66 0.56 0.91 0.01", "ulm + 1*own mRNA 0.77 0.67 0.91 0.02", "ulm + 2*own mRNA 0.94 0.86 0.89 0.03", with "ulm 0.45 0.35" above them and the masked table's "ulm 0.34 0.28" below. The 279 / 90 / 19 split is one scratch count (not committed, no new script per T-007) over decoupler's own preprocessing: `dc.pp.prune(features=var, net=net, tmin=5)` on the extracted knockTF matrix, comparing each experiment's obs.source against net.source and pnet.source; 279 + 90 + 19 = 388 and the 279 match F-001's scored set and its 155 TFs.
Source: analysis/leak.py, analysis/controls.py
Confidence: high, deterministic reruns of committed scripts on cached data; the drop split is a set membership count with no free parameter other than tmin=5, which is the value every script here uses. The own-mRNA weights are not fitted or cross-validated: 2 is simply the largest of the three tried, so "94%" is an upper bound over a 3-point grid, and a larger weight would push it higher still (in the limit the score is the own logFC alone).
Depends on: F-005

## F-017 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist (entered verbatim by main session) · status: proposed
Claim: The provenance of a CollecTRI self-edge's sign does not change how it acts on ULM, but it does say which hits the unmasked knockTF benchmark is buying. F-011's 176 "+1" self-edge experiments split by `sign_decision` into 92 default activation (50 TFs, median regulon 78 targets), 74 PMID (29 TFs, 186 targets) and 10 regulon (5 TFs, 97 targets); the 21 "-1" are 20 PMID and 1 regulon, and every crosstab reproduces F-016 (42990 edges, 461 self-edges, 96 knockTF TFs with a self-edge, 197 + 82 = 279 experiments). Of the 33 top-10% hits ULM loses when the perturbed TF's self-edge is removed, 22 sit on a default-activation self-edge, 7 on PMID and 4 on regulon: two thirds of the loss rests on an edge whose sign is CollecTRI's fill for "no information", against a predicted proportional 17/14/2. By group, top-10% goes 0.565 -> 0.326 on self-edge removal for default (52/92 -> 30/92, masked 29/92, paired worse 0.96), 0.541 -> 0.446 for PMID (40/74 -> 33/74, masked 34/74, worse 0.91), 0.600 -> 0.200 for regulon (6/10 -> 2/10), and 0.400 -> 0.550 for PMID -1 (8/20 -> 11/20, better 0.80); the 82 no-self-edge experiments stay 20/82 throughout. The skew is size, not provenance: within regulon-size bins the median |change in the TF's own score| is the same for default and PMID (<=60: 1.154 vs 1.527 t-units, n=33 vs 5; 61-150: 0.551 vs 0.578, n=40 vs 29; >150: 0.225 vs 0.253, n=19 vs 40), as the algebra requires, and the residual rank-change difference in the 61-150 bin (+58 vs +18 ranks of 766) tracks starting rank (median rank fraction 0.084 default vs 0.055 PMID), not sign source. Whole-regulon accounting: the median perturbed TF has 53% of its scored regulon default-signed (IQR 0.15-0.62), and masked ULM does NOT separate the two halves — 0.352 top-10% (45/128) for regulons under 50% default vs 0.338 (51/151) for 50% or more, against a predicted 0.40 vs 0.28; within size bins 0.180/0.172 (<=60), 0.422/0.340 (61-150), 0.515/0.575 (>150). So sign quality, at the resolution knockTF can see, is not what limits ULM; regulon size still is.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python signsource.py` (about 1 min; imports selfedge.py, which reruns propagate.py, controls.py and leak.py, so F-001/F-004/F-006/F-010's tables print above blocks 5 and 6). Conditions ("ulm (full net)", "no self-edge", "masked"), ULM kernel and evaluate() are selfedge.py's, imported unchanged; the sign split is recomputed from `adjm_orig[var_i[tf], src_i[tf]]` exactly as selfsign.py does (selfsign.py is not imported, it was being edited) and labelled with `sign_decision` read from data/collectri.parquet's source == target rows; V-011 established raw and pruned self-edge signs agree cell by cell on all 279. The regulon-composition number uses the pruned scoring network with `sign_decision` merged back on (source, target), since dc.pp.prune drops extra columns. Predictions were in the docstring first: "about 17 default / 14 PMID / 2 regulon" (observed 22/7/4), "provenance should NOT matter beyond size" (confirmed on the score perturbation, the quantity the algebra fixes), "0.40 vs 0.28 masked" (observed 0.352 vs 0.338). No gain over ULM is claimed, so no shuffled network; "no self-edge" and "masked" are the controls and are in every block. Final assert: crosstab totals 42990 / 461 / 279 and the six sign_decision groups' hits sum to selfedge.py's 126 (full) and 96 (no self-edge).
Source: analysis/signsource.py
Confidence: High for the crosstabs and the 22/7/4 attribution (read off the file and off F-010's own hit sets; totals reconcile to F-016 and to 126/96). Medium for "provenance does not matter beyond size": the score-perturbation equality is solid and algebraically expected, but the hit-level comparison has 5 PMID experiments in the <=60 bin, 19 default experiments above 150, and 279 experiments over 155 TFs with the PMID arm the more clustered (74 experiments, 29 TFs); no TF-level rerun. Low-medium for the block-4 non-difference (0.352 vs 0.338): a hard 0.5 cut on default fraction, no threshold sensitivity, size still differs within bins, and the strata are not independent. Nothing here says a default +1 sign is biologically right or wrong, only that flipping the label it carries would not move this benchmark.
Depends on: F-016, F-011

## F-018 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: In knockTF, the own-logFC slope that F-015's EB-slope model is built on is not distinguishable from a constant knockdown offset, and the dose variation needed to identify it is not in the data. On the 279 scored experiments (155 TFs; 52 TFs with >= 2 experiments = 176 experiments; 103 singletons), with a = the self-edge-dropped ULM score (selfedge.py's es_ns, RAW, not type_p x a) and m = the perturbed TF's own logFC read from the scored matrix: corr(a, m) = 0.095; OLS with an intercept gives slope 0.413, intercept -0.901; the within-TF slope (TF fixed intercepts, 176 experiments) is 0.079 with a TF-cluster bootstrap 90% CI of [-0.26, +0.38] (sd 0.20), which INCLUDES 0. The through-origin pooled slope is 0.815 on real m against 0.743 (5-95%: 0.68-0.82) on own logFC permuted across experiments within regulon-size tercile (500 permutations), so the placebo recovers 91% of the real slope and the real value sits at the 94th percentile of the placebo distribution, not outside it; in-sample RMSE ratio vs a = 0 is 0.905 real, 0.921 placebo (5-95%: 0.903-0.935), and 0.904 for subtracting the constant mean(a) = -1.68, i.e. a one-parameter constant beats the one-parameter slope in sample. Out of sample (leave-one-experiment-out on the 176, every quantity re-estimated inside the fold: global terms on the other 278 experiments, per-TF terms on that TF's remaining experiments) RMSE is 4.50 null (a = 0), 3.98 global slope x m, 3.96 global mean, 3.19 per-TF slope x m, 2.74 per-TF mean (median |error| 1.62 / 1.66 / 1.73 / 1.65 / 1.39); the no-logFC predictor wins at both levels, and RMSE(per-TF slope) - RMSE(per-TF mean) = +0.46 with a TF-cluster bootstrap 90% CI of [+0.17, +0.77], excluding 0 in the direction "slope is WORSE". Dropping REST (6 experiments, 45% of the null's squared error; V-015's preview said 5 and 42%, the scored set has 6) leaves null 3.40, global slope 2.93, global mean 2.93, per-TF slope 2.99, per-TF mean 2.69, difference +0.30, CI [+0.12, +0.53], same direction. 50 of the 155 raw per-TF through-origin slopes are negative (32.3%, median b_t 0.35) against a model-implied expectation of 0.318 +/- 0.037 at the fitted mu = 0.815, tau = 1.07, sigma = 2.82 (DerSimonian-Laird), so F-015's kill condition K4 ("more than 30% negative") fires 0.5 sd BELOW the fitted model's own prediction and is a miscalibrated threshold, not a test. The reason is dose: m is negative in 279 of 279 (min -8.53, q25 -2.16, median -1.74, q75 -1.32, max -1.01) and the within-TF range of m over the 52 multi-experiment TFs has median 0.71; only 20 of 52 TFs (20 of 155 overall) have a within-TF range above 1 logFC and 8 above 2, so for 32 of the 52 TFs that could in principle identify a slope there is under 1 logFC of variation to identify it with. F-018 does not refute a dose-response in general; it says knockTF cannot see one, and that the abundance component of F-015 should be written as a constant offset, not as btilde x m, unless a dose-resolved set says otherwise.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python slopecheck.py` (44 s wall; imports selfedge.py, which reruns propagate.py, controls.py and leak.py, so F-001/F-004/F-010's tables print above this one and reproduce unchanged). Predictions were stated in the docstring before the run, taken from V-015's scratch preview, and every one landed: corr 0.10 (0.095), slope with intercept 0.41 / -0.90 (0.413 / -0.901), within-TF slope 0.08 +/- 0.34 (0.079, bootstrap sd 0.20), real mu 0.82 vs placebo 0.75 [0.69-0.83] (0.815 vs 0.743 [0.68-0.82]), LOO RMSE 4.50 / 3.98 / 3.96 / 3.19 / 2.74 (exact to 2 dp on all five), negative slopes 50/155 (50/155). The two CIs are new (the preview had none): the within-TF CI includes 0 and the out-of-sample CI excludes 0 in the wrong direction for F-015, which are the two results declared in advance as the only things that could overturn the claim -- the declared overturning conditions were "within-TF slope CI excluding 0" or "per-TF slope beating per-TF mean out of sample with a CI excluding 0", and neither happened. Controls, stated in the script: the perturbed-TF mask is DEGENERATE here because the input is already the self-edge-dropped score and F-010 shows self-edge deletion reproduces masking for ULM (96/279 both), so zeroing the gene moves a_te by a median 0.002 t (V-015 (B)); the degree-preserving shuffle has nothing to protect because no rank gain is claimed and the script prints no top-10% number of its own; the control that bites is the permuted-own-logFC placebo, and the real fit does not clear it. Judgment calls: LOO folds estimate global terms on the other 278 experiments (singletons inform the global fit, as F-015 designs) and per-TF terms on that TF's remaining experiments; regulon size = nonzero column count of the self-edge-dropped pruned adjacency; 2000 TF-cluster bootstrap draws, 500 permutations, seed 0. Assert: 279 / 155 / 176 counts, m < 0 in every experiment, and the through-origin fit exactly zero-residual on all 103 singletons.
Source: analysis/slopecheck.py (stdout only, no files written); imports analysis/selfedge.py
Confidence: high for the numbers (deterministic apart from two seeded resamplings; six of six preview numbers reproduced independently). High for "not distinguishable from a constant offset IN KNOCKTF": three independent readings agree (within-TF CI includes 0, placebo recovers 91% of the pooled slope, per-TF mean beats per-TF slope out of sample with a CI excluding 0). Medium for the mechanism being lack of dose range rather than absence of a dose-response: (5) shows the range is small, but small range and zero true slope are not separable here; the 20 TFs with a range above 1 logFC are too few to fit a credible slope-vs-range interaction. Not tested: a nonlinear or saturating dose-response, anything at m near 0 (the applied regime, where knockTF has no data at all: max m is -1.01), and whether the same holds outside knockTF x CollecTRI.
Depends on: F-010, F-015

## V-012 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — every number reproduces and the two committed arms are V-007's constructions (coreg picks the same gene as my scratch in 256 of the 260 scored experiments where my scratch made a pick). Four wordings to fix when citing: the entry's explanation of 5.1 vs 5.9 shared regulators is wrong (both counts are over all of CollecTRI; the gap is 53 experiments whose TF gene has no regulator, which V-007 left unmasked and coregmask.py fills with a non-co-regulated gene); the "what each arm zeroes" numbers are over all 388 experiments, the same 388-vs-279 slip the entry corrects in F-007 (on the 279 scored: coreg median -1.445, 65% with |logFC| >= 1, 6.3 shared; logfc -1.423, 87%; own -1.74); "0% exactly zero" is 3 of 1164 picks (one scored experiment, where the coreg mask is a no-op) and nothing in the code forbids it; "supersedes F-007's control arms" should read "replaces them as the arms to cite", F-007 stays confirmed and its numbers reprint unchanged here
Claim checked: F-012 — zeroing, instead of the knocked-down TF's own gene, (coreg) the most negative-logFC gene among the 100 genes whose CollecTRI regulator set is most Jaccard-similar to the TF gene's, or (logfc) the gene whose logFC is nearest the TF's own among genes in at least (TF gene's regulon count - 3) regulons, leaves ULM at 0.437 / 0.444 and up/sum/a=0.5 propagation at 0.563 / 0.566 (shuffled 0.292 / 0.300), against 0.34 / 0.28 for the real mask; so the 57->28 collapse is that one cell and not "a large negative co-regulated logFC".
How verified: (1) Numbers. Reran `cd analysis && uv run python coregmask.py` (1 min 5 s, exit 0, assert passes). Printed: coreg ulm 0.437 [0.437-0.437] top5 0.362; coreg up sum a=0.5 0.563 top5 0.459 better 0.595 worse 0.358; coreg SHUF 0.292 top5 0.184; logfc ulm 0.444 top5 0.358; logfc up sum a=0.5 0.566 top5 0.473 better 0.609 worse 0.355; logfc SHUF 0.300 top5 0.189; any ulm 0.452 [0.452-0.452] 0.355, any up 0.566 0.473 better 0.594 worse 0.370, any SHUF 0.307 [0.306-0.307]; matched ulm 0.452 [0.444-0.455] 0.356, matched up 0.566 0.472 better 0.596 worse 0.366, matched SHUF 0.307 [0.306-0.309]. The any/matched block is character-identical to randmask.py's table as I verified it in V-007. Masked-gene table: any 1940 picks median 0.000 / |0.066| / exactly zero 0.36 / |lfc|>=1 0.05; matched 1940, 0.000 / 0.131 / 0.19 / 0.07; coreg 1164, -1.601 / 1.601 / 0.00 / 0.70; logfc 1164, -1.464 / 1.464 / 0.00 / 0.89; own logFC median -1.68, negative in 100%. Regulon gap 2.05 over 388, 2.69 over 279; median regulons of the TF gene 8 all, 12 scored; coreg mean shared regulators 5.1. Stdout is identical to the experimentalist's coreg.log apart from one blank line. The reference tables above it (leak.py / controls.py) print 0.45, 0.57, SHUF x10 0.31 [0.28-0.33], masked 0.34 / 0.28 / 0.26 [0.24-0.27]. Every number in F-012 matches. Predictions were numeric and in the docstring (0.44/0.56/0.29 and 0.45/0.565/0.30). No gain is claimed; both convention controls are nevertheless in the output through the imports. (2) Same construction as V-007? Scratch script v012_audit.py (session scratchpad, imports coregmask and reads its pick table; nothing written into the repo, no .pyc written, nothing under .venv touched). COREG: my V-007 scratch (rm_audit.py, `coreg(e,t,k=100,"min")`) used regulator sets over ALL CollecTRI sources restricted to measured targets, Jaccard, top 100, idxmin of logFC; coregmask.py does the same with a matrix product. Picks agree in 324 of 388 experiments (256 of 279 scored). The 64 disagreements are: 53 experiments (19 scored) whose TF gene has NO regulator in CollecTRI, where my scratch returned None and masked nothing, and coregmask.py, with every Jaccard equal to 0, takes the 100 first genes in column order and zeroes the most negative of those; and 11 (4 scored) tie-breaks at the rank-100 boundary (stable argsort on column order vs pandas nlargest on groupby order). That accounts for coreg ULM 0.437 here vs 0.441 in V-007, which is ONE experiment of 279 (122 vs 123 hits); propagation 0.563 and shuffled 0.292 are identical to V-007's. The entry's explanation of the shared-regulator gap is wrong: V-007's 5.9 was `len(regs[g] & regs[t])` over all of CollecTRI, not over the TF-TF upstream set. On the 335 experiments where V-007 made a pick the committed arm shares a mean 5.89 regulators against my 5.93; the 53 filler picks share 0.00 and pull the 388-mean to 5.09. Counted over the TF-TF propagation-upstream set, the committed picks share 4.32 of 15.2 (all 388) or 6.00 of 21.2 (scored), so that reading would not give 5.9 either. Same story for the medians: V-007's -1.51 was over its 335 picks (recomputed: -1.515), the committed -1.601 is over 388. LOGFC: not the same picker, by design and in two ways. V-007 drew at random from the 10 nearest-logFC genes with regulon floor max(memb-3, 1), 3 seeds (0.452 / 0.565 / 0.304); coregmask.py takes the single nearest with floor max(memb-3, 0) (0.444 / 0.566 / 0.300). Different genes are zeroed, so 2 ULM hits and 0.4 points of shuffled null are within what V-007's seed range showed. The floor of 0 matters: 50 of the 279 scored experiments (127 of 388) have a TF gene in <= 3 regulons, so the arm has no regulon constraint there, and 28 scored picks (10.0%; 71 of 388) sit in NO regulon and can reach a ULM score only as one more y value, not through any edge. (3) 388 vs 279. Every descriptive number in the "what each arm zeroes" table and the 5.1 is pooled over all 388 experiments, 109 of which cannot contribute to any rate in the table above it. On the 279 scored: coreg median logFC -1.445, |lfc| >= 1 in 65%, mean shared regulators 6.32 (median 3), pick more negative than the TF's own value in 39%, median pick/own ratio 0.80, 9.3% of picks are themselves scored TFs; 23 picks (8.2%) share no regulator with the TF gene (the 19 no-regulator experiments plus 4), and with those removed the median is -1.455 (n=256). logfc scored: median -1.423, 87% with |lfc| >= 1, median |pick - own| 0.049, 20.4% of picks are targets of the perturbed TF, 13.3% are scored TFs. Own logFC: -1.683 over 388, -1.739 over the 279 scored (V-005's -1.74), negative in 388 of 388, never exactly 0. any/matched on scored: |0.062| / 35.0% exactly zero and |0.134| / 15.1%, equal to V-007's audit. None of this weakens the control (a -1.45 median is still about 4 per-experiment sd and the ULM and propagation rates are computed on the scored set regardless), but the entry faults F-007 for printing 2.05 over 388 instead of 2.69 over 279 and then does the same thing in its own table. (4) Assert audit. 6208 = 388 x (5+5+3+3) picks; `(P.tf != P.gene).all()` holds (0 equal). `zeroed` has 16 flags (every arm x seed), each testing that all picked cells are 0 and that every perturbed TF's own cell equals the original; all 388 TFs are measured, so that clause covers all 388 rows and would also catch a pick equal to the TF. It tests the DataFrame X before `ad.AnnData(X)` is built rather than the AnnData that is scored; I rebuilt the coreg seed-0 AnnData and checked a.X directly: picked cells 0, own cells intact. Index and columns are unique, so `get_loc` returns scalars. Can a pick be the perturbed TF's own gene? No, in all four arms by construction: any filters `memb.index != tf`, matched drops tf, coreg sets the TF's Jaccard to -1 (so it sorts last of 21985 and can never be in the top 100), logfc sets `ok[tf] = False`. Can a pick be a gene that is already zero? YES, nothing prevents it, and the assert does not test it: any 699 of 1940 and matched 373 of 1940 (as the entry says), coreg 3 of 1164 (one scored experiment x 3 seeds: the rebuilt coreg matrix differs from the original in 387 cells, not 388), logfc 0 of 1164. "0% exactly zero" for coreg is 0.26% rounded. Both new arms are identical across their 3 seeds, as the entry says. (5) "Supersedes". F-007 is confirmed by V-007 on its numbers and on its conclusion, and V-007 already said its any/matched arms should not be cited as the control. F-012 refutes nothing in F-007; it reprints F-007's table unchanged and commits the controls V-007 asked for. "Supersedes F-007's control arms" is right only in the sense "cite these arms instead of those"; F-007 is not withdrawn, and the index should keep it as confirmed with F-012 as the place to cite the control from. The 2.05 -> 2.69 correction is a correction of a description, already made in V-007.
Would flip if: a rerun prints different numbers; or a single-gene arm restricted to genes that share most of the TF gene's upstream regulators WITH COHERENT SIGNS and carry a logFC at or beyond the TF's own dropped propagated top-10% by more than a few points (the committed coreg pick is less negative than the own value in 61% of scored experiments, median ratio 0.80, and shares 6 of 21 upstream regulators, so it remains a weaker perturbation than the real mask, as the entry's confidence note concedes; no single gene can be an exact analogue); or the 23 scored experiments whose coreg pick shares no regulator turned out to carry the result (they cannot: removing them changes ULM by at most the one experiment that separates this run from V-007).
Severity: medium — this is now the committed control behind any sentence of the form "masking removes only the perturbed TF's own value" (F-004, F-006, F-007 do not depend on it; README wording does). Owed, not blocking: (a) coregmask.py should report its masked-gene table and shared-regulator mean on the 279 scored experiments, or on both; (b) when the TF gene has no CollecTRI regulator the coreg arm should either skip the experiment or say in its docstring that the pick is an arbitrary gene (53 experiments, 19 scored); (c) logfc floor max(memb-3, 1) as in V-007, so the pick is always on the network (28 scored picks are not); (d) the assert could add `(X0.values[r, c] != 0)` for the coreg and logfc arms, which today would fail on exactly one experiment and so would need the pick to skip zero cells; (e) the sentence about 5.1 vs 5.9 should be corrected in any write-up: same counting, different handling of the no-regulator experiments.

## V-013 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — all three own-mRNA lines and the 279 / 90 / 19 split (155 / 63 / 16 TFs) reproduce, the split by an independent hand count that does not use dc.pp.prune. One wording to tighten: "fewer than tmin=5 measured targets" is true, but for 15 of the 16 TFs the reason is that CollecTRI lists fewer than 5 targets for them at all; measurement removes a target only for OVOL2 (2 -> 1)

How verified: (1) Reran `cd analysis && uv run python leak.py` (15 s, not "about 1 min"; exit 0, both asserts pass). Printed: "ulm 0.45 0.35 0.00 0.00", "ulm + 0.5*own mRNA 0.66 0.56 0.91 0.01", "ulm + 1*own mRNA 0.77 0.67 0.91 0.02", "ulm + 2*own mRNA 0.94 0.86 0.89 0.03", masked "ulm 0.34 0.28". Exact counts from a scratch import of controls.py (v013_exact.py, scratchpad): ULM 126 / 99 of 279; w=0.5 185 / 155 (0.6631 / 0.5556), better 254 worse 2 ties 23; w=1 216 / 188 (0.7742 / 0.6738), better 253 worse 5 ties 21; w=2 263 / 241 (0.9427 / 0.8638), better 249 worse 9 ties 21. The scale is 0.6535 (std of the experiments x 766 scored-TF-columns logFC block, as the entry says). Code read: controls.py adds `a * own` to the signed score S and evaluate() ranks type_p * score, so with every own logFC negative (388 of 388, none exactly 0) and type_p = -1 the term always helps the true TF; it is the trivial competitor the docstring says it is. The entry's closing remark checks out: w = 4 gives 272 / 264 (0.975 / 0.946), w = 8 gives 274 / 266, and the own logFC alone (w = 1e6) gives 274 / 267 = 0.982 / 0.957, so 94% is an arbitrary point on a curve that saturates at 98%, and "better" falls slowly as w grows (254 -> 246) because ULM's information is being thrown away. (2) Independent split (v013_drop.py, scratchpad, absolute data paths, nothing written into the repo): counted, by hand, each source's CollecTRI targets that are among knockTF's 21985 measured genes (no duplicate edges in the parquet; 50 measured genes are all-zero and none belongs to the 16 TFs' target lists, so excluding them changes nothing; 0 NaN cells), then classed each of the 388 experiments (234 distinct TFs, all type_p = -1): scored 279 experiments / 155 TFs; TF absent from net.source 90 / 63; TF present with < 5 measured targets 19 / 16; 279 + 90 + 19 = 388 and 155 + 63 + 16 = 234. The hand-counted scored set equals, experiment for experiment, the set for which decoupler's `dc.mt.ulm(tmin=5)` returns a column. The 16 TFs and their measured targets: BATF3 3, CBFB 4, CDC5L 1, FOXK1 3, FUS 4, GTF2F1 1, HMBOX1 3, HOXC4 3, OVOL2 1, PRDM14 1, RBFOX2 1, SIX5 1, SP100 3, TET2 1, ZNF431 1, ZNF750 1; range 1-4, as claimed. Their TOTAL CollecTRI target counts are the same numbers except OVOL2 (2), so this group is dropped because CollecTRI is thin for them, not because knockTF fails to measure their targets. The smallest scored TFs have 5, 5, 6 measured targets, so tmin=5 is applied as ">= 5". Every perturbed TF's own gene is measured (0 of 388 missing).
Would flip if: a rerun prints different numbers, or a freshly fetched knockTF / CollecTRI changes the counts (the split is a property of these two cached files and of tmin=5; at tmin=3, 7 of the 16 TFs would enter). The "up to 94%" reading cannot flip: it is a statement about a grid, and the curve beyond the grid is now measured.
Severity: medium — (1) fixes how F-005's headline number may be quoted ("up to 94% at the largest of three weights; 98% for the own logFC alone", never "94%" bare); (2) is the denominator of every rate in this log and belongs in README next to the 45%: 28% of knockTF's experiments (109 of 388; 79 of its 234 TFs) are never scored, 83% of those because the TF is not in CollecTRI at all. Owed, not blocking: README should state the 279-of-388 conditioning once; nothing else.

How verified: (1) Rerun. `cd analysis && uv run python signsource.py`, exit 0, 33 s wall. The final assert checks 42990 / 461 / 279 and the 126 / 96 hit sums only; `grep 19396\|1895\|21699 analysis/signsource.py` returns nothing, so V-016's owed item (1) is still owed (the three counts do print: 19396 / 1895 / 21699). No shuffled-network control, correctly: no gain over ULM is claimed; "no self-edge" and "masked" are in every block. All further checks are scratch scripts v017_dump.py / v017_a.py / v017_b.py / v017_c.py / v017_e.py in the session scratchpad, importing signsource.py / selfedge.py unchanged, absolute output paths, nothing written to the repo, nothing under .venv touched. (2) Merge. pnet has 40728 rows, all with a scored source; after the left merge 40728 rows, 0 NaN sign_decision, 0 duplicate (source, target) in either table, 0 weight mismatches pruned vs raw, and reg_size equals the pruned row count for all 279. Pruned vs raw default fraction per TF (155 TFs): Pearson 0.9989, median |diff| 0.001, max 0.100; experiment-level raw median / IQR 0.522 / 0.148-0.631 vs pruned 0.533 / 0.148-0.622. Three TFs cross the 0.5 cut between raw and pruned (SOX4 0.455 vs 0.500, BCL6 0.495 vs 0.505, RELB 0.500 vs 0.475), which is a statement about how little a hard 0.5 cut means, not about the merge. The merge is clean. (3) 22 / 7 / 4. The 33 lost hits are spread over 29 TFs (default: 20 TFs, max 2 per TF, NR2F2 and YBX1; PMID: 7 TFs, 1 each; regulon: FOXM1 3, SALL4 1), so no single TF makes the count. Permuting sign_decision among the 84 +1 self-edge TFs (experiments stay with their TF, 20000 permutations): null mean lost-in-default 19.6 (95% 14-25), because default is 50 of 84 TFs (60%) even though it is 92 of 176 experiments (52%); the entry's 17 / 14 / 2 is the experiment-proportional expectation and the PMID arm has more experiments per TF (2.6 vs 1.8). One-sided p for the excess over experiment-proportional = 0.051, for the loss-rate difference default minus rest (0.239 vs 0.131) = 0.054, two-sided 0.115. TFs with at least one lost hit: 20/50 default vs 7/29 PMID, Fisher p = 0.22. So 22 / 7 / 4 is not distinguishable from a label-blind split at the TF level. Shuffling labels only within the three size bins: null mean 21.4 (16-26), one-sided p = 0.33, two-sided 0.67; within size sextiles 20.8, p = 0.29. So the entry's explanation is sufficient overall: once size bin is fixed the expected count is 21.4 and 22 was observed. Per bin (lost / full hits / n): <=60 default 12/20/33, PMID 3/3/5, regulon 1/1/3; 61-150 default 10/21/40, PMID 1/15/29, regulon 3/5/7; >150 default 0/11/19, PMID 3/22/40. The overall null hides one bin that does not balance: in 61-150, 10 of the 11 default-or-PMID losses are default, TF-label permutation within that bin p = 0.024 (null mean 6.4); one bin of three, picked after looking, so weak. (4) The 61-150 bin. Bin edges are harmless (no regulon of exactly 60 or 150; nearest are 59, 61, 62, 151), medians are not tie artefacts, and leave-one-TF-out leaves the medians at 52-62 (default) and 15-24 (PMID). STAT3 (10 experiments) is PMID +1 with 425 targets, so it sits in the >150 bin, not here: it is 10 of the 40 PMID >150 experiments and IS that cell's "median size 425" (without STAT3: n = 30, median size 300, rank change +15, |ds| 0.223, hits 18 -> 15, nothing moves); it has default fraction 0.000 and is 10 of block 4's 33 ">150 mostly evidence" (17/33 becomes 13/23; whole table 41/118 = 0.347 vs 51/151). In 61-150 the PMID arm is 29 experiments of 13 TFs with FLI1 6, SOX2 5, MITF 4 (15 of 29) sitting at starting ranks 0.137 / 0.007 / 0.032 with rank changes +11 / +4 / +6; the default arm is 40 of 18 TFs with NR2F2 6 and YBX1 6 at +138 / +95. Per experiment: Spearman(rank change, starting rank) = 0.52 and (rank change, |ds|) = 0.42, n = 69. Median rank-change gap 40, TF-cluster bootstrap CI [2, 62]; experiment-level Mann-Whitney p = 0.013, on TF medians p = 0.11. OLS of rank change on group: +17.1 ranks (TF-cluster CI [-47, +60]); adding |ds| +10.3; adding starting rank +15.2; both +6.3 [-46, +43]. Counterfactual with the score shift held at exactly 0.55 t-units for every experiment: median TFs overtaken 46 default vs 30 PMID, so where the TF starts in its experiment's score distribution accounts for about 16 of the 40. But the groups' starting ranks do not differ detectably (Mann-Whitney p = 0.37 by experiment, 0.52 by TF), the |ds| MEANS are 0.769 vs 0.655 although the medians are 0.551 / 0.578, and among the experiments that matter for 22 / 7 / 4, the bin's full-network hits (21 default, 15 PMID), starting rank is the SAME (median 0.020 vs 0.017) while |ds| is 0.767 vs 0.426, rank change +35 vs +5, no-self-edge rank 0.090 vs 0.020 and masked rank 0.087 vs 0.020. So "tracks starting rank, not sign source" is wrong as the account of why this bin loses 10 default hits and 1 PMID hit: the default hits start in the same place, get a larger push from the self-edge, and have little behind them once it is gone. Whether that is provenance or "well-studied TFs have regulons that carry the hit without the self-edge" cannot be separated here. (5) Block 4. Masked top-10%, evidence minus default, TF-cluster bootstrap 95% CI (4000 resamples of the 155 TFs): cut 0.25, 26/77 = 0.338 vs 70/202 = 0.347, diff -0.009 [-0.19, +0.16]; cut 0.5, 0.352 vs 0.338, +0.014 [-0.14, +0.17]; cut 0.75, 89/248 = 0.359 vs 7/31 = 0.226, +0.133 [-0.06, +0.35], but the 31 have median size 32 vs 82; size-bin-stratified at 0.5: +0.016 [-0.12, +0.16]. TF-level mean hit rate 0.235 / 0.294, 0.275 / 0.281, 0.295 / 0.175 at the three cuts. Spearman of masked rank fraction vs default fraction (+ = more default, worse): experiments -0.024 [-0.18, +0.13], partial on log size -0.033 [-0.19, +0.13]; TF-level (n = 155, mean rank fraction per TF) -0.102 [-0.26, +0.06], partial -0.110 [-0.26, +0.05]; regulons over 60 only -0.007 / -0.002. No threshold and no level shows an effect and the point estimates sit at zero, but every interval contains the entry's own predicted gap of 0.12; what is excluded is a gap above about 0.17. That is "cannot tell at this n", and the stated conclusion needs the weaker wording. Also: default fraction does NOT track regulon size (TF-level Spearman -0.02, p = 0.81; the two halves' median sizes are 75 and 79), so the script's closing NOTE ("sign provenance tracks how well studied a TF is, which tracks regulon size") holds for the self-edge's label (78 vs 186) and is false for the regulon fraction; block 4 was never confounded by size, it is simply flat. (6) A more direct probe of "sign quality", because a flat default fraction is a weak test (exploratory, mine, post hoc, no shuffled-network control, NOT a claim). Rescoring leak.py's masked matrix with selfedge.py's kernel (baseline reproduces 96/279 and equals leak.py's rank fractions exactly): all signs set to +1: 81/279, top-5% 78 -> 55, 31 hits lost / 16 gained, exact McNemar p = 0.040, Wilcoxon on rank fraction p = 0.005; all default edges removed: 89/279 (21 lost / 14 gained, p = 0.31; 5 experiments of 4 TFs become unscoreable, none was a hit; adjacency not re-pruned to tmin = 5); default edges flipped to -1 for the TFs whose PMID-signed edges are majority repressing (what the paper's sentence, as opposed to the script's rule, would do; V-016 (c)): 96/279 (7 lost / 7 gained), those TFs' own experiments 5/43 -> 5/43 with 65% ranked worse; their default edges removed instead: 99/279, 5/43 -> 7/43. So signs carry something (erasing them costs 15 hits), and neither deleting nor "correcting" the default fills buys anything: this supports the weak form of F-017's conclusion better than block 4 does. (7) V-016's ten (default +1 self-edge, PMID-majority-repressing regulon: ID1, NR2F2, TWIST2, NCOR1, YBX1, HINFP, TFAP4, FOXP1, NANOG, SOX17; 23 experiments, median size 79): full-network 12/23, no-self-edge and masked 2/23 (0.087), all 23 worse on removal, median masked rank 0.321; the other 40 default +1 TFs: 40/69 full, 27/69 = 0.391 masked, median masked rank 0.197, median size 76. Ten of the 22 default lost hits are theirs (10 of their 12 full hits; the 36 majority-activating default TFs lose 10 of 38, the 4 tie/none 2 of 2), and 6 of the 61-150 bin's 10. They behave like the +1 label says on removal (as V-011 (c) requires) and are then nearly unrecoverable. Generalising the class to all 155 TFs: PMID-majority-repressing with default edges (25 TFs, 43 experiments, median size 45) masked 5/43 = 0.116 vs majority-activating (85 TFs, 154 experiments, median 93) 66/154 = 0.429; TFs with no default edges 23/69; tie/none 2/13. TF-label permutation p = 0.0013, within size bin p = 0.0054 (<=60: 2/24 vs 13/53; 61-150: 3/19 vs 21/45; no majority-repressing TF above 150); but TF-level mean masked rank fraction 0.387 vs 0.323, Mann-Whitney p = 0.14, so the gap is in top-10% counts more than in ranks. Flipping or deleting their default edges does not rescue them (step 6), so this is not evidence that the fill is what hurts them; candidates not separated here: mixed-sign small regulons, knockdown of a repressor giving a weaker target signature, size within bin.
Would flip if: any rerun number differed (none does). For (a): a TF-level permutation p well under 0.05 two-sided for the 22 / 7 / 4 split, or a within-size-bin null mean far from 22 (it is 21.4). For (b): if among the 61-150 bin's full-network hits the default arm started materially further down the ranking than the PMID arm (it does not: 0.020 vs 0.017), the starting-rank account would stand. For (c): a TF-cluster CI on the masked hit-rate difference that excluded the predicted 0.12, which needs roughly twice the TFs, or an intervention on the default signs that moved masked ULM (flip and delete both leave it at 96-99 of 279). The merge check would flip on any NaN sign_decision after the merge or a row-count change (40728 before and after).
Severity: medium — nothing downstream is built on F-017 yet, but two of its sentences are the kind that get copied into README. Cite as: "of the 33 top-10% hits ULM loses on self-edge removal, 22 sit on a default-filled +1 self-edge (20 TFs), 7 on PMID, 4 on regulon; this split is not distinguishable from a label-blind one at the TF level (p = 0.05 one-sided, 0.33 within size bins)". Do NOT cite "against a proportional 17 / 14 / 2" as a skew, do NOT cite "the residual in the 61-150 bin tracks starting rank", and replace "sign quality is not what limits ULM" with "no association between a regulon's default-signed fraction and masked top-10% at any cut (difference +0.014, 95% CI -0.14 to +0.17; Spearman -0.02); gaps above about 0.17 are excluded, the predicted 0.12 is not; deleting or flipping default signs leaves masked ULM at 96-99 of 279 while erasing all signs costs 15 hits (p = 0.04)". "Regulon size still is" stands (Spearman of masked rank vs size -0.27). Owed: (1) the assert on 19396 / 1895 / 21699, asked for in V-016, still missing from signsource.py; (2) fix or drop the script's closing NOTE, default fraction does not track size; (3) if the sign-erasure / flip / delete numbers in step 6 are to be cited they need their own F-entry and script with the predicted number stated first (they are a skeptic's scratch probe); (4) the PMID-majority-repressing stratum (5/43 vs 66/154 masked, p = 0.005 within size bin) is a post hoc lead that needs a pre-stated prediction, a TF-level rank-based test (currently p = 0.14) and a check against knockdown efficiency (own logFC) before anyone calls it a finding; (5) F-017's own caveat stands: no TF-level rerun of the group tables ships in the script.

## V-018 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: split — every number reproduces. CONFIRMED, and with teeth: F-015's through-origin per-TF slope at the size it predicted (mu about 1) is rejected by knockTF, not merely undetected; the design had 73% power for it (60% with m measured at SE 0.3) and the two observed within-TF statistics sit in that model's 2-5% and under-2% tails. NOT established: that no dose-response exists in knockTF's range (a within-TF slope of 0.5 had 35-45% power and is as compatible with the data as a pure offset, likelihood ratio about 2), and REFUTED as a modelling recommendation: "the abundance component should be written as a constant offset". Two of F-018's three "independent readings" do not carry the weight given them: the placebo reading does not discriminate (the slope model itself predicts 85% recovery; the observed 91% is in the OFFSET model's 4% tail), and overturning condition (ii) had 7% power. Confidence "High" should read "high that mu >= 0.8 through the origin is wrong; low on anything below 0.5"
Claim checked: F-018 — on knockTF, regressing the self-edge-dropped ULM score a of the knocked-down TF on that TF's own logFC m gives nothing a per-TF constant does not give: within-TF slope 0.08 with a CI through 0, a permuted-m placebo recovering 91% of the pooled through-origin slope, a per-TF mean beating a per-TF slope out of sample by 0.46 RMSE; and the reason offered is that knockTF has almost no within-TF dose range. Conclusion drawn: write the abundance term as an offset, not btilde x m.
How verified: (1) RERUN `cd analysis && uv run python slopecheck.py` (32 s wall, assert passes, repo untouched). All match: corr 0.095; slope 0.413 / intercept -0.901; within-TF 0.079, 90% CI [-0.26, 0.38], sd 0.20; real 0.815 vs placebo 0.743 [0.68-0.82], real above 93.8% of placebos; in-sample ratios 0.905 / 0.921 [0.903-0.935] / 0.904; LOO 4.50 / 3.98 / 3.96 / 3.19 / 2.74 (med|err| 1.62 / 1.66 / 1.73 / 1.65 / 1.39), without REST 3.40 / 2.93 / 2.93 / 2.99 / 2.69; differences +0.46 [+0.17, +0.77] and +0.30 [+0.12, +0.53]; negative slopes 50/155 vs 0.318 +/- 0.037 at mu 0.815 / tau 1.07 / sigma 2.82; m quartiles -8.53 / -2.16 / -1.74 / -1.32 / -1.01, negative 279/279; range median 0.71, 20 of 52 above 1, 8 above 2. One number is a seed artefact: the upper CI end 0.38 is seed 0's draw; over 25 other seeds of the same 2000-draw bootstrap it is 0.40-0.45 (lower end -0.23 to -0.26). Cite [-0.25, +0.42]. REST: F-018 is right, 6 experiments and 45%. a = -19.8, -14.4, -9.7, -16.7, -17.8, -17.4; V-015 (me) dropped DataSet_01_211 (LNCaP, a = -9.7): the five with a <= -14 give 1498 / 3564 = 42%, all six give 1592 / 3564 = 45%. (4, sign and inputs) a is es_ns.loc[e, t] raw, m is mat[obs_i[e], var_i[t]] from the same extracted matrix, equal to obs.logFC in 279/279 (0 fallbacks); type_p = -1 in all; a < 0 in 199/279. Confirmed. (2) POWER, scratch only (numpy, seed 0, real m and TF structure kept, closed-form LOO and a vectorised TF-cluster bootstrap that reproduce 0.079 / +0.458 / 0.815 on the real data, 1000 simulated data sets per row, 1000 bootstrap draws each). a = b_t m + e, b_t ~ N(mu, 1), e ~ N(0, 2.8^2). Test (i) = within-TF slope 90% CI above 0; test (ii) = per-TF slope beats per-TF mean LOO with 90% CI below 0. mu 0.5: (i) 0.35, (ii) 0.04. mu 1.0: (i) 0.73, (ii) 0.07. mu 1.5: (i) 0.95, (ii) 0.20. At the fitted 0.815 / 1.07 / 2.82: (i) 0.56, (ii) 0.06. Heavy-tailed noise (real within-TF residuals, kurtosis 4.3, rescaled) changes nothing (0.31 / 0.72 / 0.95). With m observed at SE 0.3: (i) 0.29 / 0.60 / 0.86. On the 20 TFs with range > 1 alone (i) is 0.34 / 0.69 / 0.94: they carry all the power. So (ii) was never a fair overturning condition: even a true mu = 1.5 passes it one time in five, and the slope's point estimate wins only 49% of the time at mu = 1. But the observed result is not "no detection", it is the opposite tail. Probability of the observed statistics: P(within slope <= 0.079) = 0.154 / 0.018 / 0.001 at mu 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.5 (0.046 at the fitted values, 0.051 at mu 1 with SE 0.3 on m), against 0.59 under F-018's offset model (a = c_t + e, c_t ~ N(-1.68, 1.58^2), sigma 2.53, all fitted). P(RMSE difference >= +0.46) = 0.009 / 0.004 / 0.004 (0.016 with noisy m), against 0.20 under offset. P(both F-018 outcomes: CI includes 0 AND CI says slope worse) = 0.105 / 0.024 / 0.001, against 0.74 under offset. So F-015's model at mu >= 0.8 is rejected at about the 2-5% level on the within slope and under 2% on the LOO gap, and the offset model produces the observed within-TF pattern three times in four. THE PLACEBO DOES NOT DISCRIMINATE: under the true slope model a permuted m still recovers 0.85 of the slope (5-95%: 0.75-0.94 at mu 1; about mean(m)^2 / mean(m^2) by algebra), under offset 1.00 (0.92-1.08). The observed 0.916 (0.911 with tercile blocks) is inside the slope model's range and at the offset model's 4th percentile; likewise corr(a, m) = 0.095 is at the offset model's 92nd percentile (5-95%: -0.10 to +0.10) and unremarkable at mu 0.5 (-0.05 to +0.27). Those pooled statistics are between-TF and cannot show a dose-response, but they do not "agree" with the offset reading either; they lean slightly the other way. A third model, per-TF offset PLUS a common within-TF slope (a = c_t + w (m - mbar_t) + e): at w = 0.5 test (i) has power 0.45, P(slope-worse CI) = 0.56, P(both F-018 outcomes) = 0.35 against offset's 0.74; at w = 1.0 power 0.90 and P(within slope <= 0.079) = 0.002. knockTF cannot tell offset from offset + 0.5. (3) FAIRNESS OF THE LOO. The two per-TF predictors correlate 0.889; m_held / mean(m_rest) has median 0.98 (5-95%: 0.53-1.80), so the through-origin slope is the per-TF mean multiplied by a noisy ratio. The +0.46 lives entirely where m varies: range > 1 (20 TFs, 92 exps) slope 4.01 vs mean 3.29, +0.72 [+0.31, +1.22] (+0.50 [+0.25, +0.87] without REST; range > 2, 8 TFs: +0.78 [+0.26, +1.54]); range <= 1 (32 TFs, 84 exps) 1.93 vs 1.96, -0.03 [-0.20, +0.12], a tie. The fair comparator, per-TF mean + pooled within-TF slope x (m_held - mean m_rest), all inside the fold, scores 2.76 vs 2.74: +0.024 [+0.011, +0.035]. So the honest reading of (3) is two statements: own logFC adds nothing to a per-TF constant (+0.02, a fitted slope near 0 costs nothing), and PROPORTIONALITY through the origin is wrong where it can be tested (+0.72). It is evidence against a = b_t x m, not against a dose-response with an intercept. Within-TF slope on the range > 1 subset: +0.034, 90% CI [-0.29, +0.37], 95% [-0.35, +0.42]; range > 2: -0.080 [-0.50, +0.26]; range <= 1: +0.545 [-0.89, +1.95], empty. Bootstrap mass at or above 0.5 / 0.815 / 1.0 on all 52: 0.022 / 0.001 / 0.000. Leverage: 5 TFs hold 46% of the within-TF sum of squares of m (TP53 16%, NR2F2 9%, SP1 8%, MITF 7%, STAT3 7%); dropping TP53 gives +0.031, dropping the top three +0.144, dropping all five +0.497. The near-zero slope is a statement about roughly 20 TFs and leans on five. (4) ERRORS IN VARIABLES. F-015's 3% uses the raw second moment of m (mean m^2 = 4.21), which is right for a through-origin fit and wrong within TF. Pooled within-TF variance of m is 0.455 (sd 0.67, not the 0.3 one would guess from the median range; the 20 wide TFs dominate it). Reliability = (0.455 - s^2) / 0.455: 0.80 at SE 0.3; 0.78 leverage-weighted with each experiment's MAD-sd of nonzero logFC over the 15680 genes that are nobody's target (median 0.24); 0.87 on the 20 wide TFs; 0.06 on the 32 narrow ones. Treating the whole sd of non-target logFC (median 0.35, q90 0.86) as noise gives 0.19, a bound not an estimate, since that spread includes the knockdown's real effects. So a true slope of 1 would show as about 0.8, not 0.08; attenuation does not rescue F-015. Corrected upper end: 0.42 / 0.8 = about 0.5. (5) WORDING. "Should be written as a constant offset" is not supported. What the data support is negative only: the b_t x m form, fitted through the origin on knockdowns, is a rescaled mean and extrapolates badly even inside knockTF. A per-TF knockdown offset is not an abundance model: it predicts a nonzero abundance effect at m = 0, the regime the method is for (knockTF's max m is -1.01; decoupler's copy is truncated at logFC < -1, which is also why there is no dose range). V-015's severity line said this; F-018's last sentence lost it.
Would flip if: to plain confirmed: F-018's conclusion restated as "in knockTF a through-origin slope of F-015's predicted size (mu >= 0.8) is rejected (power 0.73, observed in the 2-5% tail); a within-TF slope below about 0.5 can be neither detected nor excluded (power 0.35-0.45); knockTF cannot calibrate the abundance term, in either form", with the placebo reading dropped from the list of agreeing evidence and the CI cited as [-0.25, +0.42]. The rejection of mu about 1 would weaken to "uninformative" if logFC noise within TF were shown to be near sd 0.6 rather than 0.3 (reliability under 0.3; needs replicate-level data knockTF does not ship), or if the five high-leverage TFs (TP53, NR2F2, SP1, MITF, STAT3) were shown to be special cases (e.g. mutant-p53 lines where knockdown has no downstream effect): without them the slope is +0.50. It would strengthen to "no dose-response" only from a dose-resolved set with m spanning 0 to -3 within TF (Replogle 2022 guide-level efficiency, X-Atlas/Orion) giving a within-TF slope CI inside about +/- 0.2.
Severity: high — decides README "Where this points". Citable from F-018: all numbers (upper CI end as +0.42); "F-015's EB-slope as designed (through origin, knockTF-calibrated, mu about 1) is dead"; "knockTF cannot calibrate the abundance term". Not citable: "the abundance component is a constant offset", "three independent readings agree", "no dose-response". The decoupling direction itself is untested, not refuted: it needs a calibration set with own logFC between 0 and -1 and real within-TF range, which knockTF as shipped by decoupler excludes by construction.

## F-019 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: Supersedes F-014 part (3) only (F-014 is not edited); F-014 (1), (2), (4) stand as V-014 left them. (a) Under a tie-free matcher the size-matched self-edge comparison is about 0 with the OPPOSITE sign to F-014's: matching each of the 59 no-self-edge TFs to ALL self-edge TFs at the minimum |log10 target count| distance and averaging their hit indicators (42% of the 59 have 2-3 tied nearest neighbours; 36 distinct self-edge TFs carry weight against 28 under np.argmin; median gap x1.03, max x1.29) gives matched self-edge 0.203 vs no-self-edge 0.186, difference +0.017, where F-014's np.argmin gave -0.017. The sign of F-014's number was decided by alphabetical tie-breaking (V-014 (c)), not by the data. (b) A bootstrap that resamples the 59 no-self-edge and 96 self-edge TFs independently and RE-MATCHES inside each draw (numpy, seed 0, 5000 draws) gives: matched difference +0.017, 95% CI [-0.169, +0.233] (F-014's pairs-only CI was [-0.153, +0.119], width 0.271 against 0.403); unmatched difference +0.126, CI [-0.011, +0.260]; unmatched minus matched +0.111, CI [-0.078, +0.271], P(<= 0) = 0.12; P(a matched draw reaches the unmatched 0.126) = 0.14. The matched CI contains BOTH 0 and the unmatched gap. So the weakened claim, which is what may be cited: the matched difference is about 0 under every tie-free estimator tried here and in V-014 (-0.02 to +0.05 over 13), it sits below the unmatched 0.126 every time, and knockTF CANNOT settle whether regulon size explains the residual self-edge gap -- 59 no-self-edge TFs with 11 hits do not have the resolution. F-014's "matching on size removes the gap" is withdrawn; it is not established and must not be cited, and neither is its opposite. (c) F-002's stratification at the EXPERIMENT-level tercile cut points (48.3 / 110 targets; 0.125 / 0.197 max Jaccard), which is the apples-to-apples comparison F-014 (2) did not print: TF-level miss by size 0.736 (n=72) / 0.565 (n=46) / 0.541 (n=37) against experiment-level 0.677 (n=93) / 0.495 (n=95) / 0.473 (n=91), low minus high +0.196 TF vs +0.205 experiment, i.e. the SAME size gradient at both levels with every TF-level bin 5-7 points higher; by max Jaccard 0.694 (n=62) / 0.700 (n=50) / 0.488 (n=43) against 0.573 / 0.600 / 0.458, still non-monotonic with the HIGH-overlap tercile missing least, so V-002's flip condition is not met at common cut points either. F-014's "gets stronger for size" was a cut-point artefact: its +0.330 low-minus-high uses TF-level cut points 27 / 90.7, a different and smaller low stratum (median 11 targets vs 16). (d) Median convention, now stated in the script: pandas' median averages the two middle values, so each of the 26 two-experiment TFs must have BOTH experiments hit. Under the lower median instead, ULM is 0.452 (70/155, top-5% 0.316), no self-edge 0.297 (46/155, 0.213) and masked 0.297 (46/155, 0.219), against 56 / 41 / 41 under the averaging median. The level moves 14 TFs, the self-edge DROP is 24 TFs under the lower median and 15 under the averaging one; cite the drop as 10-16 points, never as "9-12 whichever rule is used" (F-014's wording, corrected by V-014 (a)).
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python tflevel.py` (about 1 min 20 s; unchanged imports, so F-001/F-002/F-004/F-006/F-010's tables still print above). New printed blocks (5) tie-averaged match, (6) two-pool bootstrap, (7) stratification at experiment-level cut points, (8) lower-median collapse, all APPENDED after blocks (1)-(4), whose stdout is byte-identical to a capture taken before the edit (diff of the first 212 lines, empty). Predictions were stated in the docstring first, all four from V-014, and all four hit: (5) +0.017 predicted and observed; (6) matched CI [-0.169, +0.233] predicted and observed to three decimals, unmatched +0.126 CI [-0.011, +0.260], unmatched minus matched predicted +0.109 [-0.079, +0.271] and observed +0.111 [-0.078, +0.271] (V-014 used 5000 draws in a different order); (7) 0.736 / 0.565 / 0.541 with n = 72 / 46 / 37 and overlap 0.694 / 0.700 / 0.488 predicted and observed exactly; (8) 70 / 46 / 46 predicted and observed. No gain over ULM is claimed, so no shuffled network is owed; the own-gene-masked row is in block (1) and is identical to self-edge removal at top-10% (41/155), as F-010 predicts for univariate ULM. The assert now tests the matching instead of the tautology V-014 (e) found: `lo <= d <= hi` is replaced by "every matched self-edge TF really has a self-edge" (both matchers), "max |log10 size gap| < log10(2)" (both matchers), and "tie weights sum to 1 for all 59 rows", alongside the three surviving clauses (155 rows, selfedge == gapcheck fractions to 1e-12, self-edge removal leaves no-self-edge TFs untouched).
Source: analysis/tflevel.py
Confidence: high for (a), (c), (d) -- deterministic, and (c)/(d) are recomputations of published numbers at a stated convention. High for the NEGATIVE part of (b) (the CI is wide and contains both endpoints); medium for the point estimate +0.017 itself: 36 of 96 self-edge TFs carry all the weight, the Kish effective n on the self-edge side is about 15 (V-014), and matching on target count leaves curation depth and TF family uncontrolled. The bootstrap is a percentile bootstrap of a matched estimator on 11 hits in 59 TFs, so its coverage is itself approximate; do not read the 0.14 and 0.12 tail probabilities as tests. What is NOT established either way: whether a self-edge TF stripped of its self-edge behaves like an ordinary TF of the same size. That question needs a benchmark with more small self-edge TFs than CollecTRI x knockTF has (33 self-edge TFs at <= 60 targets against 50 no-self-edge ones).
Depends on: F-002, F-010, F-014

A logfc-v arm with the regulon floor max(memb-3, 1) instead of max(memb-3, 0), which stops genes in NO regulon from being eligible when the TF's gene sits in <= 3, scores 0.444 / 0.566 / 0.300, identical to logfc to three decimals; its scored picks are median -1.420 with 87% at |logFC| >= 1. So F-012's conclusion is unchanged on the scored set and under both variants: an equally large, equally negative, co-regulated logFC costs propagation 0.0-0.3 points where the perturbed TF's own value costs 29 (0.566 -> 0.276), and ULM 0.8-1.5 points where the TF's own value costs 11 (0.452 -> 0.344). (c) Housekeeping: 3 of 1164 coreg picks land on an exactly-zero cell in the original arm (one scored experiment, where the mask is a no-op); the variant arms make that impossible by construction, and the assert now requires it.

New printed blocks appended after the existing ones -- the scored-only masked-gene table with the shared-regulator and no-regulator counts, then the variant arm table with its own scored masked-gene rows -- and the first 73 lines of stdout are byte-identical to a capture taken before the edit (diff empty), so F-012 and F-007 stay reproducible as written. Confidence: high -- deterministic picks, decoupler's own ULM, the same evaluate() as F-001/F-004/F-007/F-012, and the two variants bound the picker's freedom. The interpretive limit is F-012's and V-007's, unchanged: no single-gene control can reproduce the algebra, since in "up sum" propagation the perturbed TF's own value enters its own score once per upstream regulator with the same sign (about 21 terms) while a co-regulated gene's terms carry mixed signs; and the control says the loss is that ONE value, not whether that value is an abundance artifact or legitimate autoregulatory signal for the 71% of TFs with a self-edge (F-010, F-011). The 19 scored experiments coreg-v skips are not scored under a different arm, so (b) compares 260 masked scored rows against 279; that is why coreg-v's ULM is 0.004 HIGHER, not evidence about the fill.
