## F-021 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: CollecTRI's signs carry a little information for masked ULM and its default-activation fills carry none that can be recovered by deleting or flipping them; and V-017's post hoc "repressor-majority TFs rank worse" lead fails its own pre-stated primary test while passing the secondary one. All numbers are own-gene-masked ULM (the perturbed TF's gene zeroed in its own row), decoupler's ULM kernel, and every intervention that removes edges is RE-PRUNED to tmin=5, which V-017's scratch probe did not do. Baseline masked ULM 96/279 top-10%, 78/279 top-5%. PART A (variant: top-10% of 279 counting unscoreable as misses; gained/lost and exact McNemar and TF-cluster bootstrap 95% CI on the paired common scoreable set): (1) all weights set to +1, signs erased: 81/279, top-5% 55/279, 16 gained / 31 lost, McNemar p = 0.040, difference -0.054 with CI [-0.119, +0.010] (predicted 81/279 and 31/16, exact). (2) default-activation edges removed: 86/279 = 86/253, 26 experiments of 21 TFs become unscoreable (1 of them a masked hit), 13 gained / 22 lost, p = 0.175, -0.036 [-0.094, +0.022] (predicted 89/279; re-pruning is the difference, V-017's un-re-pruned run lost only 5 experiments). (3) default edges set to -1 for the 197 TFs whose PMID-signed edges are majority repressing (3207 edges; 316 such TFs in all): 96/279, 7 gained / 7 lost, p = 1.00, +0.000 [-0.025, +0.023] (predicted 96/279 and 7/7, exact). (4) those TFs' default edges removed instead: 98/279 = 98/271, 8 unscoreable (none a hit), 5 gained / 3 lost, p = 0.73, +0.007 [-0.010, +0.027] (predicted 99/279). (5a) control for (1), a RANDOM 5822 edges (the size of the -1 set) sign-flipped, 5 seeds: mean 88.6/279, range 83-99, top-5% 59-70; sign erasure at 81 is below every seed, so erasing the real signs does cost more than scrambling an equal number of arbitrary ones, but by about 8 hits with a 16-hit seed spread (predicted 85/279, and the predicted direction held). (5b) control for (2), a per-TF-matched random removal of exactly as many edges from each TF's regulon as it has defaults, 5 seeds: mean 79.2/279, range 74-85, same 26 unscoreable experiments; 3 of 5 seeds sit at McNemar p <= 0.003 against masked ULM while removing the actual defaults sits at p = 0.175 (predicted 90/279, i.e. indistinguishable from removing the defaults -- WRONG in the informative direction: removing the default-signed edges costs LESS than removing the same number of random edges from the same regulons, so the default fills are below-average edges, not average ones). No intervention beats 96/279 by more than its bootstrap CI, so no gain is claimed and no degree-preserving shuffled-network row is run. PART B, V-017 (7)'s lead with the test stated first: PMID-majority-repressing TFs that also have default edges score 5/43 masked top-10% (25 TFs, median scored regulon 45, median masked rank fraction 0.372) against 66/154 for PMID-majority-activating TFs with default edges (85 TFs, median 93, 0.142); TFs with no default edges 23/69, tie-or-no-PMID 2/13. PRIMARY (pre-stated) TF-level median masked rank fraction, permutation of the class label among TFs within regulon-size bin, two-sided: 0.385 vs 0.206, difference +0.179, p = 0.158 -- NOT significant, as predicted (predicted 0.39 vs 0.32 and p about 0.14; the 0.32 is reproduced by the per-TF-mean summary, 0.387 vs 0.323, and plain Mann-Whitney gives p = 0.101). SECONDARY experiment-level top-10% rate, same permutation: -0.312, p = 0.0042, significant, so the lead lives in the top-10% cut and not in the ranking. Confounds: knockdown efficiency is NOT one (median own logFC -1.780 vs -1.657, TF-level Mann-Whitney p = 0.79); regulon size is only partly controllable, because no PMID-majority-repressing TF has more than 150 scored targets at all (per bin, repressor vs activator: <=60 2/24 of 19 TFs vs 13/53 of 41; 61-150 3/19 of 6 vs 21/45 of 23; >150 0 TFs vs 32/56 of 21). Mechanism: the median repressor-majority TF's scored regulon is 58.8% default +1, 23.5% PMID -1, 11.7% PMID +1 (activator-majority: 57.1 / 8.3 / 33.3), so in exactly the TFs whose own evidence says "repressor" the activating fill outnumbers the repressing evidence 2.5 to 1 -- but scoring them on PMID-signed edges only moves them 5/35 to 7/35 on the 35 of 43 experiments still scoreable (3 gained, 1 lost), while the whole benchmark goes 95/251 to 88/251. The fill is not what is hurting them.
Evidence: analysis/signprobe.py (41 s, exit 0, imports selfedge.py which reruns propagate.py/controls.py/leak.py). One assert: the masked baseline reproduces leak.py's rank fractions to 1e-9 and 96/78 of 279, every perturbed TF's own gene is zero in the scored matrix, the no-default network contains no default row, and each per-TF-matched random removal dropped exactly 21699 edges. Bootstrap is numpy default_rng(0), 4000 TF-cluster resamples; permutations are 20000, default_rng(0); McNemar is scipy binomtest, exact two-sided. Also done here, V-016/V-017's owed small fixes to analysis/signsource.py: its assert now checks 19396 / 1895 / 21699, and its closing NOTE is corrected (the SELF-EDGE's label tracks regulon size, 78 vs 186 targets; the whole-regulon default fraction does not, TF-level Spearman -0.02, so block 4 is flat rather than size-confounded). signsource.py's stdout is byte-identical apart from those NOTE lines.
Source: T-033, from V-017 owed items (3) and (4). Predictions for all six Part A arms and both Part B tests are in signprobe.py's docstring, written before the first run.
Confidence: (A1) medium -- 15 hits and McNemar p = 0.04, but the TF-cluster CI contains 0 and the random-flip control only separates by about 8 hits against a 16-hit seed spread, so "signs carry information" is supported in direction and weakly in magnitude. (A2/A5b) medium -- the ordering (defaults cost less to remove than random edges) is 4 of 5 seeds and all 5 on the mean, but a 5-seed control is thin. (A3/A4) high -- flipping or deleting the default fills moves masked ULM by 0 to 2 hits with CIs that exclude anything above +0.03, which is the cleanest statement in the entry. (B primary) high for the negative result, the p is 0.158 as predicted. (B secondary) medium -- p = 0.0042 within size bin, but 0 of 25 repressor-majority TFs exceed 150 targets so the largest size bin cannot contribute, and the class was chosen after looking; what is NOT explained is why a top-10%-cut effect that strong leaves the ranking test at p = 0.16. Not separated: mixed-sign small regulons, and knockdown of a repressor giving a weaker target signature.
Depends on: F-016, F-017.

## F-022 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: On the UNFILTERED knockTF a within-TF own-logFC dose-response IS identified, at about half a ULM t-unit per logFC, and it survives every control except a leverage check; but it does not reach zero at zero knockdown, it does not pay off out of sample, and the benchmark does not fall to chance when the TF's own mRNA does not drop. `analysis/fulldose.py` builds the same pipeline as selfedge.py/slopecheck.py (dc.pp.extract / prune tmin=5 / adjmat, every TF's self-edge zeroed, dc.mt.ulm.func, a = RAW score not type_p x a, m = the perturbed TF's own logFC read from the scored matrix) on data/knocktf_full.h5ad = `dc.ds.knocktf(thr_fc=None)`. The two files share an identical 21985-gene var axis and ULM scores rows independently, so the pipelines are provably the same: restricted to logFC < -1 this script reproduces slopecheck.py exactly -- 279 scored, 155 TFs, 176 multi-TF experiments, corr 0.095, OLS slope 0.413 intercept -0.902, within-TF slope 0.079 (asserted). On the full file: 907 experiments, 456 TFs, of which 643 experiments over 263 TFs get a score, 130 TFs have >= 2 scored experiments = 510 experiments; m from the matrix equals obs.logFC exactly (max |diff| 0.0; 2 TFs absent from the matrix use obs). (A1) corr(a, m) = 0.203 (0.095 on the filtered subset); OLS with intercept slope 0.565 95% TF-cluster CI [0.29, 0.85], intercept -0.555 95% CI [-0.91, -0.20], i.e. the intercept EXCLUDES 0 (a pure through-origin dose-response) and also excludes the pure-offset value (mean a on the deep knockdowns is -1.68); the within-TF slope with TF fixed intercepts is 0.480 with a 95% TF-cluster bootstrap CI of [+0.19, +0.74], EXCLUDING 0, and the CI is stable over 5 bootstrap seeds ([+0.17,+0.75] [+0.16,+0.74] [+0.21,+0.76] [+0.19,+0.77] [+0.19,+0.74]); restricted to the 67 TFs with within-TF range > 1 logFC it is 0.469 [+0.17, +0.73] (338 experiments) and to the 31 TFs with range > 2 it is 0.537 [+0.22, +0.84] (191 experiments). (A2) The binned dose-response (mean a, 95% TF-cluster CI) is monotone: m < -2 (n 85) -2.62 [-3.67, -1.67]; -2..-1 (n 194) -1.27 [-2.05, -0.64]; -1..-0.5 (n 143) -0.81 [-1.41, -0.23]; -0.5..0 (n 144) -0.64 [-1.08, -0.18]; m >= 0 (n 77) -0.54 [-1.44, +0.41]. It flattens rather than returning to 0: the last three bins are within 0.3 of each other and the m >= 0 bin is still -0.54, so about half of the deep-knockdown score is a dose-independent offset. (A3) Placebo (own logFC permuted across experiments within regulon-size tercile, 500 draws): within-TF slope real +0.480 against placebo mean +0.001 (5-95% -0.195 to +0.203), real above 100% of placebos -- unlike in F-018, on this design the placebo discriminates. (A4) Leave-one-experiment-out on the 510, everything re-estimated inside the fold: RMSE 3.56 null, 3.29 global through-origin slope x m, 2.81 per-TF mean, 2.77 per-TF mean + pooled within-TF slope x (m_held - mean m_rest), 22.10 per-TF through-origin slope x m (502 folds; undefined when the TF's remaining m are all 0); median |error| 1.49 / 1.54 / 1.55 / 1.47 / 1.55. Adding the slope to the per-TF mean gains 0.035 RMSE with a 95% TF-cluster CI of [-0.112, +0.039], INCLUDING 0. (A5) Own logFC is barely confounded with Knock.Method (eta2 = 0.030, 9 methods) and mildly with Platform (0.145, 64 platforms); adding Knock.Method dummies to the TF fixed effects moves the within-TF slope from +0.480 to +0.531, and the dose bins differ in method composition only in the shallow tail (siRNA 0.73 -> 0.48, shRNA 0.20 -> 0.38 going from m < -2 to m >= 0). Leverage is the one failure: the top 5 TFs by within-TF sum of squares of m (GABPA 65, MAF 33, TP53 28, SOX2 19, FOXM1 14) carry 44% of the total within-TF SS(m), and without them the slope falls to +0.315 with 95% CI [-0.02, +0.65], INCLUDING 0 (474 experiments, 125 TFs). (CONTROL) Degree- and sign-mix-preserving TF-gene shuffle (each TF keeps its count of +1 and -1 targets, targets redrawn at random from the measured genes other than its own, 10 seeds): binned mean a becomes +0.02 / +0.00 / -0.01 / +0.01 / -0.05 (per-bin seed ranges within [-0.22, +0.17]) and the within-TF slope becomes -0.020 [-0.154, +0.056], so both the level and the slope are regulon-specific, not a global "deep knockdowns disturb everything" artefact. (B) Benchmark by dose bin, rank of the true TF among 766 scored TFs. Unmasked ULM top-10% 0.588 (50/85) / 0.392 (76/194) / 0.315 (45/143) / 0.250 (36/144) / 0.299 (23/77) over the five bins, 0.358 overall; own-gene-MASKED ULM 0.471 (40/85) / 0.289 (56/194) / 0.280 (40/143) / 0.229 (33/144) / 0.299 (23/77), 0.299 overall, top-5% 0.388 / 0.232 / 0.189 / 0.160 / 0.208, masked vs unmasked paired better 0.15 worse 0.53 tied 0.32. The hit rate falls with dose but does NOT go to the 10% chance rate: it floors at 0.23-0.30, and in the m >= 0 bin -- knockdowns that did not lower the TF's mRNA at all -- masked ULM is 0.299 top-10% and 0.208 top-5%, three to four times chance. The masked and unmasked numbers are identical in that bin because 82 of the 140 m >= 0 experiments have logFC exactly 0.0, where masking is a no-op.
Evidence: analysis/fulldose.py, stdout tables (A1)-(A5), (CONTROL), (B). Controls in the same script: the Part A input is the self-edge-dropped score (F-010: self-edge deletion IS own-gene masking for ULM), Part B scores the own-gene-masked matrix the way leak.py does, and the degree-preserving shuffled network is reported for both the binned curve and the within-TF slope. The final assert requires the logFC < -1 subset to reproduce slopecheck.py's six numbers, m to equal obs.logFC, and the shuffle to preserve every source's target count, its +1 count, and to place no self-edge.
Source: T-034, from V-018. All six predictions (within-TF slope 0.45 with CI excluding 0; OLS intercept -0.4; binned means -2.3 / -1.6 / -0.9 / -0.4 / -0.1; mean+slope beating mean by 0.05 RMSE with CI excluding 0; masked ULM by bin 0.36 / 0.33 / 0.22 / 0.15 / 0.11; shuffled slope 0.0 +/- 0.1) were written in fulldose.py's docstring before the first run by the main session. Hits: the within-TF slope (0.480 vs 0.45 predicted, CI excludes 0) and the shuffled-network null (-0.020 vs 0.0 +/- 0.1). Misses: the intercept is -0.555, further from 0 than predicted; the binned curve is right in the first two bins but flattens at -0.6 instead of falling to -0.1, so the predicted return to 0 at m >= 0 FAILS; the out-of-sample gain is 0.035 with a CI including 0, so that prediction FAILS; and the benchmark-by-dose prediction FAILS badly at the shallow end (0.28 / 0.23 / 0.30 observed against 0.22 / 0.15 / 0.11 predicted) -- ULM does not fall to chance when the TF's mRNA did not drop.
Confidence: High that a within-TF dose-response exists in the unfiltered data and is regulon-specific -- the slope is 0.48 with a bootstrap CI excluding 0 that is stable over 5 seeds, the placebo gives 0.001 (100th percentile), the shuffled network gives -0.020 over 10 seeds, and Knock.Method adjustment moves it the wrong way for a confound. Medium on its magnitude: 44% of the identifying variance is 5 TFs and without them the CI covers 0, so "about 0.5" rests on GABPA, MAF, TP53, SOX2 and FOXM1 more than on 130 TFs. High that it is not a pure dose-response through the origin: the OLS intercept CI is [-0.91, -0.20] and the m >= 0 bin sits at -0.54, so a knockdown-offset component of roughly half the deep-knockdown score remains unexplained (possible causes not separated here: off-target effects of the reagent, selection of which experiments get published, protein-level knockdown with unchanged mRNA, and residual own-mRNA leak through OTHER TFs' regulons, which the self-edge drop does not close). High that the slope does not yet pay: LOO RMSE gains 0.035 of 2.81 with a CI including 0, so calibrating it buys nothing measurable on this data. High for the Part B reading, which is the entry's most consequential number: masked ULM at 0.299 top-10% and 0.208 top-5% on experiments whose TF mRNA did not fall is direct evidence that ULM's recovery of the perturbed TF is NOT only abundance detection -- the abundance channel is worth about 0.19 top-10% at m < -2 and about 0 at m >= 0, and a regulon signal three times chance survives underneath. Not controlled: the shuffle preserves each TF's regulon size and sign mix but not each gene's in-degree, so a hub-gene artefact is not excluded; the m >= 0 bin mixes 82 exactly-zero logFC values (masking a no-op) with 58 genuine increases; the bins are unbalanced in Platform (eta2 0.145) and Platform was not added as a fixed effect; and the 264 experiments dropped for having no CollecTRI regulon at tmin=5 are not random with respect to dose.
Depends on: F-010, F-018.

Pool = self-edge TFs with more than 150 targets: both gap clauses go False. argmin's idx permuted, or argmax in place of argmin: the argmin gap clause goes False. So the self-edge and 2x-gap clauses are real. Two limits: the self-edge clause reads the same `has_self` column the pool was filtered on, so it guards the pool definition and not the flag itself (I checked the flag independently: has_self equals sign != 0 from selfedge.py's adjacency on all 155 TFs, and none of the 36 weighted self-edge TFs has sign 0); and "tie weights sum to 1" holds by construction of W and cannot fail short of NaN, the same kind of clause V-014 (e) objected to, harmless beside the others. The assert does not cover the matches made inside bootstrap draws; I measured them: max gap per draw median x1.29, 97.5% x1.80, worst x3.20, 0.2% of 5000 draws contain a pair beyond 2x, so the CI is not being widened by bad matches. (4) Wording. No residue of "matching removes the gap": (b) withdraws it and its opposite, the docstring ends on "NOT 'matching removes the gap'", block (6) prints the "cannot tell" sentence, and Confidence calls only the negative part high. "-0.02 to +0.05 over 13" is the erratum's count. "10-16 points" is 15/155 = 9.7 to 24/155 = 15.5, V-014 (a)'s range rounded. Predictions were numeric, stated first, and mine, so "all four hit" is a reproduction of V-014's scratch and not an independent test; the entry says as much. No gain is claimed, so no shuffled network is owed; the masked row is in block (1).
(5) The unclaimed observation: at common cut points the low-minus-high overlap contrast is +0.205 at TF level and +0.115 at experiment level, and the script prints "gradients differ by +0.090". Scratch v019_overlap.py, v019_x.py, v019_strat.py (TF-cluster bootstrap: 155 TFs resampled with all their experiments, cut points fixed, 10000 draws, seed 0). Each contrast: experiment level +0.115, CI [-0.053, +0.278]; TF level under the script's averaging median +0.205 [+0.013, +0.391]; under V-010's rule (mean over TFs of the TF's miss rate) +0.149 [-0.027, +0.323]; under the lower median +0.123 [-0.072, +0.317]. DIFFERENCE TF minus experiment: averaging median +0.090, CI [-0.023, +0.199], P(<= 0) = 0.06; V-010's rule +0.034 [-0.066, +0.137]; lower median +0.008 [-0.105, +0.120]. For size the same three differences are -0.009 / -0.039 / -0.039, all CIs spanning 0. So the +0.090 splits into +0.034 from giving each TF one vote and +0.056 from how a TF with mixed experiments is called, and the second part is the n=2 convention of F-019 (d) landing unevenly: of the 11 two-experiment TFs that straddle 0.10, 6 sit in the low-overlap bin, 4 in mid, 1 in high; of all 29 mixed TFs the averaging median calls 9 of 10 a miss in the low bin, 6 of 11 in mid, 4 of 8 in high. Relative to each TF's own experiment-level miss rate the rule adds 4.0 TF-misses to the low bin (62 TFs, +6.5 points), 1.4 to mid, 0.4 to high (43 TFs, +0.9 points). Composition in the sense asked about is NOT the cause: STAT3 (10) is in the mid bin and TP53 (8) in the high bin; experiments per TF are 1.55 / 2.00 / 1.93 by bin; leave-one-TF-out moves the +0.090 only within [+0.063, +0.108] (most influential REST, -0.027, then RELA +0.018); dropping all 18 TFs with four or more experiments leaves the TF-level contrast at +0.201 and lifts the experiment-level one to +0.156. The 103 single-experiment TFs, where no convention applies, give 0.644 / 0.759 / 0.483 (n 45 / 29 / 29), +0.161. Cut-point scan (high vs rest, thresholds from the 40th to the 80th TF percentile): TF level +0.09 to +0.18, experiment level +0.04 to +0.10, so the TF excess is not specific to 0.125 / 0.197 either; it follows the collapse rule. Partial Spearman of rank fraction with max Jaccard given size: TF level -0.016 (averaging median; TF bootstrap CI [-0.178, +0.148]), +0.036 (lower median), -0.039 for the miss indicator; experiment level +0.057 (TF-cluster CI [-0.111, +0.237]); TF minus experiment -0.073, CI [-0.188, +0.043]. Size-stratified low-minus-high (three size strata at the experiment cuts, Mantel-Haenszel weights): +0.207 [+0.001, +0.409] under the averaging median (lower bound is -0.002 on another seed), +0.085 [-0.125, +0.299] lower median, +0.141 [-0.049, +0.338] V-010's rule, +0.050 [-0.117, +0.256] experiment-weighted. Reading: the larger TF-level overlap contrast is real arithmetic but it is a property of one collapse convention; its difference from the experiment level has a CI that contains 0 and it disappears (+0.008) under the other median. V-002's standing conclusion is UNCHANGED. "Overlap does not hurt" is if anything firmer: no collapse rule, cut point or size adjustment gives a miss rate that rises with max Jaccard, and V-002's flip condition is not met. "High overlap helps" is still not supported: one of four collapse rules has a CI that excludes 0, by 0.013, chosen after the fact among rules and cut points; the low and mid bins are equal (0.694 / 0.700), so there is no gradient, only a high-tercile-versus-rest contrast (+0.208 [+0.035, +0.384] averaging median, +0.147 [-0.026, +0.328] lower median); and every monotone test is null (Spearman -0.083, p = 0.30; partial given size -0.016).
Would flip if: a rerun printed different numbers, or the first 212 lines differed from the pre-edit capture. The matching claim would move from "cannot settle" only on the evidence V-014 names (a CI of unmatched-minus-matched that excludes 0; at [-0.078, +0.271] it does not). On the overlap observation: I would call "high overlap helps at TF level" supported if the high-versus-rest contrast excluded 0 under BOTH median conventions and after size stratification, or if the partial Spearman given size had a CI below 0; today it holds under one convention only and the partial is -0.016. I would call the TF-versus-experiment difference real if its TF-cluster CI excluded 0 under a convention that does not depend on how n=2 TFs are called; under the two that do not (V-010's rule, lower median) it is +0.034 and +0.008.
Severity: medium — this is now the entry to cite for F-014 (3), and it removes the last support for "a self-edge TF without its self-edge is an ordinary TF of its size" as an established statement (it remains a consistent point estimate, about 0 with roughly +/-0.2). The overlap note matters because block (7) prints "gradients differ by +0.090" with no caveat and the next reader will be tempted to cite it; it should carry the words "averaging-median convention; +0.008 under the lower median". Owed, not blocking: print the lower-median overlap and size contrasts next to block (7)'s, and drop or replace the "tie weights sum to 1" clause.

## V-020 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — every number reproduces, the first 73 lines of stdout are unchanged, and the new assert clause can fail (it is False on the original coreg picks). One sentence in Confidence is wrong as well as unclear and must not be quoted: the 19 scored experiments coreg-v skips ARE scored, on their unmasked rows, inside the same 279; and the +0.004 IS the fill's effect, exactly one experiment (NFYB, DataSet_01_238)
Claim checked: F-020 — F-012's descriptive numbers restated on the 279 scored experiments (coreg median -1.445, 65% with |logFC| >= 1, 6.32 shared regulators; logfc -1.423, 87%; own -1.739; any |0.062| / 35% zero; matched |0.134| / 15%), the 5.1-vs-5.9 gap attributed to pooling plus the 53 no-regulator experiments (19 scored), and two variant arms (coreg-v: skip the no-regulator experiments and step past zero cells; logfc-v: regulon floor 1) that score within 0.004 of the arms they copy, so F-012's conclusion does not rest on the picker's two judgment calls.
How verified: (1) Numbers. Reran `cd analysis && uv run python coregmask.py` (1 min 4 s, exit 0, assert passes). Scored-only table: any 1395 picks 0.000 / 0.062 / 0.35 / 0.04; matched 1395, 0.000 / 0.134 / 0.15 / 0.07; coreg 837, -1.445 / 1.445 / 0.00 / 0.65; logfc 837, -1.423 / 1.423 / 0.00 / 0.87; own median -1.739; coreg shared regulators 6.32 scored, 5.09 over 388, median regulators of the TF gene 12; no CollecTRI regulator in 53 of 388, 19 scored. Variant table: coreg-v ulm 0.441 top5 0.362, up sum a=0.5 0.563 / 0.459 better 0.595 worse 0.358, SHUF 0.292 / 0.184, masked 335, skipped 53, deltas 0.004 / 0.000 / -0.000; logfc-v 0.444 / 0.358, 0.566 / 0.473 better 0.609 worse 0.355, SHUF 0.300 / 0.189, masked 388, skipped 0, deltas 0.000; scored picks coreg-v 260, -1.467, 0.67; logfc-v 279, -1.420, 0.87; largest delta 0.004. Every number in F-020 matches, and all equal V-012's scratch values where V-012 gave one (-1.445 / 65% / 6.3, -1.423 / 87%, -1.74, |0.062| / 35.0%, |0.134| / 15.1%, 53 / 19). "0.0-0.3 points" and "0.8-1.5 points" follow from the printed rates (0.566 vs 0.563-0.566; 0.452 vs 0.437-0.444). (2) Nothing old moved. `head -73` of my stdout is identical to `head -73` of three pre-edit captures in the session scratchpad (coreg.log 12:29, coregmask_before.txt 12:47, and my own V-012 capture coregmask_skeptic.log 12:39); F-012's and F-007's tables, the 388-pooled masked-gene table, the 2.05 / 2.69 line and the 5.1 line all reprint as V-012 recorded them. The reference tables from leak.py and controls.py still print above, so both convention controls are in the output; no gain is claimed. (3) Extended assert. Clauses added: all(vzeroed) (every variant pick is 0 in the scored matrix and every perturbed TF's own cell equals the original), (V.tf != V.gene).all(), (V.lfc != 0).all(), with V.lfc read from X0 before masking. The nonzero clause is real: evaluated on the ORIGINAL arms' picks it is False for coreg (3 zeros of 1164, the one scored experiment V-012 found) and True for logfc, so it would have failed before pick_v's `row[order] != 0` filter existed and fails again if the filter is removed. V has 335 + 388 rows (260 + 279 scored), 0 with lfc == 0. Not asserted, and reasonable to leave: the skip count of 53. (4) What the variants actually change (scratch v020_audit.py: imports coregmask, rebuilds each arm's matrix from its pick table, reruns decoupler's ULM and the script's own propagate/evaluate). Hits of 279: unmasked 126 ULM / 158 propagated; coreg 122 / 157; coreg-v 123 / 157; logfc 124 / 158; logfc-v 124 / 158. coreg vs coreg-v picks differ in 54 experiments: the 53 skipped, and one changed pick (DataSet_03_262, STAT3, the zero-cell no-op, now a real mask; no hit changes). logfc vs logfc-v picks differ in 71 of 388 experiments, 28 scored, exactly the 28 scored picks V-012 found in no regulon (logfc-v: 0 such picks); rank fractions are identical on 269 of 279 rows and no hit flips, which is what "a gene in no regulon reaches a score only as one more y value" predicts.
(5) The flagged sentence. "The 19 scored experiments coreg-v skips are not scored under a different arm, so (b) compares 260 masked scored rows against 279; that is why coreg-v's ULM is 0.004 HIGHER, not evidence about the fill." Correct reading: evaluate() runs over all 279 scored experiments for every arm, so BOTH 0.437 and 0.441 are rates over 279. In coreg-v the 19 skipped scored experiments stay in the denominator with their rows left UNMASKED: their rank fractions equal the unmasked run's on all 19 (checked, exact equality). So coreg-v is 260 masked rows plus 19 unmasked rows, against coreg's 260 masked rows plus 19 filler-masked rows. On the 260 rows both arms mask, they are identical: 117 ULM hits each (0.450, against 120 = 0.462 unmasked on the same rows), propagated 0.592 each (unmasked 0.596). The whole 0.004 sits in the 19: unmasked 6 hits, coreg-v 6, coreg with its filler 5. The one experiment is DataSet_01_238 (NFYB): the filler pick, a gene sharing no regulator with NFYB, moves NFYB from rank fraction 0.0875 to 0.2141. So the second half of the sentence is backwards: the +0.004 is precisely the fill's effect, one hit in 279, and it is the only effect the fill has (propagated hits in the 19 are 3 / 3 / 3). "Neither fill matters" stands, and is better stated with these numbers: the filler costs one ULM hit and nothing else; the zero-cell step and the regulon floor cost none. The like-for-like cost of the coreg mask, on the 260 scored rows where it is a real co-regulated pick, is 120 -> 117 ULM hits (1.2 points) and 0.596 -> 0.592 propagated (0.4 points); the entry's "0.8-1.5 points" for ULM includes the filler's 0.4 at its upper end.
Would flip if: a rerun printed different numbers or the first 73 lines changed; or a variant moved a rate by more than the entry's own 0.01 failure line (max observed 0.004 = one experiment). F-012's conclusion would be in question only on the evidence V-012 names (a sign-coherent, heavily co-regulated single-gene mask at or beyond the TF's own logFC that drops propagated top-10% by more than a few points); neither variant is that, and the entry does not say it is.
Severity: low — a restatement on the right denominator plus two robustness arms; nothing downstream changes. When citing, use the scored-set numbers from this entry (-1.445 / 65% / 6.32; -1.423 / 87%; own -1.739), and replace the Confidence sentence with: "coreg-v leaves the 19 no-regulator scored experiments unmasked inside the same 279; the arms are identical on the other 260 (117 hits each), and the 0.004 is one experiment (NFYB) where coreg's filler gene cost a hit."

## V-021 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: split — every number reproduces. CONFIRMED: the Part A table, A3/A4 (replacing or deleting the default fills of the 197 repressor-majority TFs moves masked ULM by 0 to 2 hits), the Part B primary NEGATIVE result, and the signsource.py assert. WEAKENED: the unpredicted headline "default fills are below-average edges" holds in direction only (50 seeds: random removal mean 78.4, sd 5.5, range 70-104; 86 is matched or beaten by 4 of 50 seeds, Monte Carlo one-sided p about 0.10) and is an EVIDENCE-DEPTH fact with sign_decision as a proxy (70% of default edges are ExTRI-only text-mined; removing each TF's k LEAST-SUPPORTED edges without looking at sign_decision costs even less, mean 89.7). REFUTED: "sign erasure at 81 is below every seed" (50 seeds of random flips: mean 86.5, range 74-99, 8 of 50 at or below 81). REFUTED as a reading, and the Claim's first sentence must not be quoted: "default-activation fills carry none" / "flipping the default fills is a 0-hit no-op" (T-031). Flipping ALL 21699 defaults to -1 takes MASKED ULM from 96 to 62; the default +1 is the most informative sign decision in the network, what is null is swapping it for the TF's PMID-majority sign on 15% of the default edges
Claim checked: F-021 — on own-gene-masked ULM (96/279 top-10%), (A) erasing all signs costs 15 hits, deleting or re-signing default-activation edges gains nothing, deleting the defaults costs less than deleting as many random edges per TF; (B) V-017's post hoc "PMID-majority-repressing TFs rank worse" fails a pre-stated TF-level rank test (p = 0.158) and passes an experiment-level top-10% test (p = 0.0042).
How verified: (1) `cd analysis && uv run python signprobe.py`, 45 s, exit 0. Every Part A and Part B number in the entry matches stdout to the printed digit: 96/78; sign-erased 81, top-5% 55, 16/31, p 0.040, [-0.119, +0.010]; no-default 86/253, 26 unscoreable over 21 TFs, 1 a hit, 13/22, p 0.175; 316/197/3207; flip 96, 7/7, [-0.025, +0.023]; drop-rep 98/271, 8 unscoreable, 0 hits, 5/3, p 0.727; rand-flip 83/87/86/99/88 mean 88.6; rand-drop 76/74/84/85/77 mean 79.2, all 26 unscoreable; Part B 5/43, 66/154, 23/69, 2/13; 0.385 vs 0.206, p 0.1582; -0.312, p 0.0042; -1.780 vs -1.657, MW 0.792; MW 0.101; bins 2/24 (19), 13/53 (41), 3/19 (6), 21/45 (23), 0 vs 32/56 (21); composition 0.588/0.235/0.117 vs 0.571/0.083/0.333; PMID-only 5/35 -> 7/35 (3/1), ALL 95/251 -> 88/251. `uv run python signsource.py` exit 0; the assert at lines 166-171 now contains `== [19396, 1895, 21699]`; its blocks (5), (6), (3), (4) print F-017's numbers unchanged. "stdout is byte-identical apart from the NOTE" is UNVERIFIABLE: the repo has no commit and no saved prior output to diff against. Leftover: block (4)'s header still says "(size is confounded; bins given)", which the corrected NOTE two lines below contradicts. signprobe.py's docstring says "Runtime ~4 min" (it is 45 s) and its 5a prediction sentence contradicts itself ("random flips hurt LESS than sign erasure is FALSE -- 85 < 81 ... i.e. random flips hurt slightly LESS"); the committed number 85 is unambiguous and is what I scored. All scratch below ran from analysis/ with scripts and outputs in the session scratchpad only; seeds 0-4 of my loops reproduce the script's five values exactly. (2a) per-TF-matched random removal, 50 seeds (default_rng 200-249): sorted top-10%/279 = 70 70 71 72 73 73 74 74 74 75 75 75 75 75 75 76 76 76 76 77 77 77 77 77 77 78 78 78 79 79 79 79 79 80 80 80 81 81 81 81 82 82 83 84 84 85 86 86 86 104; mean 78.4, sd 5.5, median 78. Removing the actual defaults (86) is tied by 3 seeds and beaten by 1 (104, which is ABOVE the intact network's 96: halving every regulon at random can gain 8 hits, which is the noise floor for every row of this table). Paired, no-default-only hits average 22.6 vs random-only 15.1; 7 of 50 seeds separate at McNemar p < 0.05. "3 of 5 seeds at p <= 0.003 against masked ULM" compares each arm with the baseline, not the two arms with each other. (2b) dilution: the random draw is from the whole regulon, so 66.2% of the edges it removes ARE defaults; the contrast is really "100% defaults" vs "two-thirds defaults", conservative by design, not wrong. The undiluted version is only possible where a TF has at least as many non-default edges as defaults: 210 of 886 TFs, 3232 of 21699 default edges, 64 of the 210 default-carrying experiments. There: defaults removed 96/279 (22/64 on those experiments, baseline 21/64); whole-regulon random 50 seeds mean 94.1, sd 2.4, range 89-98; NON-DEFAULT-only random mean 94.1, sd 1.9, range 89-98 (18.9/64). 96 is reached by 36% and 26% of seeds. So where the clean test can be run it shows a 2-hit gap inside a 2-hit sd, and restricting the draw to non-default edges changes nothing; the whole 7.6-hit headline lives in the 146 experiments of TFs that are MORE than half default, where "remove the defaults" means "keep only the PMID/regulon minority" and no undiluted control exists. (2c) support, all edges: default activation 83.3% single-reference, 85.1% single-resource, mean 1.36 refs; PMID 65.6% / 37.4%, mean 2.48 refs. 15137 of 21699 default edges (70%) come from ExTRI alone, and NO ExTRI-only or HTRI-only edge is PMID-signed: "default activation" is to a first approximation "text-mined, one source". Removing each TF's k least-supported edges (fewest references, then fewest resources, random ties, 20 seeds, sign_decision never read): mean 89.7/279, range 85-94, same 26 unscoreable, 77% of the removed edges are defaults; 19 of 20 seeds are at or above 86. So the "cheap to remove" result is reproduced, slightly more strongly, by evidence depth alone. The two cannot be separated on this network (in the 210-TF subset, least-supported non-default removal 95.2 vs random non-default 94.1 vs defaults 96, all inside the noise). Also: removing an edge tests whether the EDGE is worth having, not whether its SIGN is right; A2/A5b say nothing about signs. 96.2% of default-edge targets are measured vs 97.5% of PMID, so measurement is not the explanation. (2d) confirmed: the unscoreable set is the same 26 experiments in 50 of 50 seeds (and the same 4 in every restricted arm). It is "by construction" only for raw edge counts (n - k is equal in both arms); measured-target counts could differ and in 50 seeds never did for a perturbed TF. The paired comparison is fair. (3) random flip of 5822 edges, 50 seeds (default_rng 100-149): sorted 74 75 80 80 80 80 81 81 82 82 82 83 83 83 83 83 83 84 84 85 85 85 86 86 86 86 86 87 87 87 87 88 88 88 88 88 88 89 89 90 90 92 93 94 95 96 96 97 99 99; mean 86.5, sd 5.6. 8 of 50 (16%) are at or below 81; sign erasure sits about 1 sd below the mean. The supported statement is "erasing the real signs costs about as much as flipping 13.5% of edges at random", not "more"; the 5-seed mean of 88.6 was a high draw (seed 3's 99 is the joint maximum of 50). That control is also the wrong one for "signs carry information", since it changes which edges are wrong, not whether the real labelling beats an arbitrary one. The direct control: permute the weights WITHIN each TF (each TF keeps its number of -1 edges), 50 seeds: sorted 74 75 76 77 77 78 78 78 79 79 79 79 79 79 79 80 80 80 81 81 81 81 81 81 82 83 83 83 84 84 84 84 86 86 86 86 86 86 87 87 87 87 87 87 88 88 88 89 91 94; mean 82.7, sd 4.4, 0 of 50 reach 96. The real placement of the -1 labels beats a random placement by about 13 hits in 50 of 50 draws, and erasing the signs (81) is no better than scrambling them (82.7). THAT is the evidence that signs carry information, and it is firmer than F-021's "medium": p < 0.02 over label draws, while the TF-sampling uncertainty stays what the entry says (McNemar 0.040, cluster CI containing 0). (4) Part B code read and re-run with an independent seed. The permutation is at TF level within the three size bins for BOTH tests (perm_within_bin permutes isR over the 110 two-class TFs inside each bin; the secondary maps the permuted TF labels back to experiments); my rerun gives p = 0.152 and 0.0049. The >150 bin has 0 repressor TFs, so its labels never move and it adds no null variance, but its 21 activator TFs (32/56 hits) sit in the activator pool in the observed statistic AND in every permutation: the nulls are centred at -0.126 (secondary) and +0.053 (primary), not 0. The p-values are valid; the quoted EFFECT SIZES are not size-adjusted. On the <=150 bins alone: secondary 5/43 vs 34/98, -0.231, p = 0.0063; primary +0.138, p = 0.247. Six finer size bins: -0.231, p = 0.0053; +0.138, p = 0.271. Within-bin median sizes are equal (26 vs 25; 89.5 vs 89), so residual size confounding inside bins is not the story. With each TF one vote (mean per-TF hit rate, <=150, six bins): -0.187, p = 0.057; 12 of the 43 repressor experiments are NR2F2 and YBX1 (6 each, 1 hit each). Family / source: NOT concentrated. The 25 TFs spread over zinc-coordinating (7 TFs, 14 exps, 3 hits), helix-turn-helix (9, 10, 1), basic domains (5, 9, 0), and YBX1 alone in beta-barrel; 35 GEO / 8 ENCODE against 139 / 15; 31 distinct biosamples (K562 5, HUVEC 4), 37 distinct GEO series; siRNA 29 of 43 vs 105 of 154. The one asymmetry is on the other side: the immunoglobulin-fold superclass (STAT/NFKB/TP53/RUNX type, 8 TFs, 30 exps, 16 hits) has no repressor-majority member; without it the activator rate is 50/124 = 0.40, still 3.5 times 0.116. (5) T-031, scratch only, same kernel and pruning as signprobe.py, top-10%/279 unmasked | masked: real network 126 | 96; ALL defaults set to -1: 61 | 62; defaults -1 but the 343 default self-edges kept +1: 86 | 62; only the default self-edges set to -1: 96 | 97; defaults removed 93 | 86; all self-edges removed, real signs 96 | 98, defaults -1: 62 | 62. So default-activating beats default-repressing by 65 hits unmasked and by 34 masked: about half of the unmasked win (31 of 65; 25-30 of it the self-edge, the rest the own gene's other routes) is the leak T-031 asked about, and the other half survives masking. F-021's arm 3 touches 3207 of 21699 default edges (15%) for the TFs of 43 experiments, masked only; "a 0-hit no-op" describes that arm and nothing wider.
What may be cited: (i) A3/A4 as worded in the entry's Evidence, never as "the default fills carry no information"; (ii) "real signs beat within-TF-permuted signs by about 13 hits; erasing them is as bad as scrambling them" once the arm is committed; (iii) Part B as "pre-stated primary test failed (p = 0.158; 0.25 on the size bins that contain both classes). Secondary, exploratory, class chosen after looking: 5/43 vs 34/98 within comparable sizes, p = 0.006 by experiment, 0.057 with one vote per TF" — a lead for a replication set, not a finding, and -0.312 / 66/154 must not be quoted as the effect; (iv) the evidence-depth description of default edges (83% single-reference, 70% ExTRI-only). NOT citable: "default fills are below-average edges" as a statement about sign provenance, and "below every seed".
Would flip if: headline to confirmed — a committed 50-seed (or more) control puts 86 outside the central 95% of per-TF-matched random removal AND an arm that holds evidence depth fixed (random removal among single-reference, single-resource NON-default edges vs defaults of the same support) still shows defaults cheaper; to refuted — the same run puts 86 inside the middle half. Part B secondary to citable — the same class definition, fixed now, reproduces on an independent perturbation set (knocktf_full's extra experiments or another resource) at the TF-vote level. T-031 reading — a committed all-defaults-flipped arm that does NOT show the 96 -> 62 masked drop would refute my scratch number and restore the experimentalist's reading.
Severity: medium — nothing built depends on A5b yet, but two wrong sentences were about to propagate: "default fills carry no information" into T-031 (it would have closed a lead whose real answer is "about half leak, half real, and default-activating is justified even masked"), and "signs carry little information, 8 hits against a 16-hit spread" into any decision on whether a sign-aware method is worth building (the within-TF permutation says the real sign placement is worth about 13 of masked ULM's 96 hits).
Owed: signprobe.py — 50 seeds for 5a/5b or the claim reworded to the 50-seed numbers above; add the within-TF sign-permutation arm, the least-supported-k arm, and the all-defaults-to--1 arm masked and unmasked with a stated prediction (that arm is T-031's answer and needs the experimentalist's commit, not my scratch); report Part B's effect on the <=150 bins; fix the docstring runtime and the 5a sentence. signsource.py — block (4) header "(size is confounded; bins given)". T-031 stays open for: the paper's own metric and benchmark (t = 337 is not a top-10% count), the 1895 regulon-tier edges whose sign would also change under a different default, and zeroing vs flipping per TF class.

## V-022 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: split — every number reproduces. CONFIRMED: a within-TF own-logFC dose-response exists in the unfiltered knockTF and is regulon-specific (it survives dropping the exact-zero rows, restriction to m < 0, a rank-based estimate, Platform fixed effects, the placebo and the shuffle). Magnitude stays "medium to low": it is a pooled, SS-weighted number, not a per-TF property (equal weight per TF: median 0.27, CI includes 0, 38 of 67 TFs positive). REFUTED as worded: "the curve flattens at -0.54 / a dose-independent offset of about half" and the entry's headline positive claim "masked ULM is 0.299, three times chance, on knockdowns whose TF mRNA did not fall, so recovery is not only abundance detection". The m >= 0 bin is not one thing: of its 77 scored experiments 38 have own logFC exactly 0 = gene NOT MEASURED (dose unknown), 21 have 0 < m <= 0.25 (inside the experiment's noise), and only 18 have a measurable increase (m > 0.25); on those 18 the mean score is +1.01 (not -0.54) and masked ULM is 2/18 = 0.111 against a per-TF base rate of 0.101, i.e. chance, median rank 0.71. NOT ESTABLISHED: "the intercept excludes 0, so it is not a dose-response through the origin" (the interval is right, the inference needs own logFC to be an error-free dose). SURVIVES, and newly controlled: masked ULM beats a per-TF base rate (0.344 vs 0.123 on the filtered 279), so the 34% baseline in CLAUDE.md is not TF-identity bias; but inside multi-TF studies it barely beats the same TF's rate when ANOTHER TF of that study was perturbed (0.277 vs 0.225, CI includes 0)
Claim checked: F-022 — (A) on `dc.ds.knocktf(thr_fc=None)` the perturbed TF's self-edge-dropped ULM score falls about 0.48 t-units per logFC of its own mRNA within TF, regulon-specifically; the curve does not return to 0 at zero knockdown (offset about half); calibrating it gains nothing out of sample; (B) masked ULM stays at 0.23-0.30 top-10% as the dose goes to 0 and is 0.299 where the mRNA "did not fall", read as direct evidence that ULM's recovery is not only abundance detection.
How verified: (1) `cd analysis && uv run python fulldose.py` (21 s wall), assert passes. Every number in the entry matches stdout: 907 / 456 / 643 / 263 / 130 / 510; reproduction block MATCH (279, 155, 176, 0.095, 0.413, -0.902, 0.079); corr 0.203; OLS 0.565 [0.29, 0.85], intercept -0.555 [-0.91, -0.20]; within-TF 0.480 [+0.19, +0.74], range>1 0.469 [+0.17, +0.73] (338 exps, 67 TFs), range>2 0.537 [+0.22, +0.84] (191, 31); five seed CIs as printed; bins -2.62 / -1.27 / -0.81 / -0.64 / -0.54 with n 85 / 194 / 143 / 144 / 77 and the CIs as printed; placebo +0.001 (-0.195 to +0.203), 100%; LOO 3.56 / 3.29 / 2.81 / 2.77 / 22.10 (502 folds), gain -0.035 [-0.112, +0.039]; eta2 0.030 / 0.145; Knock.Method 0.531; leverage 0.315 [-0.02, +0.65] (474 exps, 125 TFs, 44% of SS); shuffle +0.02 / +0.00 / -0.01 / +0.01 / -0.05 and -0.020 [-0.154, +0.056]; Part B unmasked 50/85, 76/194, 45/143, 36/144, 23/77 (0.358) and masked 40/85, 56/194, 40/143, 33/144, 23/77 (0.299), top-5% and paired 0.15 / 0.53 / 0.32 as printed. Everything below is scratch (session scratchpad: setup.py, zeros.py, partA.py, partB.py, samestudy.py; same pipeline, scratch asserts 96/279, 192/643, 230/643), nothing written into the repo. (2) THE EXACT ZEROS. "82 of 140" is over all 907 rows (54 matrix zeros + 28 TFs absent from the matrix). Among the 643 SCORED: 566 have m < 0, 38 have m exactly 0 (36 matrix zeros + the 2 YBX3 experiments whose gene is not in the matrix and whose obs.logFC is 0.0), 39 have m > 0. obs.logFC is exactly 0.0 for all 38, and obs.logFC is never 0 where the matrix is not. Exact 0 is a fill value, not a measurement: the matrix holds 6,997,274 exact zeros against 55,323 values with 0 < |x| < 0.001, a point mass no continuous logFC produces; the 36 experiments have a median 43% of genes at 0 (26% for the other scored experiments); of the 34 that share a Platform with another experiment, in 23 the TF's gene is 0 in EVERY other experiment on that platform and in 6 more (platform "-", BACH1 and NONO) in 83%, against 1.2% for scored experiments whose own value is nonzero; NONO is zero-filled in 64% of all 907 experiments and POU5F1 in all 209 other GPL570 experiments, which is symbol/probe mapping, not non-expression. 5 exceptions (TP53 x3 on GPL10558, YBX1, HOXA1) have the gene measured elsewhere on the platform, so there it is dataset-level filtering; still missing, not "unchanged". knockTF as shipped by decoupler has no expression-level, detection-call or p-value information (14 obs columns, empty var, no layers), so nothing in the file can tell a real small logFC from low-expression noise. The only internal yardstick is the experiment's own logFC spread: in the -0.5..0 bin the own |logFC| is below 1 sd of that experiment's measured genes in 56% of experiments and below the experiment's median |logFC| in 26% (19% and 0% in the -1..-0.5 bin, 1-2% below -1), and for the 39 with m > 0 it is 67% and 46%. So "dose near 0" is largely "dose not resolved". THE m > 0 GROUP: 22 shRNA, 15 siRNA, 1 CRISPRi, 1 CRISPRko; median +0.16; 21 are <= 0.25, 13 are > 0.5, 5 are > 1, and two are GABPA at +9.50 and +9.88 in experiments whose logFC sd is 4.6-4.9 (not a log2 scale like the rest, or a reversed contrast); they look like noise around 0 (21) plus failed or reversed knockdowns (18), not compensation that one could interpret. THREE-GROUP CURVE (mean a, 95% TF-cluster CI; median): m < -2 -2.62; -2..-1 -1.27; -1..-0.5 -0.81; -0.5..0 (n 144) -0.64 [-1.09, -0.19]; m == 0 exactly (n 38, 24 TFs) -0.95 [-2.23, +0.49], median -0.69, which is where an average knockdown of unknown dose sits (mean a over all m < 0 is -1.19); m > 0 (n 39, 33 TFs) -0.15 [-1.51, +1.10], median +0.34, and split further: 0 < m <= 0.25 (n 21) -1.14 [-3.11, +0.60], median +0.13, the mean carried by HNF1A -11.4 and SF1 -8.4 / -7.3; m > 0.25 (n 18, 16 TFs) +1.01 [-0.25, +2.33], median +1.04. The curve does not flatten at -0.54: that number averages unmeasured knockdowns with measured increases, and where the mRNA measurably rose the score is positive, which is what a dose-response predicts and an offset does not. What is left of the offset is the -0.5..0 bin alone: -0.64 at a mean measured m of -0.27, CI excluding 0, in the bin where half the doses are inside the noise. OLS: dropping the 38 zeros, slope 0.585 [0.30, 0.88] intercept -0.509 [-0.91, -0.13]; m < 0 only, 0.574 [0.26, 0.94] and -0.513 [-0.92, -0.10]; so "the intercept CI excludes 0" SURVIVES numerically and the zeros moved it by only 0.05. The inference from it does not: a through-origin truth with classical error in m gives intercept = (1 - lambda) x beta x mean(m), and the observed pair is reproduced exactly by beta 1.0-1.07 with reliability 0.55-0.57, i.e. an error sd of 0.6-0.77 logFC between measured own logFC and effective dose. That is more than probe noise alone (V-018 used 0.3) but not more than mRNA-vs-protein knockdown mismatch can supply, and F-022 itself lists "protein-level knockdown with unchanged mRNA" as a candidate; so "High that it is not through the origin" should read "cannot tell", and note what it implies: the errors-in-variables reading puts the true slope near 1, the value V-018 rejected on the filtered set at 60% power. Restricted to -1 < m < 0, where the method would be applied, nothing is identified: within-TF slope +0.22 [-1.39, +1.72], intercept CI [-1.23, +0.13]. WITHIN-TF SLOPE: 0.457 [+0.15, +0.71] without the zero rows (477 exps, 126 TFs); 0.329 [+0.03, +0.66] on m < 0 only (437, 117); within-TF Spearman of TF-centred a on TF-centred m +0.170 [+0.05, +0.28], +0.168 [+0.04, +0.29] without zeros, +0.152 [+0.04, +0.27] on m < 0: the existence claim survives every version. LEVERAGE without the zero rows is worse than printed: top-5 removed 0.212 [-0.12, +0.54], top-10 removed 0.079 [-0.33, +0.53] (all rows: 0.315 and 0.226 [-0.17, +0.63]). (4) Jackknife over the 130 TFs: leave-one-TF-out slope from +0.398 (SOX2) to +0.519 (MAF), 5-95% 0.463-0.497, jackknife SE 0.140 (0.480 +/- 0.275, agreeing with the bootstrap); most influential SOX2, MAF, GABPA, MITF, BACH1, SMARCA4, POU5F1, FOXM1; no single TF carries it, GABPA's two +9.5 rows included (0.441 without GABPA). Removing the most supportive TFs greedily takes 5 TFs to get under 0.2 (SOX2, GABPA, BACH1, FOXM1, POU5F1) and 11 of 130 to reach 0. Equal weight per TF (per-TF OLS slopes): range > 0.5, 95 TFs, mean +0.49 [+0.02, +1.05], median +0.24 [-0.12, +0.45], 52/95 positive (sign p 0.41); range > 1, 67 TFs, mean +0.30 [-0.13, +0.76], median +0.27 [-0.12, +0.51], 38/67 positive (p 0.33, Wilcoxon 0.25); range > 2, 31 TFs, median +0.38 [-0.01, +0.87], 20/31 (Wilcoxon 0.053). Capping each TF's SS(m) weight at 5 / 2 / 1 gives 0.373 / 0.293 / 0.345. So the pooled slope is real and the typical TF's slope is about 0.3 with a CI that includes 0. Platform fixed effects do NOT explain it away, they raise it: TF+Platform 0.709 [+0.39, +1.03], TF+Method+Platform 0.737 [+0.44, +1.01]; without the zero rows 0.605 [+0.29, +0.88] and 0.607 [+0.29, +0.87]. (3) PART B NULLS, masked ULM top-10%. Three groups: m == 0 exactly 13/38 = 0.342 (top-5% 0.237); 0 < m <= 0.25 8/21 = 0.381; m > 0.25 2/18 = 0.111 (top-5% 1/18), median masked rank 0.711 against 0.28-0.40 in the other groups. For the 39 with m > 0 masking moves 24 ranks better, 1 worse, 14 tied with no hit gained or lost, so the entry's "identical because masking is a no-op" is true of 38 of the 77, not of the bin. (a) fulldose.py's TF-gene shuffle on the masked matrix, 10 seeds: 0.093 / 0.090 / 0.104 / 0.105 for the four negative bins, 0.103 at m == 0, 0.074 at m > 0, 0.091 on the filtered 279, 0.097 overall (seed range 0.082-0.110). (b) In-degree-preserving version: because ULM is univariate, giving TF t the regulon of a size-matched other TF is exactly reading that TF's score in the same experiment, so the null is the top-10% rate of the 20 nearest-size other TFs in the same masked experiment, no seeds needed: 0.131 / 0.127 / 0.106 / 0.142, 0.114 at m == 0, 0.103 at m > 0.25, 0.128 on the 279. (c) Per-TF base rate = how often the same TF is top-10% in the experiments (of all 907) where a DIFFERENT TF was perturbed. Over the 766 TFs it has mean 0.099, sd 0.044, max 0.41 (E2F4 0.41, E2F1 0.39, MYC 0.38, E2F3 0.32, E2F2 0.30, TFDP1 0.28, SP1 0.26), Spearman with regulon size +0.35; for the perturbed TFs it averages 0.123, so chance here is 12%, not 10%. Hit minus base, 95% TF-cluster CI: m < -2 0.471 - 0.121 = +0.350 [+0.23, +0.46]; -2..-1 0.289 - 0.123 = +0.165 [+0.09, +0.24]; -1..-0.5 0.280 - 0.126 = +0.154 [+0.07, +0.24]; -0.5..0 0.229 - 0.125 = +0.105 [+0.02, +0.19]; m == 0 exactly 0.342 - 0.128 = +0.215 [+0.05, +0.37]; 0 < m <= 0.25 0.381 - 0.116 = +0.265 [+0.04, +0.48]; m > 0.25 0.111 - 0.101 = +0.010 [-0.11, +0.18]; F-022's m >= 0 bin 0.299 - 0.118 = +0.181 [+0.06, +0.30]; FILTERED 279 0.344 - 0.123 = +0.221 [+0.15, +0.29] (96 hits against 34.2 expected; top-5% 0.280 - 0.067 = +0.213); all 643 0.299 - 0.123 = +0.175 [+0.13, +0.22]. Excluding the experiment's own study from the base changes nothing (0.122). So the 34% masked baseline is NOT hub-TF identity bias: it is 2.8x its base rate, and "three times chance" for the deep knockdowns stands as "2.8x". (d) New, and the one result here that cuts the other way: 155 scored experiments (101 TFs, 45 studies) come from a study that also perturbed a different TF. There the true TF is top-10% in 0.277 and the SAME TF is top-10% in 0.225 of that study's experiments on a different TF: +0.052, TF-cluster CI [-0.02, +0.13], study-cluster [-0.03, +0.14]; on the 77 of them inside the filtered 279, 0.338 vs 0.277, +0.061 [-0.04, +0.16], while the same TFs' rate in OTHER studies is 0.131. Read the other way (the study's other perturbed TFs scored in this experiment) 0.273 vs 0.191, +0.08 [-0.01, +0.18]. Same cell line in a different study gives 0.151 against 0.127. So most of masked ULM's margin over its base rate in multi-TF studies is shared by whichever TF that study perturbed: shared controls, shared cell context, or designed-in related TFs (mean regulon Jaccard between a TF and its study-mates is 0.066 against 0.024 for random pairs). Scratch lead, not a finding; it is 24% of the scored experiments. (5) "Residual own-mRNA leak through OTHER TFs' regulons" is excluded for a_te by construction, not just by F-010's numbers: ULM is univariate, a_te is computed from column t of the adjacency only, and F-010's other-regulon path exists only for propagation, which adds other TFs' scores. With the self-edge dropped the own value enters a_te only as one of about 22,000 zero-weight background genes: rescoring on the own-gene-masked matrix changes a_te by at most 0.33 (mean -0.004, corr 0.99998) and the bin means by 0.013 / 0.005 / 0.001 / 0.001 / 0 / 0. Strike that candidate. The other three (off-target, publication selection, protein-level knockdown) stay, and "own logFC is a noisy dose" must be added as a fourth, since it needs no offset at all.
Would flip if: (the refuted Part B claim) a set of knockdowns with MEASURED, replicate-supported unchanged or raised own mRNA (needs per-gene SE or detection calls, which this file does not have; knockTF's raw GEO series do) showing masked ULM above its per-TF base rate with a CI excluding 0; on the 18 available the point estimate is +0.01 and the CI reaches +0.18, so about 60-80 such experiments would decide it. (the refuted flattening) the m > 0.25 group holding a negative mean a once the two GABPA rows and any reversed contrasts are checked against GEO; at present it is +1.01, and without GABPA still positive in median. (the confirmed existence) a within-TF Spearman or the m < 0 slope falling inside +/- 0.1 after conditioning on study (Profile.ID) rather than Platform; not run, because 510 experiments over 130 TF and about 300 study dummies leave almost no within-cell variation. (the base-rate result) nothing foreseeable: three different nulls agree at 0.09-0.13. (the same-study lead) would become a finding against the 34% baseline if it holds when study-mates with regulon Jaccard above 0.05 are excluded; would dissolve if the same-study base rate falls to about 0.13 under that exclusion.
Severity: high — this entry sets the direction. What Brandon can build on: a pooled within-TF dose-response exists (rank-robust, regulon-specific, CI excluding 0 in every variant run), masked ULM's 34% is 2.8x a properly computed per-TF base rate, and where own mRNA measurably ROSE the regulon score is positive and the TF is not recovered, all three of which favour abundance-coupled activity rather than argue against it. What he cannot build on: any statement about zero dose (knockTF does not contain a measured zero dose: its zeros are missing values and its small values are inside the noise), the "half is offset" decomposition, or a calibration payoff (LOO gain 0.035 of 2.81, CI includes 0; nothing identified between -1 and 0). CITE AS: (i) existence: "in unfiltered knockTF the perturbed TF's self-edge-dropped ULM score falls with its own logFC within TF: pooled slope 0.46-0.48 (95% TF-cluster CI about [0.15, 0.75]), within-TF Spearman +0.17 [+0.05, +0.28]; absent under a permuted-logFC placebo and a shuffled network; not removed by Knock.Method or Platform fixed effects (which raise it to 0.6-0.7)". (ii) size: "0.2 to 0.7; SS-weighted and carried by about ten TFs (SOX2, GABPA, TP53, FOXM1, BACH1, POU5F1 lead); the median per-TF slope is 0.27 with a CI including 0 and 38 of 67 TFs positive; own logFC is a noisy dose, so the true slope could be near 1". Do not write "about half a t-unit per logFC" without that. (iii) worth building: "not shown: LOO gain 0.035 RMSE of 2.81, CI [-0.112, +0.039]; no identification between -1 and 0". (iv) "not only abundance": may be cited only in the narrow, already-known form "masked ULM recovers the perturbed TF at 0.344 against a per-TF base rate of 0.123 on the filtered 279 (+0.22 [0.15, 0.29]), and at 13/38 = 0.342 on the 38 experiments whose own gene was never measured, so recovery does not depend on reading the TF's own mRNA value"; NOT as "recovery persists where the TF's mRNA did not fall" (2/18 where it measurably rose) and not as "three to four times chance at m >= 0". README "Where this points" may say: the unfiltered set shows a pooled within-TF dose-response of uncertain size and no out-of-sample payoff; it cannot speak to zero dose because 38 of the 77 scored "m >= 0" experiments are unmeasured genes, 21 are within noise and the 18 measured increases score positive and are not recovered; the "140 natural negative controls" sentence should be withdrawn (of the 140, 82 are missing values); judging the direction still needs a set with a measured dose and its error (Replogle guide-level, or knockTF's GEO series reprocessed with per-gene SE). Two wording slips in the entry: "82 of the 140" describes all 907 rows, the scored bin is 38 of 77; and the m >= 0 bin's masked = unmasked equality is not a no-op for the 39 positive rows (24 ranks move, no hit changes).

## V-023 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: refuted
Claim checked: main session readings of Sugimoto 2026 (refs/sugimoto-2026-tfactprofiler.md, NAR 10.1093/nar/gkag897, PMC13575427), entered for verification. Three readings: (1) TF mRNA and TF phosphorylation are uncorrelated (-0.02, 95% CI -0.32 to 0.29), so abundance carries no information about post-translational activity in signalling settings; (2) CollecTRI+ULM tracks phosphorylation-driven activity significantly worse than TFActProfiler; (3) the phosphoproteomics benchmark design is reusable here. Headline verdict is the verdict on reading 1, which is the one that would change direction. Per reading: (1) REFUTED as stated, the paper says something weaker; (2) UNVERIFIABLE as a statistic, the paper reports no test, no n and no effect size; (3) CONFIRMED and sharpened with numbers I computed.
How verified: primary source refs/sugimoto-2026-tfactprofiler.md, cross-checked against the Europe PMC JATS (https://www.ebi.ac.uk/europepmc/webservices/rest/PMC13575427/fullTextXML) because the committed Markdown DROPS the numbered Methods sub-sections (Collection of TF-mRNA priors, Collection of RNA-seq datasets, Cluster-wise regularized regression, Inference algorithms, Performance metrics are all absent from refs/ and present in the XML; refs/jats2md.py loses sub-<sec> bodies -- a conversion bug worth its own task).
  READING 1. The quote is present verbatim, Results, "TFActProfiler captures phosphorylation-driven TF activity dynamics": "Of note, the correlation between TF transcript abundance and TF phosphorylation level was -0.02 (95% CI, -0.32 to 0.29), indicating that there is no significant association between TF mRNA abundance and phosphorylation in this setting." The paper claims "no SIGNIFICANT association ... in this setting"; the reading claims "carries no information", i.e. it converts a failure to reject into an equivalence result, and generalises one cell type to "signalling contexts". Scope, from Methods "Benchmarking of TF activity inference using phosphoproteome data": ONE dataset (public reanalysis of MPXV-infected primary human foreskin fibroblasts, MOI 3, ref [60] = Nat Commun s41467-024-51074-6), FOUR timepoints (0/6/12/24 hpi; phospho log2FC computed at 3 of them, 6/12/24 vs 0), and "26 regulatory phosphosite profiles" -- sites, not TFs; the number of TFs is never stated anywhere in the paper. The paper never states the unit of observation or the n for the -0.02: not whether it is across TFs, across timepoints, or pooled. Back-solving the CI through Fisher z (half-width (atanh(0.29)-atanh(-0.32))/2 = 0.3151, n = (1.96/0.3151)^2+3) gives n ~= 42, which matches neither 26 sites nor 26x3 = 78 site-timepoints, so the unit cannot be recovered from the text. Power: at n = 42 the interval is compatible with r = +-0.3, i.e. abundance explaining up to ~9% of phospho variance. To support "carries no information" one needs an equivalence test, not a null test: 95% CI half-width 0.20 needs n ~= 96, half-width 0.10 needs n ~= 385. So ~400 TF-timepoint observations (roughly 10 datasets of this size, or one phosphoproteome with ~130 directional TF sites) before "no association" is an evidenced claim rather than an underpowered null.
  READING 2. Quote present, same section: "TF activity estimates derived from TFActProfiler showed significantly stronger positive correlations with the phosphoproteomics-based TF activity profiles than those obtained using CollecTRI (Fig. 2D and E)." That is the whole statistic. The Fig. 2E legend reads "Distribution of TF-wise Pearson correlation coefficients between RNA-seq-based TF activity estimates and phosphoproteome-derived TF activity changes ... *P < .05" -- no test named, no n, no effect size, no statement that the comparison is paired per TF ("paired" appears in the paper only for "paired multi-omics data" and "paired white/brown preadipocytes"); the paper has no Statistical analysis section. It is not even determinable from the text whether the asterisk marks TFActProfiler vs CollecTRI or each distribution vs zero (the sentence "again TENDED to show positive correlations" for the time-lag analysis suggests one-sample-vs-zero testing, in which case the headline would be the "difference in significance is not a significant difference" error). Each TF-wise Pearson r is computed over 4 trajectory points (0/6/12/24 hpi, deltas from 0 hpi, so one point is pinned at the origin for both series): r on n = 4 is essentially unbounded in variance. (a) Training/benchmark overlap: TFActProfiler is trained on DISCO, Tabula Sapiens, Human Cell Atlas and ARCHS4 (Methods, "Collection of RNA-seq datasets", XML only). ARCHS4 is described by the authors as "the majority of published bulk and single-cell RNA-seq data from human", so the HepG2/Jurkat KD set (PRJNA1100571) and plausibly RPE1/K562 (PRJNA831566, Replogle) sit inside the training corpus of the headline 1423-experiment benchmark; the paper never checks or mentions this. For the MPXV phospho benchmark the overlap risk is lower (2024 infection time course) but is also never excluded. (b) TF selection: stated rule is only "TFs lacking directional SIGNOR evidence were excluded". Nothing says TFs were required to have a usable regulon in both resources. I reconstructed the candidate set (see below): the 23 CollecTRI (tmin=5) TFs with a directional SIGNOR site measured in this phosphoproteome have CollecTRI regulons of 6, 9, 9, 10, 12, 13, 19, 29, 34, 34, 43, 63, 75, 79, 83, 100, 103, 158, 197, 305, 341, 344, 662 targets, median 63, six of them at or under 15. TFActProfiler averages 2606176/2017 = 1292 targets per TF. So at these particular TFs the comparison is ~9-target ULM against ~1000-target ULM: a regulon-size and t-statistic-noise comparison as much as a sign-quality comparison. That selection is not adversarial speculation, it falls out of SIGNOR coverage: SIGNOR curates regulatory phosphosites for signalling-proximal factors (YAP1, WWTR1, CTNNB1, NFATC4, HDACs), which are exactly the factors CollecTRI covers thinly. (c) ULM settings: decoupleR v2.1.1 ulm, t-value of the slope, as this project runs it; min_n / tmin is NEVER stated (decoupler default is 5), the gene statistic is the per-timepoint log2FC vs 0 hpi, and no gene is masked. (d) Effect size: not reported anywhere, only the asterisk. Verdict for this reading: the paper does assert it, but nothing in the paper lets anyone check it, so it must be cited as "the authors report" and never as a measured deficit of CollecTRI+ULM.
  READING 2, extra: a direct check of the paper's own showcase example fails. Fig. 2D presents "GATA2 Ser192 and YBX1 Ser174" as the phosphoproteome-derived TF activity examples. I downloaded SIGNOR human causal interactions today (https://signor.uniroma2.it/getData.php?organism=9606, 43518 rows) and filtered as the Methods describe (MECHANISM phosphorylation, EFFECT "up-/down-regulates activity", named residue, TAX_ID 9606, TYPEB protein). GATA2 S192 is there. YBX1 S174 is NOT: SIGNOR's only YBX1 S174 row is PLK1, EFFECT "up-regulates QUANTITY" (S176 likewise; YBX1's activity-directional rows are MAPK1 with no residue and PRKDC Thr89). So either the authors used a looser filter than their Methods state -- in which case their phospho "ground truth" contains sites that act through protein ABUNDANCE, which is the exact confound this project exists to avoid, and which would also partly explain the -0.02 being computed on something other than what is claimed -- or the SIGNOR release they used differed. The paper gives no SIGNOR version or access date, so I cannot separate the two; that ambiguity is itself a reproducibility defect in a benchmark being offered as orthogonal ground truth.
  READING 3. Reusable, with a measured ceiling. MPXV data: reachable and machine-readable. https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-51074-6 returns HTTP 200; the "#Sec41" anchor the authors cite is the Supplementary information block; Supplementary Dataset 2 (41467_2024_51074_MOESM5_ESM.xlsx, 2.3 MB, HTTP 200) has columns gene_name, fold_change_log2.6h/.12h/.24h with p_value and p_value_adj; Supplementary Dataset 4 (MOESM7_ESM.xlsx, 3.4 MB, HTTP 200) has ptmngroup_label, ptm_site, protein_ac, ptm_pos, ptm_AA, is_viral, plus fold_change_log2.6h/.12h/.24h AND protein_fold_change_log2 per timepoint (so the phosphosite change can be protein-abundance-corrected, which matters here). Raw data also in ENA PRJEB60728 and PRIDE PXD040811 / PXD040889. No login, no dependency: both are xlsx, and pandas cannot open them without openpyxl (not installed), but the sheets parse from the zip with stdlib. SIGNOR: public, no key, one HTTP GET, tab-separated, EFFECT and MECHANISM and RESIDUE columns; 2464 human phosphosites on 1079 proteins carry a directional up-/down-regulates-activity annotation. Overlap with CollecTRI as this project loads it (data/collectri.parquet, source with >= 5 distinct targets = 772 TFs of 1185): 150 TFs, 460 TF-site pairs have a directional SIGNOR regulatory phosphosite. That is the POOL. Restricting to sites actually quantified in the MPXV phosphoproteome (9939 gene-site pairs parsed from the Dataset 4 labels): 23 TFs, 38 sites -- the same order as the paper's 26 profiles, so the paper's number is reproducible in spirit. Conclusion on "benchmark or anecdote": the resource is benchmark-sized (150 TFs), any SINGLE phosphoproteomics run is anecdote-sized (23 TFs, and per-TF Pearson on 4 timepoints). The design is only worth reusing pooled over sites and datasets (one r over all TF-site-timepoint observations, or a mixed model), never as 26 four-point correlations.
  REST OF PAPER vs this project's established findings. (i) The string "mask" occurs ZERO times in the paper; no analysis anywhere removes, masks or down-weights the perturbed TF's own gene, and no self-edge or autoregulation control exists. (ii) knockTF IS used, twice: human KnockTF as an additional benchmark (Supplementary Fig. S1C) and KnockTF 2.0 mouse (Supplementary Fig. S1D), the latter filtered to "only experiments in which the targeted TF was downregulated relative to controls (log2 fold change < -0.5)" -- the same selection-on-own-abundance as decoupler's thr_fc=-1 documented in F-018/V-018, at a looser threshold. (iii) The headline benchmark (1423 KD experiments, RPE1/K562/HepG2/Jurkat) scores AUPRC with the perturbed TF as the single positive, via decoupleR's benchmark pipeline with the TF's own gene in the input statistic (V-009 established decoupleR keeps that gene). So yes: their entire activity-inference benchmark repeats the abundance leak of F-005/F-009, with no masked-gene control and no shuffled-network control anywhere in the paper. This is amplified, not neutral, by their construction: TFActProfiler's coefficients come from regressing each target's expression on TF TRANSCRIPT ABUNDANCE (bagging ridge, CellOracle, per Leiden cluster), so its ULM score is by construction a co-expression reconstruction of the TF's own mRNA level, which is precisely what the unmasked benchmark rewards. Their own limitations paragraph concedes the coefficients are "inferred from large-scale transcriptomic covariation". Worth a separate F-entry for the main session: "Sugimoto 2026's TFActProfiler gain over CollecTRI is reported without either control this project requires, and the resource is trained on TF-mRNA covariation, so the 1423-experiment AUPRC gain is a candidate abundance leak." There is also an internal tension the paper does not address: if TFActProfiler is a covariation readout of TF mRNA, and TF mRNA is uncorrelated with phosphorylation (their own -0.02), then it should not track phosphorylation better than CollecTRI; the paper offers the hand-wave that training cells "may include states with both high TF mRNA and enhanced TF function".
Would flip if: reading 1 flips to confirmed if the authors' code (https://github.com/HikaruSugimoto/tfactprofiler, Zenodo 20779282) shows the -0.02 computed over >= ~100 TF-timepoint observations with an equivalence bound, or if a rerun on the MPXV tables gives |r| < 0.1 with a CI inside +-0.15. Reading 2 flips to confirmed if Supplementary Fig. S1F or the repo supplies a named paired test, per-TF r values for both resources and an effect size, AND the comparison survives restricting to TFs with comparable regulon sizes in both resources. Reading 2 flips to confounded if the per-TF r gap tracks CollecTRI regulon size. Reading 3 flips to refuted if the MOESM5/MOESM7 xlsx links stop resolving or if a rerun finds fewer than ~10 usable TFs after requiring a site quantified at all three timepoints. The YBX1 finding flips if an archived SIGNOR release from before 2026-09 annotates YBX1 S174 as activity-directional.
Severity: high — reading 1 is the stated justification for moving the project onto activity-vs-abundance validation by phosphoproteomics, and as worded it is not what the paper shows; reading 2 is the only external evidence that CollecTRI+ULM has a measurable deficit this project could beat, and it is an authors-benchmark-own-method result with a single asterisk behind it; reading 3 is the go/no-go on a data download. Medium severity on the abundance-leak observation: it does not change any number here, it removes Sugimoto 2026 as an independent corroboration of anything and makes it another instance of F-005.

## F-023 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic V-023 observation (entered by main session) · status: proposed
Claim: Sugimoto 2026's TFActProfiler (refs/sugimoto-2026-tfactprofiler.md, NAR 10.1093/nar/gkag897) reports beating CollecTRI+ULM without either control CLAUDE.md requires, and its construction makes its headline benchmark a candidate abundance leak of exactly the kind F-005/F-009 document. Three facts, each from the paper: (i) the string "mask" occurs ZERO times; no analysis anywhere removes, masks or downweights the perturbed TF's own gene, and there is no self-edge, autoregulation or shuffled-network control. (ii) knockTF is used twice as a benchmark, and the mouse KnockTF 2.0 arm is filtered to "only experiments in which the targeted TF was downregulated relative to controls (log2 fold change < -0.5)", i.e. the same selection-on-own-abundance as decoupler's thr_fc=-1 (F-018/V-018), at a looser threshold. (iii) The headline benchmark scores AUPRC over 1423 knockdown experiments with the perturbed TF as the single positive, through decoupleR's pipeline, with the TF's own gene in the input statistic (V-009 established decoupleR keeps that gene). Amplifying, not neutral: TFActProfiler's coefficients come from regressing each target's expression on TF TRANSCRIPT ABUNDANCE (bagging ridge, CellOracle-style, per Leiden cluster), so its ULM score is by construction a co-expression reconstruction of the TF's own mRNA level, which is precisely what an unmasked benchmark rewards. Their own limitations paragraph concedes the coefficients are "inferred from large-scale transcriptomic covariation". Internal tension the paper does not address: if TFActProfiler is a covariation readout of TF mRNA, and TF mRNA is uncorrelated with phosphorylation (their own -0.02), it should not track phosphorylation better than CollecTRI.
Evidence: V-023, which read the paper and the Europe PMC JATS (PMC13575427). Not tested here: nobody has rerun their benchmark masked. The prediction this claim implies is testable on their released resource (github.com/HikaruSugimoto/tfactprofiler, Zenodo 20779282): masking the perturbed TF's own gene should cost TFActProfiler more than it costs CollecTRI, because more of its score is own-mRNA reconstruction.
Source: refs/sugimoto-2026-tfactprofiler.md; V-023.
Confidence: high for the three facts (absence of the string, the quoted mouse filter, decoupleR keeping the gene per V-009, and their own description of the training). Medium for "candidate abundance leak": it is a mechanism argument plus the absence of a control, not a measured deficit. Low for any claim about the size of the leak, which is unmeasured.
Depends on: F-005, F-009, F-018

## F-024 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: INCONCLUSIVE at n=51. On the only phosphoproteomics ground truth this project has (MPXV-infected primary human fibroblasts, 6/12/24 hpi, Nat Commun s41467-024-51074-6 Suppl. Datasets 2 and 4), masked ULM on CollecTRI does NOT measurably track TF activity that the TF's own mRNA cannot explain -- but nothing is measurable at this size. Pooled over TF-timepoint observations, protein-abundance-corrected phospho activity vs masked ULM: Spearman -0.018, 95% TF-cluster bootstrap CI [-0.33, +0.33] (Pearson -0.036), n=51 (19 TFs x 3 timepoints, minus site-timepoints with no protein_fold_change). Unmasked ULM -0.010 [-0.32, +0.32]; self-edge-deleted (F-010's equivalent of masking) -0.029 [-0.35, +0.32]. The prediction stated in the docstring before running was +0.25 with a CI excluding 0; observed is 0 with a CI that also contains +0.25 and -0.25, so this REFUTES NOTHING and CONFIRMS NOTHING. The masked-gene control is structurally unnecessary here and is reported only to show it: no TF is perturbed, so there is no own-mRNA drop to leak, and masking moves the pooled Spearman by 0.008 (max |masked - self-edge-deleted| over the panel = 0.058 score units). CONTROLS, both required, both uninformative at this n: degree-preserving shuffled network (fulldose.py's shuffler, 10 seeds) gives Spearman +0.097, range [-0.066, +0.293] -- the shuffled null is NOT centred tightly on 0 and 6 of 10 shuffled seeds beat the real network, which is the cleanest statement of how little this benchmark can resolve; placebo ground truth (each TF's ULM paired with a different TF's phospho trajectory, 500 derangements, seed 0) +0.029 [-0.33, +0.39], one-sided p = 0.590 for the real masked value. Retrieval analogue of the knockTF hit rate (leak.py's evaluate() with sign(phospho activity) in the role of type_p, rank of the TF among 622 scoreable TFs): masked ULM top-10% 0.04, top-5% 0.04 against 0.10/0.05 chance, unmasked 0.06/0.04, shuffled seeds 0.04-0.18; paired masked vs unmasked better 0.22 worse 0.39. So on the corrected readout ULM is at or below chance at retrieval too, with 51 observations behind it.
Second result, the one worth carrying: the abundance correction is what kills the signal. With the SAME sites and the SAME ULM, uncorrected phospho activity (site log2FC, no protein subtraction -- what a benchmark without Dataset 4's protein columns would use) gives Spearman +0.209 [-0.16, +0.54] unmasked, +0.203 [-0.17, +0.55] masked, n=57; subtracting each site's own protein_fold_change_log2 at that timepoint takes it to -0.018. The two CIs overlap heavily so this is a suggestion, not a result, but it is the same direction as F-005/F-009/F-023: an uncorrected phosphosite benchmark is partly a protein-abundance readout, and the part ULM appeared to track is the part that moved with the protein.
Third, the number Sugimoto 2026 reports as -0.02 with an unstated n and unit (V-023): recomputed here with the unit and n stated, TF-timepoint observations, own mRNA log2FC vs phospho-derived activity -- corrected n=51 Spearman -0.170 [-0.45, +0.13], Pearson -0.393 [-0.66, -0.09]; uncorrected n=57 Spearman -0.038 [-0.34, +0.25], Pearson -0.148 [-0.49, +0.07]. The uncorrected Spearman -0.038 is the closest analogue of their -0.02 and reproduces it in spirit; the corrected Pearson CI excludes 0, but Spearman does not, so it is outlier-driven and must not be cited as "own mRNA anti-predicts activity". The obvious mechanical worry (subtracting protein log2FC induces a negative mRNA correlation because protein tracks mRNA) is checked in the script and is not the explanation: own mRNA vs own protein log2FC over the same observations is Spearman +0.039 / Pearson -0.162, i.e. flat.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python phospho.py` (about 40 s, assert passes). Data, all public, no login, no new dependency, written to data/ (gitignored): mpxv_transcriptome.xlsx (MOESM5, 2.3 MB), mpxv_phospho.xlsx (MOESM7, 3.4 MB), signor_human.tsv (https://signor.uniroma2.it/getData.php?organism=9606, 21 MB, 43518 rows, ships WITHOUT a header row -- the 29 columns are assigned positionally in the script). xlsx parsed from the zip with stdlib (zipfile + ElementTree) exactly as V-023 did, because openpyxl is not installed and pandas cannot read xlsx without it. SIGNOR filter as V-023 specified: MECHANISM phosphorylation, EFFECT "up-regulates activity" or "down-regulates activity" ONLY (never "regulates quantity" -- V-023 found their showcase site YBX1 S174 is a QUANTITY annotation), named three-letter residue, TAX_ID 9606, TYPEB protein -> 4363 rows -> 2335 sites on 1043 proteins, 50 sites dropped for carrying both an up and a down annotation. V-023 got 2464 sites / 1079 proteins from the same URL and filter; the 5% gap is the conflict drop plus residue-string parsing, not a different filter. Phosphoproteome: 9983 non-viral non-contaminant site rows, 211 with a directional annotation on 142 proteins.
