Panel: 19 CollecTRI TFs (CTNNB1, EZH2, FOS, GATA2, HDAC1, HDAC5, JUN, MECP2, MEF2D, NCOA3, NFATC4, NPM1, PML, RB1, RUNX1, SMAD1, STAT1, WWTR1, YAP1; 33 site rows, YAP1 alone has 7), not V-023's 23, because CollecTRI is pruned at tmin=5 against the 12971 genes measured in THIS transcriptome (622 scoreable TFs, not 772). Regulon sizes 5 to 385, median 46, five at or under 17 -- V-023's regulon-size objection to the comparison applies here too. Phospho activity is the MEAN (not sum) over a TF's directional sites of sign * log2FC, sign flipped for down-regulates-activity sites, corrected version subtracting that site's protein_fold_change_log2 at the same timepoint.
Source: analysis/phospho.py
Confidence: high that the experiment is inconclusive and that this dataset cannot decide the question -- the CI half-width is 0.33, the shuffled null itself ranges over 0.36 of Spearman, and the placebo covers [-0.33, +0.39]. Medium for "the correction is what kills the signal": the corrected and uncorrected CIs overlap, and the two use slightly different observation sets (51 vs 57). Low for any claim about the sign of the masked-ULM correlation. SIZE NEEDED: CI width scales as 1/sqrt(n_TF), so +/-0.10 needs about 206 TFs with a directional quantified site, roughly 11x this phosphoproteome's panel -- about ten MPXV-sized phosphoproteomes, or one deep run with ~130 directional TF sites at three timepoints, which is the same order V-023 back-solved from Sugimoto's own CI (~385 observations). Nothing in a single phosphoproteomics time course will settle the activity-vs-abundance premise.
Depends on: F-010, F-023

## V-024 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — every number reproduces and the inconclusiveness is real, not manufactured (it survives dropping small regulons and leaving out any one TF). Two parts must be recited differently: the shuffled null's "+0.097, not centred on 0" is a 10-SEED ARTEFACT (50 seeds: mean +0.036, sd 0.159, se 0.023), and the "6 of 10 shuffled seeds beat the real network" is not the Spearman count (9 of 10 at 10 seeds, 33 of 50 at 50). The second result is UNDERSTATED, not overstated: the paired test the entry says it did not do gives d = -0.245 with one-sided p 0.034 and a mechanism (+0.360). REFUTED: the SIZE NEEDED projection — 206 TFs is more than the whole CollecTRI x SIGNOR pool contains, so "about ten MPXV-sized phosphoproteomes" is not a route to +/-0.10
Claim checked: F-024 — masked ULM on CollecTRI does not measurably track protein-abundance-corrected phospho activity in the MPXV fibroblast time course (Spearman -0.018, 95% TF-cluster CI [-0.33,+0.33], n=51), nothing at this size can, and the one carryable observation is that the abundance correction is what kills an apparent +0.209; plus a recomputation of Sugimoto's -0.02 and a projected 206 TFs / ~10 phosphoproteomes to reach +/-0.10.
How verified: reran `cd analysis && uv run python phospho.py` (13 s, exit 0, assert passes). EVERY claimed number reproduces exactly, including the ones the task listed: corrected masked -0.018 [-0.33,+0.33] / unmasked -0.010 / self-edge-deleted -0.029 at n=51; shuffled 10-seed mean +0.097 range [-0.066,+0.293]; placebo +0.029 [-0.33,+0.39] p 0.590; uncorrected masked +0.203 [-0.17,+0.55] and unmasked +0.209 at n=57; own-mRNA-vs-phospho corrected Spearman -0.170 / Pearson -0.393 [-0.66,-0.09], uncorrected -0.038 / -0.148; own mRNA vs own protein +0.039 / -0.162; 19 TFs, 33 site rows, 51/57 observations, 622 scoreable TFs, 2335 SIGNOR sites on 1043 proteins, 12 of 19 panel TFs with a self-edge, max |masked - self-edge-deleted| 0.058. Audit in a scratch script that imports phospho.py's namespace so it uses the same objects, not a reimplementation.
  (1) THE ATTACKED RESULT, "the correction is what kills the signal": SURVIVES and is stronger than F-024 claims. The observation sets are nested, not merely different: the 6 uncorrected-only observations are EZH2 and FOS at all three timepoints, the only two panel TFs with no `protein_fold_change_log2` at any timepoint, so the corrected analysis is 17 TFs x 3, and "19 TFs x 3 timepoints minus site-timepoints" in the Claim should read "17 TFs x 3". Within the 51 common observations the site sets used by the two versions are IDENTICAL in 51 of 51, so "same sites, same ULM" is exact. Paired on those 51: uncorrected +0.227, corrected -0.018, d = -0.245, TF-cluster bootstrap over the 17 clusters (4000 reps) 95% CI [-0.542,+0.017], one-sided P(d >= 0) = 0.034; Fisher-z difference -0.249 [-0.587,+0.017]. So the drop is marginal-but-supported, not the "CIs overlap heavily, this is a suggestion" the entry settles for — comparing two marginal CIs is the wrong test and it understates the entry's own result. The alternative explanation the entry does not raise is REFUTED: the 6 extra observations are not driving +0.209 (on those 6 alone the uncorrected Spearman is +0.143, BELOW the pooled value, and restricting to the common 51 RAISES uncorrected from +0.203 to +0.227).
  (2) MECHANISM, checked and then pushed further than the entry goes. `protein_fold_change_log2.<tp>` in Dataset 4 is PER-PROTEIN, repeated across that protein's sites: of 1697 proteins with more than one quantified site, 1697 have <= 1 distinct protein value per timepoint and 0 have more; it does vary across timepoints (9215 of 9983 rows have 3 distinct values). So subtracting it does what the entry claims — it turns a site log2FC into an occupancy change. On the panel the decomposition is exact: mean(sign*site) = mean(sign*corr) + mean(sign*prot) to 1.1e-16 over the 51. That makes the direct test available, and the entry never runs it: masked ULM vs the PROTEIN component alone is Spearman +0.360, Pearson +0.363, TF-cluster CI [-0.026,+0.645], against -0.018 for the occupancy component. The uncorrected +0.227 is therefore ULM tracking protein abundance, with the two components near-orthogonal (Spearman(corr, prot) = -0.102) and the protein component carrying comparable spread (sd 0.737 vs 1.035). That is the sentence worth carrying, and it is a stronger and more specific one than F-024's. Caveat on the correction's own mechanics, unmentioned in the entry: because activity is mean(sign * x), the protein term enters as mean(sign) * prot, so a mixed-sign TF gets an attenuated correction; here that is YAP1 only (mean sign 0.43 over 7 sites), 1 of 19.
  (3) IS THE INCONCLUSIVENESS REAL? Yes, and it is robust to both ways of attacking it, but the shuffle sentence is wrong. (a) 50 SEEDS of the same degree-preserving shuffler against the corrected readout: mean +0.036, median +0.025, sd 0.159, range [-0.274,+0.447], 30 of 50 positive, 33 of 50 above the real masked -0.018, mean/se = 1.60. The +0.097 of the first 10 seeds is sampling noise in the number of seeds, not a bias: the null IS centred near 0, it is merely enormously wide (a single shuffled network's Spearman has sd 0.16 at n=51). Not a bug in the shuffler and not hub genes: timepoint-centring both series leaves the shuffled seeds at mean +0.034, range [-0.265,+0.482], 30 of 50 positive, and the real masked value at -0.021, so no timepoint-level trend is generating it. Also, at 10 seeds the Spearman count of seeds beating the real network is 9, not 6 (the 6 is the fraction-better>0.5 count in the paired retrieval column, a different comparison, and the entry attaches it to the Spearman sentence). The entry's conclusion from this control is right for the wrong reason: the resolution limit is the null's WIDTH (sd 0.159), not a positive centre. (b) REGULONS >= 20 measured targets: 10 TFs, n=30, masked corrected Spearman +0.051 [-0.33,+0.53]; >= 30 targets: 8 TFs, n=24, +0.096 [-0.36,+0.58]. Dropping the small regulons does not rescue a signal, and the CI gets wider, so V-023's regulon-size objection cannot be the reason for the null here. (c) JACKKNIFE over the 17 contributing TFs: leave-one-out pooled Spearman spans -0.112 (drop GATA2) to +0.073 (drop STAT1), max |d| = 0.094; YAP1's 7 sites move it by +0.005 (d for dropping YAP1). No single TF carries the pooled value, in either direction. The uncorrected +0.203 is slightly more concentrated but still not a one-TF result (span +0.124 dropping GATA2 to +0.304 dropping STAT1, max |d| = 0.100).
  (4) SIZE CLAIM, REFUTED as a research route though the arithmetic is right. 19 * (0.329/0.10)^2 = 205.9 -> 206 TFs, and 206/19 = 10.8, so "about ten" should be "about eleven". The 1/sqrt(n_TF) scaling is the right scaling for a TF-CLUSTER bootstrap (within-TF correlation across timepoints is already absorbed by the clustering; more timepoints on the SAME TF buy almost nothing, which is exactly why 51 observations behave like 17). What is wrong is the conversion to datasets: pooling 11 phosphoproteomes does not give 206 TF clusters, because the clusters are TF identities and the same signalling-proximal factors recur in every run. The ceiling is fixed by the resources, not by how many datasets are pooled: 174 of CollecTRI's 1185 sources have a directional SIGNOR activity phosphosite at all, and 142 of the 622 TFs scoreable at tmin=5 in this transcriptome do. 206 > 174, so +/-0.10 is UNREACHABLE with CollecTRI x SIGNOR-directional as the ground truth, at any number of datasets. Best-case half-widths, scaling the observed 0.329 by sqrt(19/n): 0.109 if every one of the 174 were measurable, 0.120 at 142, and about 0.185 at a realistic union of ~60 distinct TFs over ten runs (one run detected 19 of the 142 available, and detection repeats across runs because it tracks protein abundance). So the honest statement replacing the Confidence field's last two sentences is: ten more phosphoproteomes buy roughly +/-0.18, the whole resource buys about +/-0.11, and +/-0.10 needs a ground truth broader than SIGNOR's directional-activity annotation.
  (5) ROUTE BOOKKEEPING (new CLAUDE.md conventions). F-024 is the first TF-sparing entry; F-001 to F-023 are TF-perturbing. What the project can now say about cross-route agreement: NOTHING about any method's performance, and the launching reading "the TF-sparing route is ~11x too small to agree or disagree" is right in substance but slightly too strong in both directions. Too weak in one place: the retrieval analogue is on knockTF's own scale and does exclude knockTF's level — masked ULM top-10% here is 0.039 (2/51), TF-cluster 95% CI [0.000,0.098], and no bootstrap replicate of 4000 reaches knockTF's masked 0.344. That comparison must NOT be cited as a route disagreement: the positive is defined differently (rank among 622 by sign(phospho activity)*score, versus the perturbed TF as the single positive), the panel is selected by SIGNOR coverage, and this readout's own shuffled null spans top-10% 0.04-0.18, so the 0.10 "chance by construction" is not the operative null. Too strong in the other place: ONE cross-route corroboration now exists, and it is not about a method — the abundance-leak family (F-005/F-009/F-023) gets its first TF-sparing support from (2), where the only thing masked ULM tracks in this dataset is the protein-abundance component (+0.360 [-0.03,+0.65]) and not the occupancy component (-0.018). Weak, one dataset, CI touching 0, but it is the leak claim appearing on a route where no TF is perturbed and the TF's own mRNA is not on the causal path. So: "no cross-route evidence about any method's accuracy; one weak cross-route corroboration that uncorrected benchmarks read abundance."
  Minor: the Claim's "19 TFs x 3 timepoints" is 17 for the corrected numbers (see (1)); the bootstrap in the script correctly uses only the 17 contributing clusters, so no CI is affected.
Would flip if: (1) flips to refuted if the paired d on the common observations moves above 0, e.g. if the protein correction is recomputed per-site rather than per-protein (Dataset 4 does not support that) or if a second phosphoproteome gives d >= 0 — the present interval [-0.542,+0.017] is one dataset. The +0.360 ULM-vs-protein-component result flips if it does not replicate in a second dataset or if it turns out to be driven by the panel's protein levels tracking the global transcriptome response rather than anything TF-specific (a per-timepoint-centred replication with a protein-level null would settle it; not run here). (3a) flips back to "the null is biased positive" if 500 seeds give a mean more than 2 se from 0. (4) flips if the ground truth is widened beyond SIGNOR directional-activity sites (PhosphoSitePlus regulatory sites, or a kinase-substrate model), which would raise the 174 ceiling; the arithmetic itself does not flip. The headline INCONCLUSIVE flips only with more distinct TFs, never with more timepoints on these 19.
Severity: high — F-024 is the project's only TF-sparing measurement and its Confidence field's size projection is the go/no-go on whether to spend effort pooling public phosphoproteomes. As written that projection points at a target the resource cannot reach; the corrected version (ten runs buy +/-0.18) changes the decision. Medium for the second result, which is now the most citable thing in the entry and should be cited with the paired number (d = -0.245, one-sided p 0.034) and the mechanism (+0.360), not with the two overlapping marginal CIs. Low for the shuffle-sentence correction: it changes no conclusion, but "the shuffled null is not centred on 0" would be repeated as a property of this readout and it is not one.

## V-025 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: refuted — V-024's filing of the +0.360 under the abundance-leak family is REFUTED on mechanism: the own-transcript channel is not merely unlikely, it is arithmetically shut, and the +0.360 was already computed on MASKED scores. The main session's replacement reading is right about the mechanism and overstated about the scope: the effect is real against both nulls V-024 left unrun, but it lives entirely BETWEEN TFs and the SIGNOR sign flip is load-bearing, so "ULM detects abundance-driven activity through its regulon" is one of two explanations the data cannot separate at 17 TFs
Claim checked: F-024 second result / V-024 (2) — the competing readings of "masked ULM correlates +0.360 [-0.03,+0.65] with the PROTEIN-abundance component of the phosphosite change and -0.018 with the occupancy component". V-024 filed it as a cross-route corroboration of the abundance leak (F-005/F-009/F-023) and the main session relayed it as "the only thing ULM tracked was abundance"; the competing reading is that on a TF-sparing route a target-derived score correlating with the TF's own protein level is ULM working, not leaking, and that the null on the occupancy component is what matters for decoupling.
How verified: two scratch scripts that `import phospho` and reuse its objects (no reimplementation), plus a third for the sign decomposition; run with `cd analysis && uv run python <scratch>.py`. phospho.py's own output is unchanged and its assert passes; V-024's +0.360 reproduces exactly (scratch assert on |rho - 0.360| < 0.001). The decomposition site = occupancy + protein holds to 2.2e-16 over the 51 rows, and the protein-component rows are the SAME 51 as the occupancy rows.
  (1) SELF-EDGE / OWN-TRANSCRIPT ROUTE: REFUTED, directly. Masked ULM vs the protein component +0.360 [-0.03,+0.64]; self-edge deleted +0.359 [-0.02,+0.64]; the TF's own gene zeroed out of EVERY regulon (whole adjacency row) +0.359; that condition AND masking together +0.358; own gene removed from every OTHER regulon with the self-edge kept +0.342; plain unmasked ULM +0.342. So every condition that closes the own-transcript channel gives the same number, and the channel-open condition is the LOWEST of the six. This is not a marginal survival: `es_masked` zeroes the TF's own gene in the input matrix before scoring, so the number V-024 promoted was computed with the channel already shut, and the leak framing was a category error at the point it was filed. Mediation is also excluded on magnitudes: own mRNA log2FC vs masked ULM score Spearman +0.029, own mRNA vs the protein component -0.153 (Pearson +0.171), own mRNA vs own protein +0.039 (F-024, reproduced), so the largest correlation the transcript could mediate is about 0.03 x 0.15 = 0.005 against an observed 0.360. 7 of the 19 panel TFs have no self-edge at all, and max|masked - self-edge-deleted| over the panel is 0.058 score units. The F-005/F-009/F-023 leak is "the perturbed TF's own TRANSCRIPT reaches its own score through the self-edge"; here nothing is perturbed, the transcript contributes nothing, and the correlate is a PROTEIN measurement of the TF. The Index line "on a route where no TF is perturbed, the only thing masked ULM tracks is protein abundance" must not be cited as leak evidence, and "the only thing" is separately unsupported (the occupancy CI is +/-0.33 wide, so "tracks nothing else" is unmeasured, not measured).
  (2) THE CONFOUND V-024 NAMED AND DID NOT RUN: the +0.360 is NOT a timepoint effect and NOT panel-level tracking of the global response. Within timepoint: 6h +0.137, 12h +0.353, 24h +0.635 (n=17 each), all positive. Per-timepoint centring BOTH series raises it to +0.388, TF-cluster 95% CI [+0.03,+0.64] (4000 reps), per-timepoint-ranked +0.375. Derangement null exactly as specified (each TF's masked ULM paired with a DIFFERENT TF's protein component, 500 draws, seed 0): mean -0.034, sd 0.211, [-0.44,+0.37], real +0.360, one-sided p = 0.032; on the centred series mean -0.032, sd 0.197, real +0.388, p = 0.014. The same null on the occupancy component reproduces F-024's negative (mean +0.029, real -0.018, p = 0.590). Degree-preserving shuffled network, 50 seeds, against the protein component: mean -0.037, sd 0.147, range [-0.377,+0.270], 0 of 50 at or above +0.360 — note this null is TIGHTER and better behaved than the same shuffler against the occupancy component (V-024 (3a): mean +0.036, sd 0.159), so the readout does resolve this signal even though it cannot resolve the occupancy one. Jackknife over the 17 contributing TFs: +0.283 (drop CTNNB1) to +0.435 (drop MECP2), no single TF carries it. So the confound is answered: a random TF's ULM score does NOT predict this TF's protein change, and a shuffled regulon does not either.
  (3) NEW, AND IT LIMITS BOTH READINGS. (a) The effect is entirely BETWEEN-TF. Per-TF medians (one point per TF, 17 points) give Spearman +0.480, but WITHIN a TF across its three timepoints masked ULM vs its own protein change averages -0.118 and is positive in 7 of 17 TFs. "More TF protein, more target response" is not observed as a trajectory in any TF; it is a ranking over 17 TFs, which is the same shape that one common driver with TF-specific sensitivity (MPXV host shutoff hitting some TFs and their regulons harder) produces. The derangement null excludes a driver that moves every TF identically; it does not exclude a TF-specific-sensitivity version, and nothing in a single unperturbed time course can. (b) The SIGNOR sign flip is load-bearing, and F-024's "protein component" is not TF protein abundance: it is mean(sign * protein log2FC), where sign is the phosphosite's up/down activity annotation, a per-TF constant (+1 for 12 panel TFs, -1 for 7, YAP1 +0.43). Against UNSIGNED protein log2FC the pooled Spearman is +0.204 [-0.14,+0.52] (Pearson -0.030), not +0.360. Split by sign group: in the 12 mean-sign +1 TFs (n=33) masked ULM vs unsigned protein is +0.331 and vs signed +0.353 (identical quantity); in the 7 mean-sign -1 TFs (n=18) it is +0.028 unsigned and -0.028 signed, i.e. nothing. The pooled +0.360 beats both groups because flipping the -1 group's mostly falling protein values to positive aligns them with that group's higher mean ULM score (+0.187 vs -0.460), a between-group offset: ULM score vs the sign alone is -0.391 over the 17 TFs. So +0.360 is partly a property of the signed construction. The defensible numbers for "masked ULM tracks the TF's protein level" are +0.331 on 12 TFs / 33 observations, or +0.204 pooled unsigned — both with CIs that include 0.
  (4) VERDICT ON THE TWO READINGS. V-024's: refuted, on (1). The main session's: supported on mechanism, not established as an interpretation. "ULM appears to detect abundance-driven activity correctly through its regulon" is the leading explanation but is not separated from a common upstream driver at 17 TFs (3a), and rests on a quantity whose sign convention does half the work (3b). "Shows no detectable response to the phosphorylation-driven component" is right as F-024 states it (-0.018, CI [-0.33,+0.33], derangement p 0.590) but is a null with a CI half-width of 0.33, so "the PTM channel is what ULM misses" is NOT licensed — F-024's own headline INCONCLUSIVE stands for that half, and V-024 (4) already showed this resource cannot narrow it. What changed is only which mechanism is excluded: the own-transcript one, definitively.
  (5) "ULM IS ESSENTIALLY TRACKING ABUNDANCE" IS NOT SUPPORTABLE and should be withdrawn from anything the user reads. Against it: masked ULM is 0.344 against a 0.123 per-TF base rate on the 279 (V-022, three nulls agree at 0.09-0.13), with the own transcript removed; net 30 of 126 knockTF hits, 24%, need the self-edge, so 76% do not (F-010/V-010, which explicitly forbids "largely autoregulation"); masked ULM is 13/38 = 0.342 on experiments whose own gene was never measured (V-022); and on the one TF-sparing route the own-transcript channel is shut and what survives is a correlation with the TF's PROTEIN, which is a different claim in kind. What IS supportable, and is the wording to use: knockTF-style benchmarks reward abundance detection and roughly a quarter of ULM's hits there come through the autoregulatory self-edge, but the three quarters that remain still beat the per-TF base rate about threefold, so ULM's regulon signal is real and the leak is a benchmark-inflation term, not the substance.
Would flip if: (1) flips only if `es_masked` stops being the masked object — i.e. if the +0.360 were recomputed on unmasked scores and moved materially (it moves to +0.342, the wrong way), or if some indirect route from the own transcript to the protein component were demonstrated (own mRNA vs own protein is +0.039 and vs the protein component -0.153, so there is none here). (2) flips back to "timepoint effect" if a 500-draw derangement on a second dataset centres on the real value, or if per-timepoint centring killed it (it raises it). (3a) flips if a dose- or time-resolved TF-sparing dataset shows the within-TF relation the between-TF one implies — that is the experiment that would separate the two readings, and it needs protein and regulon measured across several levels of the SAME TF, not more TFs. (3b) flips if the unsigned-protein correlation reaches the signed one in a panel with a balanced sign mix. The whole entry flips if a second phosphoproteome gives a protein-component correlation near 0 with the panel's sign mix balanced.
Severity: high — V-024's reading was promoted to the Index and relayed to the user as "ULM is essentially tracking abundance", which (5) shows the body of evidence contradicts; the correction changes the project's headline about its own baseline. Medium for the positive half: the protein correlation is the first thing on the TF-sparing route that clears both its nulls, so it is worth citing, but only with the between-TF and sign-construction caveats, and it must not be cited as a result about the PTM channel. Low for the occupancy null, unchanged from F-024/V-024.

## F-025 · 2026-09-18 · lit-scout (entered by main session) · status: proposed
Claim: The project's headline is NOT novel as a bare observation, and IS novel as a mechanism plus a controlled demonstration. The bare observation — that the perturbed TF's own transcript change predicts which TF was perturbed as well as or better than regulon-based activity methods — has surfaced twice independently, in neither case as a benchmark-validity problem: (a) Trescher & Leser 2019, Sci Rep (refs/trescher-2019-tf-activity-knockdown.md), on 25 human/E. coli knockdown sets with biRte/ISMARA/RABIT/RACER, report "in 9 out of the 13 data sets, TFs were ranked within the top 5%" by differential expression alone against "only in 15 out of 54 cases, the KD TFs ranked in the top 5%" for the network methods, and they frame it as network methods underperforming, not as inflation; the same paper names self-regulation as a possible cause of method FAILURE ("their inability to model TF self-regulation and feedback loops"), which is the opposite causal direction to F-010. (b) Yashar et al. 2024, iScience / Priori (refs/yashar-2024-priori.md) built an own-expression-only variant and report it "had a higher AUROC and AUPRC than the other methods" — 11 methods including decoupleR's ULM, on a 124-experiment perturbation benchmark with Pathway Commons regulons — and use it constructively as a feature, not as a critique. What is NOT found anywhere: the self-edge mechanism (F-010), the masking ablation, a degree-preserving shuffled-network control, or a per-TF base rate showing the residual is real signal. Two reviews whose explicit subject is how to validate these methods are silent on it: Karamveer & Uzun 2024 (refs/karamveer-2024-grn-benchmarking-review.md), read in full, lists knockTF as a ground truth with no discussion of the perturbed gene, masking, or autoregulation; Hecker et al. 2023 (10.1002/pmic.202200462, could not be fetched, read by the scout via an open-access mirror) has a dedicated "Limitations of TFA validation" section naming buffering, knockdown efficiency, RNA-vs-protein discrepancy and timepoint dependence, and never the perturbed TF's own transcript. CollecTRI's own limitations paragraph names a DIFFERENT confound ("it disregards possible off-target effects of TF perturbation assuming that the perturbed TF has the most deregulated activity"), and the DoRothEA benchmark argues the opposite direction, that confounds are "masking and underestimating the potential of these TF-target resources".
Evidence: lit-scout pass over refs/ariadne-corpus-knocktf.md (176 entries, all titles scanned, all abstracts grepped for mask/leak/circular/self-regulat/autoregulat/own gene/own transcript/trivial — one false positive, a circRNA paper), plus full reads of the two benchmarking reviews and greps of refs/feng-2020-knocktf.md, refs/muller-dott-2023-collectri.md, refs/garcia-alonso-2019-dorothea-benchmark.md, refs/tf-activity-benchmark-perturbation-2026.md, refs/sugimoto-2026-tfactprofiler.md. Independent corroboration of F-018 from the CollecTRI paper itself: "for a limited collection of 12 TFs where we had multiple perturbation experiments" — 12 of 234, i.e. the benchmark's authors also had almost no within-TF replication.
Source: refs/ariadne-corpus-knocktf.md; refs/trescher-2019-tf-activity-knockdown.md; refs/yashar-2024-priori.md; refs/karamveer-2024-grn-benchmarking-review.md; lit-scout report for the 2026-09-18 novelty question.
Confidence: medium-high for the two precedents (exact quotes, both papers now fetched in full to refs/ and re-checkable). Medium for the negative: a 176-paper corpus plus targeted web search is not an exhaustive literature search, the corpus is dominated by single-gene disease papers that cite knockTF only as a lookup, and the scout did not open all of them. LOW for any claim that the mechanism is unpublished outside this corpus.
Depends on: F-005, F-010

## F-026 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: The 0.344 masked-ULM baseline is NOT mostly study context, but its TF-specific margin inside a study is small and its CI includes 0, and V-022's regulon-overlap explanation for the lead does not reproduce. Route: TF-perturbing (knockTF), masked throughout. (1) The study is Profile.ID, the GEO series: 646 values, no placeholders, 46 studies perturbed >= 2 distinct TFs (Pubmed.ID has 159 '-'/'N' placeholder rows and one PMID over 228 experiments; Data.Source has two values and identifies nothing). Those 46 studies hold 155 of the 643 scored experiments (24%, 101 TFs), 77 of them inside the filtered 279; TFs per study over all 907: 600 studies with 1, 29 with 2, 11 with 3, then 7, 7, 8, 12, 19 and one screen with 78. (2) Core paired comparison, masked ULM, one mate rate per experiment: the perturbed TF is top-10% in 0.277 of its own experiments and the SAME TF is top-10% in 0.222 of its study-mates' experiments (3495 experiment pairs), d = +0.056, 95% study-cluster CI [-0.016, +0.138], TF-cluster [-0.022, +0.129]; top-5% 0.245 vs 0.168; median rank fraction 0.321 vs 0.469. On the filtered 279 subset 0.338 vs 0.271, d = +0.067 [-0.039, +0.198] study-cluster. The same TFs score 0.136 in other studies and the per-TF base rate is 0.122, so a study-mate's experiment lifts a TF from ~0.13 to 0.22 and its own experiment to 0.28. (3) V-022's overlap control gives the opposite of the lead's reading, and the Jaccard number behind it does not reproduce: study-mate TF pairs have mean regulon Jaccard 0.015 (2518 pairs) and study-mate experiment pairs 0.024, against 0.021 for random TF pairs, i.e. no enrichment overall; excluding the 78-experiment screen GSE31912 the TF-pair mean is 0.033, so what enrichment exists is in the small deliberate studies. Dropping mate pairs above Jaccard 0.05 (586 of 3495 pairs, 17%) takes d from +0.056 to +0.014 [-0.078, +0.085]; holding the surviving 114 experiments fixed and putting the high-overlap mates back gives -0.003, so the filter itself moves d by +0.017 and the rest is composition: the experiments that lose all their mates are the ones where masked ULM was doing well. (4) Study identity carries more of the confound than any single covariate: d = +0.056 [-0.016, +0.138] grouping by study, +0.105 [+0.072, +0.143] by cell type (Biosample.Name), +0.169 [+0.122, +0.222] by platform, +0.175 [+0.155, +0.203] by knockdown method. Same cell line in a DIFFERENT study gives +0.116 [+0.082, +0.160] and same cell line in the SAME study +0.043 [-0.035, +0.126], so the confound is the series, not the cell line. (5) Study-controlled baseline (top-10% in its own experiment AND better rank there than the same TF's rank in its study-mates' experiments): 0.252 [0.187, 0.342] against the median mate and 0.174 [0.091, 0.299] against the best mate, on the 155; on the 77 filtered rows 0.325 [0.209, 0.456] and 0.260 [0.157, 0.386]; per-TF base rate on those rows 0.136 and 0.132. So requiring the TF to beat its own study-mates costs the baseline 0.09 to 0.17 depending on strictness, leaves it 1.3x-1.9x its base rate, and only 77 of 279 filtered experiments can be tested this way at all.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python studymate.py` (about 3 min; imports leak.py, so propagate.py's and controls.py's tables print above). Masked ULM built as fulldose.py builds it (dc.pp.extract / prune tmin=5 / adjmat / dc.mt.ulm.func on knocktf_full.h5ad, own gene zeroed in its own row for all 907 rows); reproduces the reference numbers exactly: 907/643/263/766, filtered 0.344 (96/279), top-5% 0.280, all-scored 0.299, per-TF base 0.122, and V-022's lead set 155 experiments / 101 TFs / 45 studies with 77 in the filtered 279 and own 0.277 vs mate 0.222. Degree-preserving shuffled TF-gene network (fulldose.py's shuffler, 5 seeds) on the same pairing: d = +0.003 [-0.022, +0.032] and own10 0.07-0.15, against real +0.056 and 0.277, so the own-vs-mate gap is regulon-specific even though it is small. Not owed (no variant proposes a new scoring rule; the one value above 0.344 is 0.338 on a row subset) but run anyway. Predictions were stated in the docstring before running: +0.05 to +0.10 with a study-clustered CI EXCLUDING 0 (point estimate right, CI wrong), the overlap filter INCREASING d (it shrinks it), the confound carried mostly by cell type (it is carried by the study beyond cell type), and a study-controlled baseline of 0.20-0.25 (0.252 lenient, 0.174 strict). The main session's devastating case (CI containing 0 AND study-controlled <= 0.15) is half met: the CI contains 0, the baseline does not fall that far.
Source: analysis/studymate.py
Confidence: high for the grouping, the counts and the shuffled-network contrast (deterministic, reproduces V-022's set exactly). Medium for d and the study-controlled baseline: 155 experiments in 46 studies, one of which (GSE31912) supplies 78 of them, and both CIs are wide enough to contain 0.00 and +0.14. Low for anything about the other 76% of experiments, which have no study-mate and cannot be study-controlled at all; the claim that the remaining 0.25 is TF-specific rests on the shuffle and on the 0.136 other-study rate, not on a within-study contrast. The V-022 Jaccard discrepancy (0.066 reported, 0.015-0.033 here) is unexplained; V-022's scratch script is gone, so it may be a different pairing unit or an unpruned network.
Depends on: F-001, F-010, F-022

## V-026 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: split — every number in F-026 reproduces exactly on a clean rerun, and the survival result, the grouping table and the shuffle control stand. REFUTED: "no enrichment overall" in study-mate regulon overlap (an artefact of forcing Jaccard = 0 for the 47% of mates that have no CollecTRI regulon while the random baseline is drawn only from TFs that have one; against a study-label permutation null the enrichment is real, 1.6-2.2x, p < 0.005, and V-022's 0.066 reproduces EXACTLY as the per-experiment mean over scored mates, so V-022's number stands and only its READING needs correcting). NOT ESTABLISHED, and the entry's framing must not be quoted: "its CI reaching -0.02" / "cannot resolve +0.05" as evidence that the within-study margin is small. Power to exclude 0 at a true +0.05 is 0.28; the design's minimum detectable effect is +0.11, so a CI containing 0 here is uninformative about +0.05 and the SAME CI reaching +0.138 is equally compatible with an effect two and a half times the point estimate. CORRECTED: GSE31912 supplies 38 of the 155 rows (25%), not 78 (that is its row count over all 907, and its TF count); leaving it out changes d by 0.000. NEW, and it cuts against the entry's own conclusion: the masking asymmetry F-026 names and does not control biases d DOWNWARD, and the symmetric fix gives +0.066 with a study-clustered CI of [+0.003, +0.134]
Claim checked: F-026 — masked ULM's 0.344 knockTF baseline is not mostly study context (a study-controlled version costs 0.09-0.17 and stays 1.3-1.9x the per-TF base rate), but the within-study TF-specific margin is only +0.056 with a CI containing 0, and V-022's regulon-overlap explanation for the study-mate lead does not reproduce.
How verified: (1) NUMBERS. Reran `cd analysis && uv run python studymate.py` (exit 0, assert passes, ~3 min). Every number in the entry matches stdout to the printed digit: 907 / 643 scored / 263 TFs / 766 scored TFs / 279 filtered; 0.344 (96/279), top-5% 0.280, all-scored 0.299, per-TF base 0.122; Profile.ID 646 values, 0 placeholders, Pubmed.ID 159 placeholders and one PMID (22955616) over 228 experiments, Data.Source 2 values; TFs-per-study histogram 600/29/11/2/1/1/1/1; 46 multi-TF studies, 155 scored experiments (24%), 101 TFs, 77 in the filtered 279 (the paired table says 45 studies: 46 studies have >= 2 distinct perturbed TFs over all 907 rows, 45 of them contribute a scored row). Core: own 0.277 vs mate 0.222 over 3495 pairs, d +0.056, study-CI [-0.016, +0.138], TF-CI [-0.022, +0.129], top-5% 0.245 vs 0.168, median rank 0.321 vs 0.469; filtered 0.338 vs 0.271, +0.067 [-0.039, +0.198]; other-study 0.136 / 0.131. Jaccard 0.024 experiment pairs, 0.015 TF pairs (2518), 0.021 random, 0.033 excluding GSE31912, 586/3495 (17%) above 0.05. Overlap filter +0.056 -> +0.014 [-0.078, +0.085], same rows all mates -0.003, so the filter itself is +0.017. Grouping: study +0.056, cell type +0.105 [+0.072, +0.143], platform +0.169, knockdown method +0.175, same cell DIFFERENT study +0.116 [+0.082, +0.160] (394 rows), same cell SAME study +0.043 [-0.035, +0.126]. Study-controlled 0.252 [0.187, 0.342] and 0.174 [0.091, 0.299] on the 155; 0.325 [0.209, 0.456] and 0.260 [0.157, 0.386] on the 77; base 0.136 / 0.132. Shuffle: seeds +0.030 / -0.022 / -0.010 / -0.014 / +0.032, mean +0.003 [-0.022, +0.032], own10 0.071-0.148. CONTROLS: both required ones are present in the script and reported. Masking is on in every row of every variant (there is no unmasked arm), and the degree-preserving shuffle is run on the same pairing; the own-vs-mate gap does NOT collapse to the shuffled level (+0.056 vs +0.003), so this is not an abundance leak. Everything below is scratch in the session scratchpad (setup.py, power.py, power2.py, cellrow.py, paired.py, jaccard.py, jac2.py, jac3.py, asym.py, misc.py), rebuilding studymate.py's pipeline without importing leak.py and reproducing 643 / 263 / 766 / 279 and 0.344 / 0.299 before anything else; nothing written into the repo.
(2) THE POWER QUESTION. Cluster structure of the 155 rows: 45 studies of sizes 38, 12, 11, 9, 7, 4, 4, 3, ... ; Kish effective number of clusters 11.9 of 45. ICC of the paired difference across studies +0.155, n0 = 3.23, design effect 1.35, effective n 115 of 155. Realized study-cluster bootstrap SE of d is 0.0394 (half-width 0.077). Per-row d has sd 0.464; by study 13 positive, 8 negative, 24 exactly 0. POWER TABLE, two ways, both preserving the real study structure and the real mate counts. (a) shift bootstrap: resample whole studies from the actual per-row differences centred to 0, add the true delta, run the script's own clustered bootstrap on each simulated dataset (600 sims x 600 draws). This preserves the realized dispersion and clustering exactly. (b) homogeneous-effect simulation: own_i ~ Bern(c_i + delta), mates ~ n_i iid Bern(c_i), c_i the row's observed mate rate (400 x 400) — an upper bound, because it assumes no cross-TF effect heterogeneity and gives sd(d_hat) 0.023-0.031 against the observed 0.039.
    true delta   power (a) realized   power (b) homogeneous
      0.00           0.03                  0.04
      0.05           0.28                  0.41
      0.10           0.69                  0.84
      0.15           0.95                  0.99
      0.20           0.99                  1.00
  Minimum detectable effect at 80% power: +0.110. To reach 80% power at +0.05 this design needs about 755 eligible (multi-TF-study, scored) experiments against the 155 knockTF supplies, i.e. about 5x the data. So "the CI contains 0" is NOT evidence that the within-study margin is small: the experiment had 28% power against its own point estimate, and the interval's upper end (+0.138) is as compatible with the data as 0 is. The Confidence field's "wide enough to contain 0.00 and +0.14" is the correct statement and is the only one that should be quoted; the Claim's "its CI includes 0" and any reading of it as a near-null must not be. Type-I rate of the procedure is 0.03-0.04, so the CI is if anything slightly conservative, not anti-conservative. GSE31912: 78 rows over all 907, 38 of them scored and eligible (25% of the 155), only 5 in the filtered 279, 78 distinct TFs (the entry's "supplies 78 of them" confuses the study's row count with its share of the 155). Leave-it-out, the experimentalist's own follow-up (i): 117 rows, 44 studies, own 0.308, mate 0.251, d +0.0563 [-0.035, +0.159]; GSE31912 alone d +0.0533. The single big screen is not carrying, diluting or distorting d — it costs precision (SE 0.039 -> 0.051) and nothing else.
(3) THE WELL-POWERED CONTRAST (same cell line, DIFFERENT study, +0.116, n = 394). What it compares: TF t's top-10% rate in its own knockdown against t's rate in experiments on the same Biosample.Name from a different Profile.ID that perturbed a different TF. Same-TF pairs are excluded by construction (mate_pairs requires tf_of[j] != tf_of[i]), so the "recurring same TF" worry does not apply to the pairs themselves. It is robust: study-clustered [+0.075, +0.162], TF-clustered [+0.066, +0.164]; one value per TF (207 TFs) +0.061 [+0.014, +0.110]; dropping mate rows whose perturbed TF also appears in the judged row's own study +0.112 [+0.071, +0.156]; on the filtered 279 +0.160 [+0.096, +0.232]. BUT it is not the number to cite for TF-specificity, because it is not cell-line-controlled evidence at all: holding the same 394 rows fixed and swapping the mates for DIFFERENT-cell different-study experiments gives +0.141, and for any-different-study mates +0.140. Matching on cell line LOWERS d by 0.025; the row is the ordinary base-rate contrast (all 643 scored: own 0.299 vs any other experiment 0.123, +0.176 [+0.139, +0.216]) with cell-line composition held fixed. It says TF identity beats cell context; it says nothing about series/batch, which is the confound F-026 is about. The decisive comparison is on IDENTICAL rows: of the scored experiments, 106 have both a study-mate and a same-cell different-study mate. On those 106 rows (24 studies) own is 0.245, the same-cell other-study mate rate is 0.163 (d +0.082 [+0.027, +0.188] study-clustered) and the within-study mate rate is 0.236 (d +0.009 [-0.072, +0.071]). The paired gap between the two mate sets — the study-context lift — is +0.073, study-CI [+0.001, +0.192], TF-CI [+0.018, +0.128]. That is the finding: being in the same GEO series raises a TF's score by about as much as being the perturbed TF does, and it is the one comparison here whose CI excludes 0 in a design where both arms share the judged row. So: cite +0.116 as "TF identity beats cell line" and the base-rate contrast as "TF identity beats a random experiment"; neither is a within-study TF-specificity estimate, and the within-study estimate remains +0.056 +/- 0.077.
(4) THE JACCARD DISCREPANCY, resolved. Pruned vs raw network and self-edge inclusion are all irrelevant: per-pair means are 0.0242 (pruned+self), 0.0239 (pruned-self), 0.0240 (raw+self), 0.0237 (raw-self). The two things that move it are the averaging unit and what happens to a mate whose TF has no CollecTRI regulon. studymate.py's jac() returns 0.0 for those mates (judgment call (b) lets a mate be unscored), and 1661 of the 3495 pairs (48%) are such pairs, while the random baseline is drawn from `set(tf_of[scored])`, every member of which HAS a regulon. Grid over the 155-row set, mates with a different TF: per-pair / per-experiment / per-TF-pair = 0.0242 / 0.0514 / 0.0152 with all mates, and 0.0461 / 0.0663 / 0.0440 when the mate TF must be scored. V-022's 0.066 is the per-EXPERIMENT mean over scored mates, 0.0663, reproduced to three decimals (0.0654 on the filtered 77). F-026's 0.015 is the per-TF-PAIR mean with the forced zeros. Both numbers are right about their own construction; they are not the same statistic. Which reading stands is settled by a null that preserves TF frequency and study sizes — permute the Profile.ID label over all 907 experiments, 200 draws: with the forced zeros, per-TF-pair observed 0.0152 vs null 0.0144 [0.0111, 0.0183], p = 0.305, and per-pair 0.0242 vs 0.0209, p = 0.100 (this is F-026's "no enrichment", and on its own construction it is correct); with the mate restricted to a scored TF, per-pair 0.0461 vs null 0.0297, per-experiment 0.0663 vs 0.0302, per-TF-pair 0.0440 vs 0.0278, all p < 0.005. So study mates ARE regulon-enriched, by 1.6-2.2x, and the "no enrichment overall" sentence in F-026 part (3) is REFUTED: it compares an observed mean half of whose pairs are zero by missing data against a null computed only over pairs that cannot be. The per-experiment mean is also the statistic that answers the question as posed ("a TF and its study-mates"), since it weights experiments equally as judgment call (d) does everywhere else in the script. What does NOT need correcting is F-026's conclusion, and what DOES need correcting is V-022's reading of its own number: the overlap is real but it is not the explanation for the study-mate lead. Holding the 114 surviving rows fixed and putting the high-overlap mates back moves d by only +0.017 of the +0.056, and on the clean both-scored subset the picture is the same (139 rows, d +0.075 [-0.004, +0.173]; Jaccard <= 0.05, 96 rows, +0.036 [-0.044, +0.122]) — the apparent shrinkage from +0.056 to +0.014 is row composition, exactly as F-026 says. V-022's flip condition for the lead ("would become a finding if it holds when mates above Jaccard 0.05 are excluded") is therefore answered on the wrong statistic and should be retired, not re-run: the enrichment exists, and it accounts for about a fifth of a margin the design cannot resolve anyway.
(5) THE ASYMMETRY, quantified. In a mate row j the MATE's own gene is zeroed but TF t's gene is not, so t's score in row j can still see t's own mRNA through t's self-edge. The channel exists for 80.1% of the 3495 pairs (t has a self-edge) but is almost never loaded: t's logFC in the mate's experiment is exactly 0 in 0.5% of pairs, |logFC| > 0.5 in 8.5%, |logFC| > 1 in 2.0%, below -1 in 1.0%, median -0.020, against a median of -0.963 for the judged TF in its own row. Where it IS loaded it matters: among self-edge TFs the mate top-10% rate is 0.419 on the 31 pairs where t's gene fell below -1 in the mate row, against 0.156 elsewhere (0.115 for no-self-edge TFs). Net effect: the asymmetry inflates the mate rate and so DEFLATES d. Masking all 435 perturbed-TF genes in every row and rescoring (one extra ULM run; this is symmetric and near-free, since F-010 showed removing the gene from other regulons is a no-op — masked ULM on the filtered 279 is 0.348 this way against 0.344) gives own 0.277, mate 0.212, d +0.066, study-CI [+0.003, +0.134], TF-CI [-0.010, +0.140]. So the published +0.056 is conservative by about 0.010, and under the symmetric mask the study-clustered CI just clears 0. Do not read that as a positive result — the shift is a quarter of the SE, the TF-clustered CI still contains 0, and at 28% power neither version is informative — but it does mean the entry's headline ("CI includes 0") survives only on the asymmetric construction the entry itself flags, and the flag should be upgraded from "not controlled" to "controlled, moves d by +0.010 in the direction that would have helped the claim it argues against".
ROUTE (TF-perturbing, knockTF, masked throughout). What F-026 licenses about the 0.344 baseline: it is not mostly study context. Requiring the perturbed TF to beat the same TF's rank in its own study's other experiments costs the baseline 0.09-0.17 (0.344 -> 0.325 lenient / 0.260 strict on the 77 testable filtered rows, 0.277 -> 0.252 / 0.174 on the 155), leaving it 1.3-1.9x its per-TF base rate, and the own-vs-mate gap dies under a degree-preserving shuffle (+0.056 -> +0.003), so what survives is regulon-specific. What it does NOT license: any claim that the within-study TF-specific margin is SMALL (+0.056 +/- 0.077 at 28% power for +0.05, MDE +0.110), any use of "the CI includes 0" as a near-null, and any statement about the other 72% of experiments, which have no study-mate. It also does not license the reverse: the study-context lift is real and sizeable (+0.073 [+0.001, +0.192] on the 106 rows where both contrasts exist, and same-cell-SAME-study +0.043 against same-cell-DIFFERENT-study +0.116), so a fair share of what knockTF rewards is the series, on top of the abundance leak, and that share is not removed by masking.
Would flip if: (the power reading) a version of this contrast with about 750 eligible experiments — knockTF2 or a multi-TF-per-series resource — returning d with an SE near 0.018; at that precision +0.05 would be resolvable and the present interval would become informative. (the Jaccard refutation) a null that is matched to the observed pair set on missing-regulon rate as well as on TF frequency showing the scored-mate enrichment inside its band; the study-label permutation used here matches TF frequency and study size and puts 0.0663 outside a [0.0266, 0.0343] null band, so it would take a different pairing definition, not a different seed. (the asymmetry) nothing foreseeable — the symmetric mask is a single extra ULM run and gives +0.066 [+0.003, +0.134]; it would flip if masking the 435 genes changed the base rate materially, and it does not (0.348 vs 0.344 on the filtered 279). (the survival result) the study-controlled baseline falling to 0.15 or below on a larger eligible set; on the present 77 filtered rows the strict version is 0.260 [0.157, 0.386], so its lower end is already 0.157. (the whole entry) the docstring predictions cannot be verified as pre-registered: nothing in this repo is committed to git, so "stated blind before running" rests on the experimentalist's word. Three of four predictions failing is evidence for it, not proof.
Severity: high — this is the entry that decides how the 34% headline is quoted. The baseline itself does not move: 0.344 against a 0.122 per-TF base rate is unchanged, the study-controlled versions are its honest lower bounds, and CLAUDE.md's "masked-gene ULM baseline is 34% top-10%, not 45%" and README's "the 34% masked figure is the real ULM baseline" need no edit. What needs editing is README's one-line gloss "the same TFs are top-10% in only 12% of experiments where a different TF was perturbed (V-022)": that 12% is the all-experiments base rate, and inside the same GEO series the same TFs reach 0.222, which the sentence as written reads against. Replacement wording for README: "masked ULM puts the perturbed TF in the top 10% of 34% of the filtered knockTF experiments against a per-TF base rate of 12%; the margin is regulon-specific (a degree-preserving shuffle gives 7-15% and no own-vs-mate gap), but it is not all TF identity — inside the same GEO series the same TF already reaches 22% when a DIFFERENT TF was perturbed, and requiring it to beat its own study-mates costs the baseline 2 to 8 points (0.344 -> 0.325 lenient, 0.260 strict) on the 77 of 279 experiments where that test is possible. The within-study TF-specific margin is +0.056 (95% study-clustered CI [-0.016, +0.138], +0.066 [+0.003, +0.134] once every perturbed TF's gene is masked in every row); knockTF has 155 such experiments, enough to detect +0.11 and not +0.05, so this is unresolved, not small."

## F-028 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist · status: proposed
Claim: Inside knockTF, being in the same GEO series raises a TF's masked-ULM score by about as much as being the TF that was actually perturbed, and that study-context lift runs through the real regulons rather than through generic batch variance. Route: TF-perturbing (knockTF), masked throughout, 106 of 643 scored experiments (16%), 52 of them in the filtered 279. (1) THE SET. Of the scored experiments, 106 have BOTH a study-mate (a different TF perturbed in the same Profile.ID) and a same-cell-line mate from a DIFFERENT Profile.ID: 24 studies, 78 TFs, 19 cell lines, 3360 within-study mate pairs and 1794 same-cell-different-study mate pairs. Both contrasts are read on those IDENTICAL 106 rows, which is what makes this identified where F-026 part (4)'s +0.116 same-cell-different-study row is not: V-026 showed matching on cell line LOWERS d (0.141 -> 0.116), so that row is the ordinary base-rate contrast with cell-line composition held fixed. (2) THE SIX NUMBERS, all reproduced exactly under the asymmetric mask V-026 used: own 0.245; same-cell other-study mate 0.163, d = +0.082 (study-CI [+0.025, +0.189], TF-CI [-0.010, +0.169]); within-study mate 0.236, d = +0.009 (study-CI [-0.070, +0.072], TF-CI [-0.076, +0.092]); THE LIFT, within-study mate minus other-study mate, +0.073 with study-CI [-0.001, +0.191] and TF-CI [+0.020, +0.129]. Top-5%: own 0.217, other-study 0.106, within-study 0.169, lift +0.063 [+0.012, +0.156] study / [+0.021, +0.111] TF. Per-TF base rate on these rows 0.139 top-10% / 0.080 top-5% (global 0.122, T-038); that is the reference line, never 0.10. (3) CORRECTION TO V-026's HEADLINE, and it matters: the study-clustered lower bound on the asymmetric lift is -0.001 here against +0.001 in V-026's scratch. That is bootstrap noise between two implementations, not data. Under the ASYMMETRIC mask the CI sits ON zero and "the one comparison whose CI excludes 0" must not be quoted from that row. It does exclude 0 under the SYMMETRIC mask, [+0.019, +0.178], and under TF clustering in both. Quote the symmetric row. (4) T-054, SYMMETRIC MASK, now the convention in studymate.py (every perturbed TF's gene zeroed in every row, 435 genes, one extra ULM run; F-026's blocks still print under the asymmetric convention and are byte-identical, with symmetric counterparts printed beside them). It reproduces V-026 exactly: core d +0.056 -> +0.066 [+0.003, +0.134] study-clustered, filtered-279 baseline 0.348 vs 0.344 (top-5% 0.276 vs 0.280, all-scored 0.289 vs 0.299, per-TF base rate 0.120 vs 0.122), better/worse vs asymmetric ULM 0.47/0.47. It moves the LIFT by -0.000, +0.073 either way. It also raises the filtered-279 core d from +0.067 to +0.095 [+0.005, +0.204] and leaves the study-controlled baseline essentially where F-026 had it (0.325 -> 0.312 lenient, 0.260 -> 0.260 strict on the 77). The asymmetry biases every own-vs-mate d DOWNWARD because a mate row still carries the judged TF's own transcript while the own row has had it removed. (5) THE CONTROL V-026 DID NOT RUN, and the prediction it kills: degree-preserving shuffled TF-gene network (fulldose.py's shuffler), 10 seeds, on the 106-row lift. Shuffled lift +0.004 symmetric (study-CI [-0.007, +0.026], per-seed [-0.025, +0.063]) and +0.000 asymmetric ([-0.011, +0.018], per-seed [-0.034, +0.055]), against a real +0.073: 6% survives, the CI covers 0. The prediction stated in the docstring before running (P7: the lift shrinks but survives at +0.03 to +0.05, because series/batch structure is in the expression matrix and not in the network) is CONTRADICTED. The lift dies under a shuffle exactly as the own-vs-mate gap did (+0.056 -> +0.003), so the study-context effect is not generic batch variance any degree-matched map would read out; it runs through the real regulons, i.e. inside a GEO series a TF's actual targets move together. That is a stronger and more specific claim than the one predicted, and it is the same mechanism class the 0.344 baseline rests on, which is why it cannot be dismissed as a benchmark artefact and also why it is not evidence against the baseline.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python studymate.py` (about 60 s; imports leak.py, so propagate.py's and controls.py's tables print above). Blocks (6), (7) and (8) are APPENDED to the F-026 script; stdout lines 1-118 were captured before the edit and diff byte-identically after it, so no F-026 number moved. Masked ULM built as fulldose.py builds it (dc.pp.extract / prune tmin=5 / adjmat / dc.mt.ulm.func on knocktf_full.h5ad); reproduces 907/643/263/766, filtered 0.344 (96/279), top-5% 0.280, all-scored 0.299, per-TF base 0.122. Both clusterings (Profile.ID and perturbed TF) are reported for every new contrast, 2000 cluster-bootstrap draws, seed 0. Controls in the same script: the perturbed TF's own gene is masked everywhere by construction and there is no unmasked variant, plus the symmetric mask that removes it from mate rows too; the degree-preserving shuffle is run on the 106-row contrast itself, 10 seeds, under both mask conventions. Predictions were stated in the docstring before running: P5 the six numbers reproduce (they do, to the third decimal, except the knife-edge study-CI bound in (3)); P6 the symmetric mask moves the lift by less than 0.020 (it moves it by 0.000); P7 the shuffled lift is +0.03 to +0.05 (it is +0.004, CONTRADICTED). The stated kill conditions were the lift's CI containing 0 under the symmetric mask (it does not: [+0.019, +0.178]) and the shuffled lift matching the real one (it does not: 6%).
Source: analysis/studymate.py
Confidence: high for the set, the counts, the six numbers and the shuffle (deterministic; the shuffle is 10 seeds and every per-seed lift is inside +/-0.063 of 0 while the real one is +0.073). High for the symmetric-mask result, which reproduces V-026's independent scratch run exactly. Medium for the size of the lift: 106 experiments in 24 studies, the study-clustered CI runs [+0.019, +0.178] and the design's own MDE is around +0.11 (V-026's power note on the sibling contrast applies here too), so "about as much as being the perturbed TF" is a statement about point estimates (0.218 vs 0.255 symmetric, 0.236 vs 0.245 asymmetric) that the intervals cannot separate from either 0 or a two-fold difference. Low for anything about the other 84% of scored experiments: 537 of 643 have no study-mate, no same-cell-other-study mate, or neither, and this contrast cannot be run on them at all. The mechanism sentence in (5) is an inference from the shuffle, not a direct measurement of within-series target co-movement; a co-expression test on series-matched rows would be the direct version and was not run.
Depends on: F-026
Would flip if: the within-series target co-movement implied by (5) is absent when measured directly — take the 24 studies, compute each judged TF's regulon's mean pairwise target correlation inside its own series against the same TFs' regulons across series, and find no difference; that would leave the shuffle result needing another explanation, since a lift that dies under a degree-preserving shuffle but has no regulon co-movement behind it would mean the shuffler is destroying something other than regulon identity (regulon overlap with the mate TF is the obvious candidate, and V-026's permutation null already showed study mates are regulon-enriched 1.6-2.2x).
Severity: medium-high for how knockTF results are quoted, low for the 0.344 baseline itself. What this licenses: on the 106 rows where the test is possible, series membership is worth roughly as much to a TF's masked-ULM score as being the perturbed TF, so a study-controlled baseline (F-026 part (5): 0.325 lenient / 0.260 strict on the 77 testable filtered rows) is the honest lower bound to cite next to 0.344, and the symmetric mask is the convention that should be used for any future own-vs-mate contrast. What this does NOT license: any claim that the 0.344 baseline is mostly study context (it is not; F-026 stands and this entry does not touch it), any use of the asymmetric-mask CI as "excludes 0", and any extrapolation to the 537 scored experiments outside the 106. The lift being regulon-specific rather than batch-generic also means it is NOT removable by the usual batch corrections, which is the practical consequence.

## V-028 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed — every number in F-028 reproduces on a clean rerun, stdout lines 1-118 are byte-identical to the pre-edit capture, both required controls are present, and the knife-edge correction in (3) is right. The MECHANISM sentence in (5) is now tested and it splits: the shared-target alternative F-028 names and does not test is REFUTED (the lift is as large or larger among mate pairs with ZERO regulon overlap, and survives rescoring on non-shared targets only), and the lift is specific to the perturbed TF's regulon against a placebo-TF null far stronger than the shuffle (p = 0.001-0.007). But "inside a GEO series a TF's actual targets move together" as a direct statement is NOT ESTABLISHED: measured directly it is +0.158 [-0.245, +0.854] in mean |ES| and +0.032 [-0.034, +0.154] direction-free (placebo p = 0.066); what is elevated is the SIGNED, direction-matched score. NOT LICENSED, and the reason (5)'s inference does not carry it: the lift IS removable by the crudest batch correction (series-mean centering takes it from +0.073 to +0.020 [-0.034, +0.088] while the own arm barely moves). NEW and it bounds the whole result: the lift is entirely a per-row-weighting effect in small studies — pair-weighted it is -0.001, the one systematic 78-TF screen (GSE31912, 38 of 106 rows, 87% of within-pairs) has lift -0.004, and the other 23 studies carry +0.117
Claim checked: F-028 — on the 106 knockTF experiments that have both a study-mate and a same-cell-line mate from another series, being in the same GEO series raises a TF's masked-ULM top-10% rate from 0.145 to 0.218 (+0.073), about as much as being the perturbed TF (0.255), and that lift dies under a degree-preserving shuffle (+0.004), so it runs through the real regulons rather than through generic batch variance.
How verified: (1) NUMBERS. Reran `cd analysis && uv run python studymate.py` (exit 0, assert passes, ~90 s; the unrelated data/bench2026 job does not touch it). `head -118 stdout | diff` against the pre-edit capture /tmp/.../scratchpad/pre.txt: IDENTICAL, so no F-026 number moved. Block (6): 106 scored experiments, 24 studies, 78 TFs, 19 cell lines, 3360 within-study and 1794 same-cell-other-study mate pairs, 52 in the filtered 279, per-TF base rate 0.139 top-10% / 0.080 top-5%. Asymmetric: own 0.245, other-study 0.163 (d +0.082 [+0.025, +0.189] study / [-0.010, +0.169] TF), within-study 0.236 (d +0.009 [-0.070, +0.072] / [-0.076, +0.092]), LIFT +0.073 [-0.001, +0.191] / [+0.020, +0.129]; top-5% 0.217 / 0.106 / 0.169, lift +0.063 [+0.012, +0.156] / [+0.021, +0.111]. Block (7): 435 genes zeroed in all 907 rows, filtered-279 baseline 0.348 vs 0.344, top-5% 0.276 vs 0.280, all-scored 0.289 vs 0.299, base rate 0.120 vs 0.122, better/worse 0.47/0.47; core d +0.056 -> +0.066 [+0.003, +0.134], filtered core +0.067 -> +0.095 [+0.005, +0.204]; study-controlled 0.325 -> 0.312 lenient and 0.260 -> 0.260 strict on the 77; symmetric lift own 0.255 / within 0.218 / other 0.145, own-vs-within +0.036 [-0.049, +0.091], LIFT +0.073 [+0.019, +0.178] / [+0.020, +0.129], symmetric minus asymmetric -0.000. Block (8): 10 seeds, shuffled lift +0.004 [-0.007, +0.026] symmetric (per-seed [-0.025, +0.063]) and +0.000 [-0.011, +0.018] asymmetric (per-seed [-0.034, +0.055]), 6% survival. CONTROLS: masking is on in every arm (no unmasked variant) and the symmetric mask removes the mate-row channel as well; the degree-preserving shuffle is run on the 106-row contrast itself under both conventions and the lift collapses to it. The gain does not shrink to the shuffled level, so this is not an abundance leak. All scratch in the session scratchpad (mech0-4.py, seeds.py), rebuilt from the same pipeline, reproducing 907/643/263/766, 0.344 asymmetric and 0.348 symmetric before anything else; nothing written into the repo.
(2) THE MECHANISM. (a) SHARED TARGETS, REFUTED as the explanation. Pair-level Jaccard between the judged TF and the mate's perturbed TF: within-study mates mean 0.0233 (52% exactly 0, because 48% of within mates have no CollecTRI regulon), same-cell-other-study mates mean 0.0333 (21% zero) — the within arm is if anything the LOWER-overlap one. Row-paired lift restricted to a Jaccard band (rows keeping both arms), symmetric mask: all mates +0.073 (106 rows); jac == 0 only +0.113 [+0.018, +0.418] study / [+0.022, +0.203] TF (69 rows, 9 studies); jac <= 0.01 +0.111; jac <= 0.05 +0.069; jac > 0.05 only +0.086. Flat-to-higher at zero overlap, so the lift does not live in shared targets. (b) THE DIRECT TEST F-028 SKIPPED. Rescored the judged TF in each mate row with a restricted regulon, using decoupler's own ULM formula (verified: full regulon reproduces S_sym to 4 decimals and R_sym to 0.001), ranking the restricted score against the row's other 765 unrestricted scores. NON-SHARED targets only (median 80 shared targets dropped): lift +0.083 [+0.027, +0.191] study / [+0.037, +0.132] TF, top-5% +0.040; SHARED targets only: +0.019 [-0.052, +0.178]. The whole lift is carried by the targets the two TFs do NOT share. But the coherence statement itself does not measure: mean |ULM ES| of the non-shared regulon, within 1.755 vs other 1.597, d +0.158 [-0.245, +0.854]; direction-free "top-10% by |ES|" lift +0.032 [-0.034, +0.154] with a placebo p of 0.066, against +0.073 and p = 0.001 for the signed version. So what is elevated inside a series is the judged TF's regulon score IN THE KNOCKDOWN DIRECTION (type_p-signed), not its raw coherence: in a series that knocked down t once, t's regulon also looks down in that series' OTHER experiments. (c) THE THIRD EXPLANATION, and the strongest new control. PLACEBO-TF null (2000 draws): keep the 106 rows and both mate sets, judge a RANDOM TF instead of the perturbed one — null mean +0.0005, sd 0.025, p(null >= +0.073) = 0.0025 drawing from all 766 CollecTRI TFs and 0.0070 from the 263 perturbed ones (paired version, same random TF in both arms: p = 0.0010; the per-TF lift over all 766 TFs has mean 0.0000, sd 0.061). A random REAL regulon gets no within-series lift, so this is not generic series variance that any regulon reads out — a much stronger statement than the shuffle, which only says the gene-TF map must be real. On control structure: knockTF as fetched exposes NO control-sample identifier (obs is the 14 columns source/Species/Knock.Method/Biosample.Name/Profile.ID/Platform/TF.Class/TF.Superclass/Tissue.Type/Biosample.Type/Data.Source/Pubmed.ID/logFC/type_p; uns and var are empty), so the shared-reference hypothesis cannot be tested directly, only by proxy. The proxy is large: mean logFC correlation (genes measured in both rows) is +0.235 for within-series pairs against +0.022 for same-cell-other-series pairs, and +0.244 vs +0.023 on genes that are in NO CollecTRI regulon, i.e. a series-wide global component exactly as a shared reference would produce. Two things cut against it carrying the lift: the mate hit rate does not rise with the pair's correlation (within-series pairs, hit10 0.164 at r <= 0.10, 0.143, 0.147, 0.120 at r > 0.40), and the placebo null above. One thing cuts for it: removing the series component removes the lift (below).
(3) IS "AS MUCH AS BEING THE PERTURBED TF" SUPPORTABLE? Only as a point estimate, as F-028 concedes, and the honest quantity is the ratio. Study-clustered bootstrap (4000 draws, symmetric mask): perturbed-TF effect (own minus other-study) +0.114 [+0.056, +0.204], lift +0.082 [+0.019, +0.179], own-minus-within +0.032 [-0.048, +0.092] with SE 0.0354, P(lift > perturbed-TF effect) = 0.172, RATIO lift/(perturbed-TF effect) median 0.68, 95% [0.21, 1.60]; TF-clustered: ratio 0.67 [0.22, 2.25], gap +0.035 [-0.045, +0.118], SE 0.0419. HONEST WORDING: "the study-context lift is between a fifth of and 1.6 times the perturbed-TF effect (point estimate two thirds); the data cannot separate 'as much' from 'a fifth as much'". Resolvable at: to get 80% power against the observed +0.036 own-vs-within gap the SE must fall to 0.0128, i.e. about 800 eligible rows study-clustered and about 1130 TF-clustered, against the 106 knockTF supplies (about 420-590 rows for a +0.05 gap) — 8 to 10x this design.
(4) THE KNIFE-EDGE, CONFIRMED, AND A SECOND ONE F-028 ITSELF QUOTES. Re-ran each study-clustered CI over 20 bootstrap seeds. Asymmetric lift: lower bound mean +0.0002, range [-0.0048, +0.0045], positive in 10 of 20 seeds — a coin flip, so F-028 (3) is right that V-026's "+0.001, the one comparison whose CI excludes 0" must not be quoted, and right that the difference from -0.0011 is implementation noise. Symmetric lift: lower bound [+0.0159, +0.0205], positive in 20 of 20 — safe to quote, as instructed. BUT the symmetric CORE d, +0.066 [+0.003, +0.134], which V-026 introduced ("just clears 0", with a caution) and F-028 item (4) repeats without one, has a lower bound of [-0.0086, +0.0033] over 20 seeds and is positive in only 2 of 20: it is the same knife-edge one entry later and must not be cited as excluding 0 either. Scan of every bracketed interval in findings.md for a bound within 0.002 of zero: the only other hits are V-019's size-stratified "+0.207 [+0.001, +0.409]", which self-flags in the same sentence ("lower bound is -0.002 on another seed") and is therefore not an offender, and V-024's "[0.000, 0.098]", where the load-bearing end is the upper bound, not the zero. One place outside the entries does propagate the retired bound: the Index block's F-026 line quotes "+0.073 [+0.001, +0.192]" as "NEW and the striking one, to be committed as F-028 before citing". That line is stale after F-028 (3) and I cannot edit it; the main session should replace it with the symmetric row.
(5) WHERE THE LIFT ACTUALLY LIVES, and it bounds the claim. Row-weighted (the script's judgment call (d)) the lift is +0.073; PAIR-weighted it is -0.001 (0.144 within, 0.145 other, 3360 vs 1794 pairs). The reason: GSE31912, the 78-TF screen, supplies 38 of the 106 rows and 2926 of the 3360 within-pairs (87%) and its own lift is -0.004, while the other 23 studies (68 rows) give +0.117. Leave-one-study-out on the row-weighted lift stays in [+0.061, +0.117], so no single study creates it, but 9 of 24 studies have a positive lift, 6 negative and 9 exactly 0. That pattern is itself evidence about the mechanism: the one series that perturbed TFs SYSTEMATICALLY shows no lift, and the lift lives in the small deliberate studies that chose 2-4 related TFs, which is what a TF-selection/pathway explanation predicts and what a pure shared-reference explanation does not. (6) REMOVABILITY, against F-028 (5)'s implied inference. Series-mean centering (leave-one-out: subtract from each row the mean logFC of the other rows of its Profile.ID, the crudest possible batch correction, then rescore) takes the lift from +0.073 [+0.019, +0.178] to +0.020 [-0.034, +0.088], within 0.218 -> 0.153 against other 0.145 -> 0.133, while the own arm barely moves (filtered-279 baseline 0.348 -> 0.312, 106-row own 0.255 -> 0.245). It is circular for 2-row studies, since the mean subtracted from mate row j contains row i, where t was knocked down; so the non-circular version estimates the series component from the rows other than i and j (only >= 3-row studies, 83 rows in 10 studies): uncentered +0.085 -> +0.018 [-0.061, +0.103]. Either way the lift is carried by a component shared across the series and is removed by removing it. "Regulon-specific" therefore does NOT imply "not batch": a series-wide signed component aligned with the perturbed TF's regulon is regulon-specific, survives the shuffle test, fails the placebo test for genericity, and is removable by centering. What the data cannot separate is why that component is aligned with t's regulon — the series chose functionally related TFs (pathway co-movement), or the series' shared reference deviates along the program the series was built to study.
ROUTE (TF-perturbing, knockTF, masked throughout; 106 of 643 scored experiments, 16%, 52 in the filtered 279). (i) The 0.344/0.348 baseline is untouched: the lift is a property of the MATE arm, the own arm moves by at most 0.010 anywhere in this entry, and 537 of 643 scored experiments cannot be tested this way at all. F-028's own route paragraph is correct as written. (ii) On "ULM recovery is TF-specific": this entry strengthens the specificity of the regulon channel (placebo-TF null p = 0.001-0.007, shuffle 6%) and weakens the specificity of the BENCHMARK, on the rows where it can be measured: a TF reaches top-10% in 0.218 of its own series' other experiments against 0.145 outside, so roughly two thirds of the perturbed-TF effect is reproducible without perturbing that TF. Cite TF-specificity against the per-TF base rate and the shuffle; do not cite top-10% recovery on knockTF as evidence of TF-specific activity detection at the resolution of a GEO series. (iii) Removable by batch correction: YES on this evidence, contrary to the inference (5) invites; the CI returns over 0 after series-mean centering, and the price is 0.036 of the own-arm baseline. Anyone using knockTF should either study-control (F-026 (5)) or center by series, and should expect the 155-row multi-TF subset to be the only place the question can be asked.
Would flip if: (the shared-target refutation) a Jaccard computed on something other than the pruned adjacency's nonzero rows — co-regulation, TF-TF edges, or a shared-upstream measure — showing the jac == 0 band is not overlap-free after all; the present band is 69 rows in 9 studies and its CI runs to +0.418, so a wider one is possible but the point estimate would have to move below the pooled +0.073, not merely widen. (the placebo null) a placebo pool matched to the perturbed TFs on regulon size and base rate showing a null mean near +0.05; the 263-perturbed-TF pool used here already gives +0.007. (the co-movement sentence) about 800 eligible rows, at which the direction-free +0.032 would either resolve or die; at 106 rows it is p = 0.066 and cannot decide. (removability) a batch correction that preserves the own-arm baseline exactly and still leaves the lift at +0.07 would restore F-028's reading; series-mean centering costs 0.036 of the baseline, so the honest statement is "removable, at a price", not "removable for free". (the whole entry) the docstring predictions, including the contradicted P7, cannot be verified as pre-registered: nothing in this repo is committed to git.
Severity: high — this is the entry that says what a knockTF top-10% hit means. Nothing here moves the 34% headline or CLAUDE.md's wording, and F-026/V-026's study-controlled baselines remain the honest lower bound. What must change is how the lift is described: it is regulon-specific and perturbed-TF-specific, it is NOT shared targets, it is NOT a coherence measurement, it is carried by small multi-TF series and absent in the one systematic screen, and it is removable by series-mean centering. The "as much as being the perturbed TF" phrasing should be replaced by the ratio with its interval (0.68, 95% [0.21, 1.60]) wherever it is quoted, including in the Index line.

## F-027 · 2026-09-18 · experimentalist (entered by main session; the agent appended then reverted on an instruction conflict) · status: proposed
Claim: THE ABUNDANCE LEAK DOES NOT TRANSFER to the published 2026 benchmark's headline metric on its one public dataset. Running Zhu/Han/Wang 2026's own `methord/decoupler/dcp.py` and own `result/auc/auc.py`, UNPATCHED, on their own shipped `dataset/Inhibition/Dox/filtered.h5ad` (617 cells, 197 TP53-perturbed vs 420 CTRL, 15498 genes), their ULM+CollecTRI AUROC for TP53 is 0.9572 and stays 0.9571 when TP53's own gene is zeroed in the count matrix before their normalize_total/log1p: a change of -0.0001, against a cell-bootstrap 95% interval of [0.940, 0.971] identical to three decimals for both. Their per-cell rank metric moves 0.0633 -> 0.0629 (top-10% of TFs in 0.838 of perturbed cells masked and unmasked; top-5% 0.690 both). F-010's decomposition reproduces exactly on their pipeline: (b) deleting only TP53's CollecTRI self-edge gives the same -0.0001 as masking (0.9571, rank 0.0635), and (c) deleting TP53 as a target from the other 106 scored TFs' regulons leaves TP53's own AUROC unchanged to 0.000e+00 while moving those 106 TFs by 6.45e-3 — the same 6.44e-3 that masking moves them. The two channels are separable here exactly as in knockTF; they are simply both worth ~1e-4 of AUROC. MECHANISM, readable off their inputs before running: TP53's CollecTRI regulon here is 980 edges / 772 measured targets, and TP53's own transcript is detected in only 95 of 617 cells (raw count mean 0.198 perturbed vs 0.174 CTRL — it does not even go DOWN in counts). One dropout-dominated gene in 772 cannot move a t-statistic. This is the opposite regime from knockTF, where the perturbed TF's own value is a ~5 sd bulk logFC in a median-97-target regulon, and it is consistent with V-010's size finding (for regulons of several hundred targets the self-edge was worth 3% of hits, not 51%). Honest scope: the leak is weak IN THIS REGIME (one 10x Perturb-seq dataset, one TF, a large regulon, a TF whose mRNA barely changes), not that their benchmark is leak-free; their other 16 datasets are not public and include TFs with small regulons and strong knockdowns.
Second result, the internal control: their pipeline scores 613 TFs at tmin=3. Mean |dAUROC| vs anchor — TP53 itself 1.45e-4 under (a), 1.45e-4 under (b), 0.000e+00 under (c). The 106 TFs holding TP53 in their regulon: 6.44e-3 / 0.000e+00 / 6.45e-3. The 243 TFs with NEITHER a self-edge NOR TP53 as a target (built-in null): 0.000e+00 under both network conditions and 1.31e-5 under masking, the last being the library-size channel (zeroing TP53 changes each cell's total counts by ~0.2 of several thousand before their normalize_total). Every group moves exactly where univariate ULM says it can and nowhere else.
Third, THE CONTROL NUMBER THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE LEAK (T-038: do not quote nominal chance): the AUROC floor here is not 0.5. Ten degree-preserving, sign-mix-preserving target shuffles of their net file (self-edges held out of the swap so the null keeps real autoregulation; 13.0% of edges survive, 1.1 points of that the 388 held-out self-edges) give TP53 AUROC 0.5496, range [0.433, 0.701] over seeds — one random-regulon draw reaches 0.70 — with auprc 0.375 and rank 0.494. Their own "random" column for this dataset is 0.4939. Per-cell rank chance is likewise not 0.10/0.05: the shuffled null puts TP53 in the top 10% of TFs in 0.060 of cells and the top 5% in 0.022. So 0.957 is well clear of the floor and the CollecTRI TP53 regulon carries real signal; but any AUROC in the 0.43-0.70 band on this dataset is indistinguishable from a random regulon of the right shape, which covers their published VIPER_CollecTRI (0.508), GSVA_CollecTRI (0.568), AUCell_CollecTRI (0.671), pySCENIC (0.674), scMINER (0.626) and metaTF_PUIC (0.734, just outside).
Fourth, a stability defect in their ORA numbers, with its own control: of the ten decoupleR methods that run here, nine move by less than 0.008 AUROC under masking (waggr -0.0022, ulm -0.0001, aucell -0.0060, viper -0.0023, gsea -0.0048, gsva -0.0027, mlm -0.0078, zscore -0.0001, consensus +0.0017), but ORA moves +0.1893 (0.2636 -> 0.4529). This is NOT the leak: zeroing one RANDOM gene of the same detection rate (PRR11, seed 0) moves ORA by +0.1370, same size and direction, while leaving ulm/zscore/mlm at +/-0.0000 and aucell at -0.0082. decoupler's `_ora.py` line 275 ranks each cell's genes with `rankdata(method='ordinal')` and takes the top n_up = ceil(0.05*15498) = 775; on a matrix where most genes are 0 the 775th position sits inside a huge tie block, so deleting ANY column reshuffles the selected set for every cell. Their ORA_CollecTRI figure for this dataset is not stable to an irrelevant one-column change of the input, and is a property of tie-breaking, not of the method's biology.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python bench2026.py` (assert passes). ANCHOR: their pipeline reproduces their shipped `result/auc/*.csv` for TP53__Dox EXACTLY to six decimals on ULM_CollecTRI — auroc 0.957240, auprc 0.923125, au_rank 0.063274, au_epr 0.000000 — despite decoupler 2.2.0 here vs 2.1.1 in the paper; WAGGR 0.9534, GSEA 0.1812, GSVA 0.5676, MLM 0.8280, ORA 0.2636, Zscore 0.9874, AUCell 0.6706, VIPER 0.5079 also reproduce their shipped column exactly. Only Consensus differs (0.9137 vs their 0.8727) and only because MDT and UDT need xgboost, which is not in this project's env and which nobody authorised adding, so their dcp.py prints "mdt error: xgboost is not installed" and the consensus is over 8 methods rather than 10. Fresh clone at data/bench2026, commit b90419639d3b7a6e2ee79f85cd68a2b991f46e81 (2026-08-07, single commit). Their hardcoded `~/TFact/...` paths are NOT patched: the subprocesses get HOME=data/bench2026_home, containing one symlink TFact -> the clone. `git status` in the clone shows 0 modified tracked files; their shipped result/auc/*.csv are backed up, appended to as auc.sh does, read, and restored.
