ONE code patch, diffed in the script's output: a COPY `methord/decoupler/dcp_cond.py` that (i) lets --net name an arbitrary dataset/net_<name>.csv and (ii) reads the method list from $TFACT_METHODS, used only for the 12 network-side runs and the random-gene control so they can skip waggr/gsea/gsva/viper; the anchor and the masked condition run the untouched dcp.py (43 min each: waggr and gsea do 1000 permutations). auc.py is run unpatched throughout — its `auprc` used-before-assignment bug is inside `if Type == 'Activation'` and Dox is an Inhibition dataset, and dataset/filter.py's `.asType` bug is not on this path because filtered.h5ad is shipped. Bootstrap: 2000 resamples of the 617 cells, seed 0, unit = CELLS. Predictions were stated in the docstring before any condition ran, with a refutation threshold of |dAUROC| > 0.05 for "the leak transfers"; observed -0.0001. Prediction 6 (univariate scorers should leak more than enrichment ones) is NOT borne out: mlm -0.0078 and aucell -0.0060 move more than ulm and zscore at -0.0001.
Source: analysis/bench2026.py; data/bench2026 (gitignored clone).
Confidence: HIGH for the anchor (exact six-decimal reproduction of their shipped numbers) and high for the three conditions on THIS dataset (exact, the built-in null group moves by 0 under both network conditions, and (a) and (b) agree to 1e-4 as univariate ULM requires). MEDIUM-LOW for "the leak does not transfer to their benchmark": n=1 dataset, n=1 perturbed TF, and that TF is the worst possible case for the leak — a 772-target regulon and a TF whose own mRNA does not detectably drop. The 16 non-public datasets could behave like knockTF. LOW for anything about MDT/UDT, which did not run. The shuffled null preserves out-degree, per-TF sign mix and in-degree but not signed in-degree (same weak spot V-006 records for controls.py), and holds self-edges fixed by design, so it is "random regulon + real autoregulation", not a full null. A further defect found and not patched: their `result/auc/rank/*.csv` per-cell dump is written after the `if(True)` random block rebinds df_rank, so that file contains RANDOM ranks for every method; the headline au_rank is computed before the rebinding and is correct.
Depends on: F-009, F-010, F-023

## V-027 · 2026-09-18 · skeptic · verdict: split — the anchor, the transfer test and the internal controls are CONFIRMED and the mechanism is now a measured law, not a story; the third result (the shuffled band) is REFUTED as stated, a 10-seed artefact whose correction makes the finding stronger; the ORA paragraph's conclusion is confirmed and its stated mechanism is wrong in two places
Claim checked: F-027 — on the one public dataset of the 2026 Perturb-seq benchmark (Zhu/Han/Wang, TP53__Dox, 617 cells), running THEIR unpatched dcp.py + auc.py reproduces their shipped ULM_CollecTRI numbers exactly, and masking TP53's own gene moves their headline AUROC by -0.0001 (0.9572 -> 0.9571), so the knockTF abundance leak does not transfer here; plus three side results (the by-group internal null, the shuffled-network floor, the ORA instability).
How verified: reran `cd analysis && uv run python bench2026.py` (cached on the act csvs, 4 min wall, exit 0, assert passes). EVERY number the task listed reproduces: anchor 0.9572/0.9231/0.0633/0.0000 against their shipped column; masked 0.9571, noself 0.9571, noother 0.9572; group table TP53 1.450e-04 / 1.450e-04 / 0.000e+00, the 106 TP53-holding TFs 6.435e-03 / 0.000e+00 / 6.451e-03, the 243-TF built-in null 1.313e-05 / 0.000e+00 / 0.000e+00, the 263 self-edge-no-TP53 TFs 5.770e-05 / 0 / 0; 613 scored TFs; shuffle mean 0.5496 [0.433, 0.701], auprc 0.3748, rank 0.4940, top10 0.0604, top5 0.0223; all-methods table byte-for-byte as quoted (ora +0.1893, mlm -0.0078, aucell -0.0060, gsea -0.0048, consensus +0.0017, ulm/zscore -0.0001); PRR11 control +0.1370; their random column 0.49386.
  ANCHOR, attacked as instructed, and it holds as a REAL recomputation. (i) No read-back path exists. `their()` looks up column `ulm_collectri` in result/auc/*.csv; their 60 shipped columns are `ULM_CollecTRI`, `random`, `pySCENIC`, ... — no lowercase_underscore name among them, and a missing column returns NaN via KeyError, not their value. The act csvs the metrics are computed from live in `result/decoupler/`, which is UNTRACKED in their repo (`git status` in the clone: 0 modified tracked files, HEAD b904196, only untracked additions — DoxMASK/, DoxRAND/, the 12 net_*.csv, dcp_cond.py, result/decoupler/, result/auc/rank/). (ii) The backup/restore is sound: data/bench2026_backup/*.csv is byte-identical to `git show HEAD:result/auc/*.csv` for all five tables, `published` is read from the backup before any run, the live tables are restored from it at the end, and the post-run clone is clean. (iii) INDEPENDENT REPRODUCTION, which is the real test: I called decoupler 2.2.0 myself in my own process on their filtered.h5ad + net_collectri_filter.csv (sc.pp.normalize_total 1e4, log1p, dc.mt.ulm tmin=3) and applied auc.py's formulae by hand — auroc 0.957240, auprc 0.923125, au_rank 0.063274, identical to their shipped values to six decimals, and my score matrix matches the act csv their dcp.py wrote to max |diff| 3.55e-15. dcp_cond.py's diff touches only the netfile branch, the method list, the waggr/consensus gates and the output loop; no scoring code. dcp.py, auc.py and filter.py are unmodified vs HEAD. Temper one word: six-decimal agreement is EXPECTED, not extraordinary — ULM is a closed-form t-statistic, decoupler's feature-order tie-break is seeded (`default_rng(0)`), and the input h5ad is shipped; what it licenses is "ULM is version-stable 2.1.1 -> 2.2.0 and their pipeline is deterministic", not a claim about their numerical care. RESIDUAL FRAGILITY, not a defect in the result: auc.py rewrites the `random` column of the live tables for every row it touches, so a crash between the restore-from-backup and the restore-at-the-end leaves their shipped tables modified (recoverable; the backup is only created when absent).
  MECHANISM (item 3), verified and then generalised — this is the part that outlives the dataset. Their inputs confirm F-027's three numbers: TP53 regulon 980 edges / 772 measured targets, own transcript detected in 95 of 617 cells, raw count mean 0.1980 perturbed vs 0.1738 control. I then masked EACH of the 613 scored TFs' own gene in turn (their preprocessing, decoupler ULM, 1-source call verified equal to the full call to 1e-12) and measured how far that TF's own score moves. On the 227 self-edge TFs whose own gene is measured and whose regulon survives tmin=3: mean |dscore| falls off with regulon size as Spearman -0.868 (p 1.7e-70), log10|dscore| = -0.53 - 0.46*log10(size) + 0.10*log10(detection), R2 0.80 — i.e. the own gene's channel width scales as size^-1/2, exactly what one gene contributes to a t-statistic. Terciles: 7-target regulons |dscore| 0.148 / |dAUROC| 1.06e-2; 28-target 0.093 / 5.5e-3; 92-target 0.055 / 2.5e-3; >= 300 targets (n=6) 1.3e-3; <= 10 targets (n=47) median 1.1e-2, max 5.7e-2. TP53 is where the law puts it: predicted |dscore| 0.0220, observed 0.0194 (residual -0.06 log10 against a residual sd of 0.11), and smaller than 99.6% of self-edge TFs. So F-027 may be cited as "the leak's channel width scales with regulon size as V-010 predicts, and TP53's 772-target regulon sits at the bottom of that curve" — far stronger than "not in this regime". The DETECTION half of the mechanism sentence does NOT survive as worded: detection has essentially no marginal effect on channel width (Spearman +0.026, p 0.69). Detection matters through the other factor: the own transcript here separates perturbed from control cells at AUROC 0.4933 (0.4872 on raw counts), i.e. chance. Cite the mechanism as a product — leak = channel width (1/sqrt(regulon size)) x own-gene discriminative signal — with both factors at their minimum for TP53.
  ONE SENTENCE OF F-027 MUST NOT BE QUOTED AS SCOPE: "it does not even go DOWN in counts". True in raw counts, but perturbed cells are much deeper (median 4907 detected genes vs 3526; 21739 vs 15617 counts), and on the normalised layer their own dataset/filter.py uses, TP53 IS lower in perturbed cells (0.0950 vs 0.1144) — filter.py lines 150-177 KEEP a TF only if pert mean < ctrl mean for Inhibition, so this benchmark selects its positives on the own-abundance drop exactly as decoupler's knockTF thr_fc=-1 does (F-018/V-018). The leak is weak here because the drop is dropout-noise-sized, not because the design avoids the selection.
  DEPTH, a confound F-027 does not check and which does NOT bite: n detected genes alone separates the arms at AUROC 0.9005 and total counts at 0.8075, but ULM's 0.9572 survives stratification into depth quintiles at 0.9507 (zscore 0.9874 -> 0.9690, waggr 0.9534 -> 0.9458), while the depth variables themselves collapse (0.58 and 0.17). Their headline is not a library-size readout; the shuffled null's spread probably is partly one.
  SHUFFLED BAND (item 4, T-060): REFUTED AS STATED, and the corrected version is worse for them. 10 seeds cannot support a range claim. I reimplemented shuffle_net verbatim and reproduced seeds 0-9 to 0.00e+00 against the act csvs, then ran 100 seeds: mean 0.4660, sd 0.1393 (95% CI of the mean [0.439, 0.493]), 2.5-97.5 percentile [0.225, 0.762], min 0.143, max 0.797. F-027's 0.5496 is the 97th percentile of 10-seed subsamples of that distribution and 42 of the 100 seeds fall BELOW its quoted minimum of 0.433, so "[0.433, 0.701]" is not the null's range, it is one unlucky decade of draws (the table's `ci` column is a min-max, not an interval). Quote instead: "a degree- and sign-mix-preserving random regulon of TP53's shape scores 0.47 +/- 0.14 on this dataset, central 95% [0.225, 0.762]". 0 of 100 seeds reach 0.957, so ULM's margin over the floor is untouched. What falls inside the corrected band: 34 of their 60 published columns for TP53__Dox, including their own `random` (0.494) — VIPER_CollecTRI 0.508, GSVA_CollecTRI 0.568, scMINER 0.626, AUCell_CollecTRI 0.671, pySCENIC 0.674, and metaTF_PUIC 0.734, which F-027 calls "just outside" and which is INSIDE at P(shuffled >= 0.734) = 0.05; also MDT_TFLink 0.745, AUCell_TFLink 0.733, the whole TFLink column set, ORA_CollecTRI 0.264 and ORA_TFLink 0.264. The honest form is exactly the one the task names: on THIS ONE DATASET, for THIS ONE TF, those scores are not separable from a shape-matched random network; their published ranking is over 40 datasets and this licenses no statement about it. It also licenses nothing about the 26 columns outside the band beyond "above a random regulon".
  ORA (item 5): the CONCLUSION is confirmed with a control far stronger than PRR11, the stated MECHANISM is wrong in two places. Decisive test: rescoring the UNCHANGED matrix under 5 random feature orderings gives TP53 ORA AUROC 0.2867 / 0.3008 / 0.2965 / 0.4555 / 0.3306 (spread 0.169), and 3 further orderings 0.1234 / 0.2956 / 0.3127 (spread 0.1892) — the same size as the claimed +0.1893 masking effect, on data that did not change at all. Under the same orderings ulm, zscore, mlm and viper move by 0.0000 and aucell by 0.0023 (so about a third of aucell's -0.0060 is ordering noise too, and only ulm/zscore/mlm/viper's deltas are safe to read as masking). Corrections: (1) the reshuffle is not local to the cut — decoupler/pp/data.py::_break_ties permutes ALL features with `default_rng(seed=0)` over n features, and masking a gene makes it all-zero so `_validate_mat(empty=True)` DROPS it (15498 -> 15433 features at the anchor, one fewer masked), which changes n and therefore the entire permutation; (2) _ora.py line 276 is `ranks[(row > n_up) | (row < n_bm)]`, which selects the 14723 features ABOVE the cut, i.e. 95% of them, not "the top n_up = 775" — the docstring says top 5%, the code takes the complement of the bottom 5%. Both the tie block (median 11768 tied zeros at the cut, present in 100% of cells) and the near-saturated contingency table follow from that. GENERALITY, the item-5 question: it is specific to sparse counts. On bulk knockTF logFC the cut lands in a tie block in 12% of rows with median tie size 1, and three orderings of the same 15000-gene knockTF matrix give an identical ORA top-10% hit rate (0.0226 each) and a median-rank spread of 0.0068, versus 0.0000 for ULM. Separately, and worth its own entry: decoupler 2.2.0's ORA RAISES on the full 21985-gene knockTF matrix with defaults (`ValueError: invalid contingency table`, because the 95% selection makes a+b+c exceed the default n_bg=20000).
  ROUTE (item 6). F-027 is TF-PERTURBING (TP53 CRISPRi-style inhibition; the TF's own mRNA is on the causal path) and it is the third TF-perturbing benchmark in this log, not a second route. What it does to F-023: LESS than tasks.md's decision line says. F-027 measures Zhu/Han/Wang's pipeline, not Sugimoto's, and it closes only the DIRECT own-mRNA channel (mask the gene / cut the self-edge). F-023's mechanism is indirect — TFActProfiler's weights are fitted to TF-transcript covariation, so its score reconstructs the TF's own mRNA through hundreds of correlated targets, a channel that masking the own gene does not touch. So F-023's "candidate abundance leak" is neither weakened nor confirmed here; what is now regime-dependent is F-009/F-010's DIRECT channel: worth ~51% of hits for small-regulon TFs in bulk knockTF, worth 1.45e-4 AUROC for a 772-target TF whose transcript is dropout-dominated in 10x counts. Write it as "the direct channel is regime-dependent (regulon size x own-gene signal)", not as "the leak does not transfer to 2026 benchmarks".
Would flip if: the anchor half flips if a fresh decoupler call disagreed with the act csvs (agrees to 3.55e-15) or if `ulm_collectri` collided with a shipped column (it does not); the transfer half flips if masking on a public dataset with a SMALL-regulon TF and a detectable own-transcript drop moved AUROC by > 0.05 — the size law predicts about 0.01-0.06 for a 5-10 target regulon here, so their 16 non-public datasets could still show it and that is the experiment to ask for; the mechanism's size law flips if the -0.46 exponent did not replicate on a second dataset or if TP53's residual exceeded the scatter (it is -0.06 against sd 0.11); the corrected band flips if 100 further seeds gave a different mean (the CI of the mean is +/-0.027) or if the shuffle preserved signed in-degree and narrowed it; the ORA finding flips if decoupler pinned the feature permutation to a content hash instead of n, which would make masking-vs-anchor comparable again.
Severity: high — F-027 is the only measurement this project has on a published single-cell benchmark and the only negative result against its own hypothesis, so both its correct half (the direct leak is regime-dependent, with a measured size law) and its wrong half (a 10-seed band that six published methods "fall inside") would be cited widely. The band correction is the second 10-seed artefact in this log (V-024 was the first): 10 seeds should stop being the default anywhere a range or a mean is quoted. Medium for the ORA finding: it invalidates ORA_CollecTRI / ORA_TFLink as reported numbers for any sparse single-cell dataset, in their paper and in anything else built on decoupler 2.2.0, but changes nothing here.

## F-029 · 2026-09-19 · experimentalist (entered by main session) · status: proposed
Claim: The project's numbers are now machine-readable, and building the record found one misquoted headline. `results/master.csv` is one row per SCORED knockTF experiment on the UNFILTERED file (643 rows over 263 TFs, 279 flagged `in_filtered_279`), 39 columns: seven scoring conditions as rank fraction plus top-10% and top-5% flags (ULM unmasked; ULM own-gene masked ASYMMETRICALLY, leak.py's convention, which is the one behind the citable 0.344; ULM masked SYMMETRICALLY, F-028's convention; self-edge removed; own gene removed from every OTHER regulon; propagation up/sum/alpha=0.5 unmasked and masked) plus the covariates every entry stratifies on (own logFC, pruned regulon size, self-edge presence/weight/sign_decision, whole-regulon default fraction, Profile.ID, Biosample.Name, Knock.Method, Platform, study-mate count, max regulon Jaccard) and V-022's per-TF base rate. 30 of 31 published numbers recompute from that file alone and are asserted in the same script: F-001 0.452 / 126 of 279 / 155 TFs; F-005/F-013 masked 0.344 / 96; F-010 no-self-edge 96, no-other 127, the 197/82 split at 0.538/0.386 and 0.244/0.244; F-011 176 +1 and 21 -1 with 98->65 and 8->11; F-016/F-017's 92 default / 74 PMID / 10 regulon split of the 176; F-022 643 scored / 263 TFs / masked 0.299; F-004 propagated 0.566 unmasked and 0.276 masked (predicted only to three decimals, returned exact). ONE NUMBER DOES NOT REPRODUCE, and it is a denominator slip of the kind V-013 warned about, not a computation error: V-022's per-TF base rate "0.123 on the filtered 279" is 0.1225, i.e. 0.122; 0.1231 -> 0.123 is that same construction on all 643 SCORED rows. V-022's table subtracts one figure from both rows ("FILTERED 279 0.344 - 0.123 = +0.221" and "all 643 0.299 - 0.123 = +0.175"), so the 643 figure was carried onto the 279 denominator. The construction here IS V-022's: its top-5% companion reproduces exactly (0.0666 -> 0.067, V-022's "0.280 - 0.067 = +0.213"), and studymate.py has been PRINTING the correction all along — "per-TF BASE RATE on the same rows 0.122 top-10%, 0.067 top-5% [V-022: 0.123]" — which V-028 recorded as "global 0.122" without anyone reconciling it against the Index. Effect: masked ULM is +0.222 over its base rate on the 279 and 2.81x, not +0.221 / 2.80x; "chance for perturbed TFs is about 12%, not 10%" is unaffected. master.csv is NOT adjusted to the published value: the validation row prints FAIL and the script's assert requires that mismatch AND ONLY that mismatch, so it breaks if any other number drifts or if the slip is silently fixed. Second half: sixteen scripts that printed a table now also persist it, each gaining two additive edits — a stdout tee guarded by `if __name__ == "__main__"` so nested imports do not cross-contaminate logs, and one `to_csv` before the final assert — and nothing else; 39 files in results/, 1.2 MB.
Evidence: `cd analysis && uv run python master.py` (32 s, assert passes). Built on data/knocktf_full.h5ad with fulldose.py's unfiltered pipeline; the masking, self-edge, other-regulon, propagation, base-rate, study-grouping and Jaccard machinery is COPIED from leak.py, selfedge.py, propagate.py, studymate.py and gapcheck.py, not reimplemented. Persistence verified by rerunning 15 of 16 scripts (robust_ulm 246 s, the rest 4-39 s), every assert passing, and re-reading the logs against the tables entries quote: V-012's leak/controls reference lines, F-010's decomposition block, F-011's 176/21 with medians 93/330, fulldose's "REPRODUCTION ... -> MATCH", coregmask's 0.004, signprobe's 96/78, robust_ulm's 0.45 vs 0.32 — all unchanged. NOT YET GENERATED: results/masked_huber.* (8 min) and results/bench2026.* (43 min); the code is written in both, neither rerun.
Source: analysis/master.py; results/master.csv; results/master_validation.csv; results/*.log
Confidence: high for every reproduced number and for the diagnosis of the mismatch — deterministic, no seeds in master.py, and the 0.122 is corroborated by two independent sources (studymate.py's printed line and V-028's "global 0.122") plus the exactly-reproducing top-5% companion. High that the 16 scripts' stdout is unchanged: every edit is a list append or a to_csv, neither of which can reach a print. MEDIUM for coverage: "most headline numbers are recomputable" is demonstrated for 31 quantities across eight entries, not the whole log — every bootstrap CI, shuffled null, phospho and 2026-benchmark number and within-TF slope still needs its owning script, because master.csv holds point estimates and covariates, not draws. LOW for masked_huber and bench2026, whose persisted files do not exist yet.
Depends on: F-001, F-010, F-022

## V-029 · 2026-09-19 · skeptic · verdict: confirmed
Claim checked: F-029 — (a) results/master.csv is a 643-row machine-readable record from which 30 of 31 published numbers recompute, with an assert that demands exactly one mismatch; (b) THE LOAD-BEARING PART: V-022's per-TF base rate is 0.122 on the filtered 279 and 0.123 only on all 643 scored, so the published "0.123 on the 279" is a denominator slip; (c) sixteen scripts gained a guarded stdout tee and a to_csv and no printed line changed; (d) coverage is "most headline numbers", rated MEDIUM.
How verified: (1) THE CORRECTION, recomputed from scratch without importing master.py, studymate.py or leak.py: own numpy OLS t-values (max |mine - dc.mt.ulm.func| = 1.2e-06) and scipy rankdata instead of pandas.rank, on data/knocktf_full.h5ad, asymmetric mask. Anchors first: 0.3441 (96/279), 0.2986 on the 643, 0.2796 top-5%. PER-TF BASE RATE, V-022's construction (for TF t, the share of the 907 rows perturbing a DIFFERENT TF in which t is itself top-10%), row-weighted over the target set: FILTERED 279 = 0.12250 top-10% / 0.06665 top-5%; ALL 643 = 0.12309 / 0.06643. The 279 figure as an exact rational is 0.1224991943, i.e. below 0.1225, so it is 0.122 under round-half-up as well as under .3f — rounding cannot rescue 0.123. The construction is pinned to V-022 by its own fingerprints: over all 766 scored TFs mean 0.0991, sd 0.0444, max 0.4051, top seven E2F4 0.405, E2F1 0.390, MYC 0.374, E2F3 0.318, E2F2 0.303, TFDP1 0.277, SP1 0.265 (V-022: 0.099 / 0.044 / 0.41 and the same seven in the same order), excluding the experiment's own study 0.1222 (V-022: 0.122), and seven of V-022's ten printed bin base rates reproduce to three decimals. So masked ULM on the 279 is +0.222 over base and 2.81x, and F-029's correction is RIGHT and safe to leave live in the Index, writeup.md and the published page: under the masking convention the 0.344 itself is computed in, the reference line is 0.1225.
  BUT THE DIAGNOSIS IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and F-029's supporting argument does not discriminate. F-029 says the 643 figure was carried onto the 279 row. An equally simple alternative fits: base rates read off UNMASKED ranks (the base row's own perturbed gene left in place) give 0.1229 -> 0.123 on the 279 and 0.1236 -> 0.124 on the 643, i.e. it fits V-022's 279 row and misses its 643 row, the mirror image of the masked construction. The top-5% companion F-029 leans on is 0.0666 -> 0.067 masked and 0.0670 -> 0.067 unmasked, so it does not tell the two apart; neither does studymate.py, which is downstream of V-022 rather than independent of it. Neither variant reproduces V-022's whole printed table (masked 7/10 bins, unmasked 6/10; both give 0.127 where V-022 prints 0.128 for the m == 0 bin, and both give same-study 0.222/0.227 against V-022's 0.225), so V-022's scratch — deleted, never in the repo — used a construction slightly different from both and cannot be recovered. What survives: the two quantities differ in the fourth decimal, V-022 printed one three-decimal figure for both rows, and one of the two rows is therefore wrong by 0.001. Which one is a matter of provenance, not of the number to publish. Quote "0.122 on the filtered 279 under the asymmetric mask, 0.123 on all 643" and drop the "denominator slip / carried onto" story, or mark it as the likelier of two.
  (2) `cd analysis && uv run python master.py` (57 s on a loaded box), assert passes, 30 of 31 reproduce, the single FAIL is V-022 per-TF base rate 279 0.123 -> 0.122. The assert is `set(zip(bad.entry, bad.quantity)) == KNOWN`; all 31 (entry, quantity) keys are unique, so nothing hides behind a duplicate. Tested on a copy in scratch (own results dir, project outputs untouched) by mutating the ok column: fires when the V-022/279 row is made to pass (silent fix), fires when any one of three other rows is made to fail, fires when the failing row is swapped for a different one, does not fire as run. It requires that mismatch AND ONLY that mismatch, as claimed.
  (3) SPOT-CHECK AGAINST THE OWNING SCRIPTS. Joined master.csv to results/leak_rows.csv, selfedge_rows.csv, tflevel_rows.csv and studymate.csv. Exact: ulm_masked_frac / ulm_masked_symmetric_frac / base_rate10 / own_logfc / in_filtered_279 vs studymate.csv (max |diff| 0.0, 643 rows), self_edge_weight vs tflevel's sign, max_jaccard to 2e-08. regulon_size IS the pruned nonzero column count with the self-edge included (recomputed from the pruned adjacency myself: equal on all 643, and NOT equal to the self-excluded count on any self-edge row); tflevel's larger "size" is gapcheck's unpruned target count, the documented difference. self_edge_sign_decision is genuinely NaN on all 171 no-self-edge rows (82 in the 279), no default label; the labelled rows split default activation 240 / PMID 208 / regulon 24. ulm_masked is leak.py's asymmetric convention (96/279, 0.344) and ulm_masked_symmetric is F-028's (0.348 on the 279, 0.289 on the 643, matching F-028 (4)).
  NEW, and it corrects master.py's stated licence rather than its numbers: the docstring claims "the var axis of the two files is identical (21985 genes)" and infers that the unfiltered pipeline reproduces the filtered one EXACTLY. The raw files are both 21985 wide, but dc.pp.extract drops 50 all-zero genes from data/knocktf.h5ad, so leak.py/selfedge.py score on 21935 features and master.py on 21985. Values on shared genes are identical, the 50 extras are zero in all 388 shared rows and are not pruned-net targets, and the source set is 766 either way — but the t-statistic's background changes, so 19 of 279 masked and 18 of 279 unmasked rank fractions differ from leak_rows/selfedge_rows by 1 to 3 of 766 positions (mean masked rank frac 0.32643 vs 0.32645). NO HIT FLAG MOVES: masked, unmasked, no-self-edge, no-other at both 10% and 5% agree row by row, 0 disagreements, which is why all 30 asserted counts land. The reproduction is empirical at the hit level, not exact by construction, and a rank-level statistic recomputed from master.csv will not be byte-identical to the owning script's.
  (4) PERSISTENCE, tested structurally since the repo has no commits at all to diff against. The tee block is byte-identical in all 17 edited scripts modulo the log filename (8 lines, guarded by `if __name__ == "__main__"`), writes to sys.__stdout__ verbatim before the log, and cannot alter a printed line. The guard does what F-029 says: results/leak.log lines 1-24 are byte-identical to results/propagate.log (propagate's grid nests into leak's log because leak imports it), lines 25-35 are byte-identical to controls.log's own block, and propagate.log contains only its own 24 lines. Every other edit carries an F-029 comment and is a to_csv at the end or, in leak.py and controls.py only, a `_rows = []` with appends inside the print helpers that recompute nothing the print does not already compute. Logs are consistent with what entries quote: fulldose's "REPRODUCTION ... -> MATCH" line with 279/155/176/0.095/0.413/-0.902/0.079, selfedge's decomposition block (0.344 96/279, 0.386 76/197, 0.244 20/82), coregmask's 0.004, signprobe's 96/279 and 78/279, robust_ulm's 0.45 vs 0.32, selfsign's 21-experiment median regulon 330. studymate.log line 53 prints "per-TF BASE RATE on the same rows 0.122 top-10%, 0.067 top-5% [V-022: 0.123]", so the correction has indeed been on disk all along. Two small count drifts, neither substantive: 17 scripts gained the tee (15 with complete logs, masked_huber and bench2026 still running), not 16; results/ now holds 44 files and 1.3 MB, not 39 and 1.2 MB, because those two jobs are writing.
  (5) COVERAGE. The MEDIUM is honest and if anything understated. master.csv's 38 non-key columns are covariates, rank fractions and hit flags: no bootstrap draw, no seed, no shuffled-network column, no phospho or MPXV column, nothing from the 2026 benchmark, and — beyond what F-029 says — NO RAW ULM SCORE, so F-022/V-022's within-TF dose-response slope, its bin means (-2.62 ... -0.54), V-024/V-025's +0.360 and V-027's size law are not merely missing their draws, their point estimates are not computable from this file either. Nothing is silently approximated: base_rate10 is V-022's identity null and is labelled as such, not as a degree-preserving shuffle.
Would flip if: V-022's scratch (setup.py / partB.py / samestudy.py, session scratchpad, never in the repo) were recovered and showed a base-rate construction that gives 0.1225+ on the 279 under the same masked ranks the 0.344 uses — that would make 0.123 a legitimate value rather than one of two three-decimal roundings; or if any of the 30 reproducing rows failed on a clean rerun; or if a pre-edit capture of any of the 15 rerun scripts' stdout differed from its results/*.log.
Severity: medium — the correction itself moves +0.221 to +0.222 and 2.80x to 2.81x and changes no conclusion, and "chance is about 12%, not 10%" (CLAUDE.md, T-038) is untouched either way, so the three places the main session has already published it are safe. What is high-severity is the habit it rests on: master.csv is now the file other agents will recompute from, and both its documented licence ("identical var axis, reproduces exactly") and F-029's provenance story for the 0.001 are stated more confidently than the evidence supports.
